# UdonSharp Port Plan

Plan for porting the Java ChessBoard library to UdonSharp, to run in VRChat worlds. Nothing has been written yet; this records the design agreed so far.

This file holds the implementation steps. The design is split into topic files under `docs/`, and each step lists the ones to read before starting it.

## Design documents

| File | Contents |
|---|---|
| [overview.md](docs/overview.md) | Project layout, goals, environment |
| [udon-constraints.md](docs/udon-constraints.md) | What UdonSharp can't do, and the consequences for the design |
| [architecture.md](docs/architecture.md) | Packed moves, static rules, logical board (`ChessBoard`) vs physical board (`ChessBoardView`), listeners |
| [physical-pieces.md](docs/physical-pieces.md) | 32 piece objects, placing from the board, grab gating |
| [move-encoding.md](docs/move-encoding.md) | 32-bit move layout, derived flags, castling encoding, undo |
| [legality-checking.md](docs/legality-checking.md) | Attack-check legality, direct SAN disambiguation |
| [player-input.md](docs/player-input.md) | Drops, promotion, castling by drop, desktop mode (click to select and place) |
| [board-lifecycle.md](docs/board-lifecycle.md) | Lifecycle states, seats, starting, reset |
| [clocks.md](docs/clocks.md) | Time control, synced clock state, flag fall |
| [modes.md](docs/modes.md) | Standard, Chess960, Fog of War (later) and `prohibitSelfCheck` |
| [replay.md](docs/replay.md) | Replay mode, takeback, continuing loaded (imported or archived) games |
| [game-ids.md](docs/game-ids.md) | Game ID format, `ContinuedFrom`, ply numbering |
| [settings.md](docs/settings.md) | `ChessArbiter`, per-board overrides, snapshots |
| [organizers.md](docs/organizers.md) | `SimpleAuth` and its built-in implementations, empty-field checks |
| [archive.md](docs/archive.md) | The archives (`GameArchive`: the instance's `InstanceGameArchive` with its holders and one writer, the personal `PersonalGameArchive` in PlayerData), the history walls, the pick, loading games |
| [networking.md](docs/networking.md) | Limits, synced fields, notation fill-in, imported games |
| [ownership.md](docs/ownership.md) | Ownership rules, players by display name, organizers |
| [inactivity.md](docs/inactivity.md) | Reset-available and auto-reset timers, activity timestamp |
| [pgn.md](docs/pgn.md) | Import, export, text box, logging |
| [game-endings.md](docs/game-endings.md) | Endings, results, `Termination` values, draw rules |
| [java-bugs.md](docs/java-bugs.md) | Bugs in the Java reference (castling, pawn captures) |
| [testing.md](docs/testing.md) | Test approach: Play Mode tests, perft, multi-client |
| [open-decisions.md](docs/open-decisions.md) | Open questions (Fog of War only) |
| [customizing.md](docs/customizing.md) | What a world creator can change: the board model, scaling, chess piece sets |
| [compatibility.md](docs/compatibility.md) | Keeping worlds that use the package working across updates: editing prefabs in place, migrating saved fields, the migrator, changelog |
| [board-2d.md](docs/board-2d.md) | The 2D board view: one board at a time, stepping through the boards in use, the synced choice; its own replay, move list, locking and moving (12.14 to 12.16) |
| [prefabs.md](docs/prefabs.md) | The prefabs world creators place: what each holds, what to set after placing it, the minimum set, starting a piece set of one's own |

[overview.md](docs/overview.md) and [udon-constraints.md](docs/udon-constraints.md) apply to every step; they're listed below only where a step depends on them directly.

## Implementation steps

Ordered so that each step can be tested before the next builds on it: rules first with no world objects, then settings and the logical board, then networking, then the world. Open decisions that block a step are noted on it.

**Using this checklist.** Each item is sized to finish and verify in one session. Tick it (`- [x]`) only after its **Verify** has passed, and before starting the next item. If the work deviates from the plan, update the plan (and the docs) to match.

- Unless an item says otherwise, verification runs in Unity Play Mode (ClientSim): the step's test behaviour logs `PASS`/`FAIL` per check, and the results are read from the Unity Editor log (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\Unity\Editor\Editor.log`). Each item adds its checks to that test behaviour, so the step's final **Test** item reruns everything.
- "Several clients" means VRChat's Build & Test with multiple client instances (see [Testing](docs/testing.md)).
- Comparing against the Java means building and running it in `JavaChessBoard/` with Maven, on its `port-reference` branch, which has its known bugs fixed (item 1.6; see CLAUDE.md).

### 1. Project setup and early check

**Read:** [overview](docs/overview.md), [udon-constraints](docs/udon-constraints.md), [organizers](docs/organizers.md) (`SimpleAuth`), [java-bugs](docs/java-bugs.md), [testing](docs/testing.md)

- [x] **1.1** Create the Unity project with the VRChat Creator Companion, Worlds SDK 3.7.1 or later.
  - Verify: `Packages/vpm-manifest.json` lists `com.vrchat.worlds` at 3.7.1 or later. (Done: 3.10.5. The `io.siggi.ustools` dependency needs 3.10.4 or later, which is now the minimum.)
- [x] **1.2** Make the Java library available as `JavaChessBoard/`; move this plan to the project root.
  - Verify: `JavaChessBoard/pom.xml` and `JavaChessBoard/src/main/java/io/siggi/chessboard/` exist; `PLAN.md` is at the project root.
- [x] **1.3** Add the PGN test set in `Assets/Tests/PGN/` (see [PGN test set](docs/testing.md#pgn-test-set)), so test behaviours can read the files through `TextAsset` fields, since Udon has no file I/O. It includes a copy of `JavaChessBoard/Siggi-vs-Nuka.pgn` (original left in place), a long Lichess game, hand-written PGNs for the features Siggi-vs-Nuka doesn't cover, and malformed PGNs. Files are named `*.pgn.txt`, since Unity doesn't import `.pgn` as a `TextAsset`. (Done: Play Mode check with `Assets/Tests/Checks/TextAssetCheck.cs`.)
  - Verify: the Siggi-vs-Nuka copy is byte-identical to the original (`cmp`); every file except `formatting.pgn.txt` and the malformed ones imports in the Java on `port-reference` with the ply count and final FEN listed in the test set, and the malformed ones fail where listed (done); Unity has imported every file (a `.meta` file exists), and a behaviour with a `TextAsset` field set to `Siggi-vs-Nuka.pgn` logs its first line in Play Mode.
- [x] **1.4** Add `SimpleAuth` (`SiggiUSTools`).
  - Verify: `Assets/Siggi/USTools/SimpleAuth.cs` declares the class as in [Organizers](docs/organizers.md), and the Editor log shows it imported with no compile errors. (Since moved, with the built-ins of 6.2a, to the `io.siggi.ustools` package, a VPM dependency; see [Organizers](docs/organizers.md).)
- [x] **1.5** **Early check: Udon speed.** A throwaway behaviour that generates moves in a loop, to get a feel for real costs before the design relies on them.
  - Verify: in Play Mode it logs moves generated per second (and ms per position); record the numbers next to this item.
  - Done (2026-10-10, `Assets/Tests/Checks/UdonSpeedCheck.cs`): a stand-in generator in the planned style (`byte[64]`, packed moves, static methods, legality by make → attack check → unmake); its sanity check passed (20 legal moves from the start, 48 in Kiwipete). Results in ms per position (moves/s):

    | Phase | Editor (ClientSim) | VRChat client |
    |---|---|---|
    | Start, pseudo-legal (20 moves) | 0.083–0.088 (227k–240k) | 0.127 (158k) |
    | Kiwipete, pseudo-legal (48 moves) | 0.105 (457k) | 0.161 (298k) |
    | Start, legal (20 moves) | 0.49 (41k) | 0.74 (27k) |
    | Kiwipete, legal (48 moves) | 1.29 (37k) | 1.93 (25k) |

    The client is about 1.5× slower than the Editor. A legal move list costs roughly 0.7–2 ms in the client, almost all of it in the legality check (each pseudo-legal move costs one attack check). For a single move in live play that's fine. Anything that needs a legal list for every ply (PGN import, notation fill-in) gets through about 5–10 plies per 10 ms budget, so a long game takes tens of frames. The perft checks in 3.3a need to be spread across frames as well: start depth 4 is about 9,300 legal lists (≈5 s in the Editor), and Kiwipete depth 3 is about 2,100 (≈3 s).
- [x] **1.6** In `JavaChessBoard/`, create a `port-reference` branch from `main` and check it out in place; fix the two castling bugs there (see [Bugs found in the Java](docs/java-bugs.md)) and commit them to that branch only. (Done. Three more bugs turned up and were fixed on the branch too: castling paths accepting any king or rook, castling not removing the rights, and pawns capturing their own pieces. The branch now matches published perft counts.)
  - Verify: `mvn package` succeeds on the branch; in the Java, after a rook is captured on its starting square that side has lost the castling right, and after `Bxh8` capturing the rook Black can't `O-O`; `main` still points at `726e76d`, the commit it was on when this item was written.

The Java's `main` branch is never committed to. Its known bugs are fixed on the `port-reference` branch (1.6), so comparisons against the Java need no exceptions (see [Bugs found in the Java](docs/java-bugs.md)).

Already settled, so no early check is needed:
- `VRCObjectSync` was assumed not to fight a non-owner snapping a piece into place. Step 9.6 showed it does, so the pieces don't use it (see [Physical pieces](docs/physical-pieces.md)).
- Synced `string[]` works, but a single `null` element makes the whole serialization fail, so every element must be initialised to an empty string (see [Synced fields](docs/networking.md#synced-fields-per-board)).

### 2. Core rules (static methods, no world)

**Read:** [udon-constraints](docs/udon-constraints.md), [architecture](docs/architecture.md), [move-encoding](docs/move-encoding.md), [legality-checking](docs/legality-checking.md), [modes](docs/modes.md) (`prohibitSelfCheck`, Chess960 castling), [java-bugs](docs/java-bugs.md), [testing](docs/testing.md)

The rules live in `Assets/Siggi/ChessBoard/Rules/` (namespace `SiggiChessBoard`), as static methods and constants on abstract `UdonSharpBehaviour` classes, starting with `ChessRules`. The step 2 test behaviour is `Assets/Tests/Rules/RulesTest.cs`.

- [x] **2.1** Constants and helpers: piece types and colours, square ↔ file/rank, piece encoding in the `byte[64]` board.
  - Verify: the step 2 test behaviour checks square → file/rank → square for all 64 squares, and piece encode → decode for every colour and type, plus empty.
- [x] **2.2** Move encoding: pack/unpack helpers, and the derived en passant / double-push checks.
  - Verify: pack → unpack returns every field unchanged, at each field's minimum and maximum; the derived checks are right for hand-built moves (en passant capture, double push, ordinary pawn move, non-pawn move).
- [x] **2.3a** Make and unmake a move on the board arrays: ordinary moves, captures, en passant, promotion, and en passant restore.
  - Verify: for each kind of move on a hand-built position, the position after make is as expected, and make followed by unmake restores every array exactly.
  - `ChessPosition.MakeMove`/`UnmakeMove` in `Assets/Siggi/ChessBoard/Rules/ChessPosition.cs`, on the board and a state array (see [Architecture](docs/architecture.md)). En passant is set on every double push, as in the Java.
- [x] **2.3b** Make and unmake for castling (Chess960-aware), and castling rights and their restore.
  - Verify: as 2.3a, for both sides' O-O and O-O-O in the standard position and in Chess960 positions (including one where the king doesn't move); castling rights are lost after king and rook moves and when a rook is captured on its starting square, and are restored by unmake.
  - In `ChessPosition.MakeMove`/`UnmakeMove`; rights are lost by any move from or to a king's or rook's starting square (`ChessPosition.CastlingRightsAt`, see [Move encoding](docs/move-encoding.md#castling)).
- [x] **2.4a** Move generation per piece type.
  - Verify: for hand-built positions with a single piece of each type (plus blockers and capturable pieces), the generated moves match a hand-written list.
  - `ChessMoves.GenerateMoves`/`GeneratePieceMoves` in `Assets/Siggi/ChessBoard/Rules/ChessMoves.cs`: pseudo-legal moves into a caller's `int[ChessMoves.MaxMoves]`. The king's moves include castling, checked for the rights, the rook on its starting square and a clear path (only the castling king and rook may stand on it), but not for check, which 2.5 adds. The test also compares whole-side counts with the Java at `prohibitSelfCheck = false` (start 20, Kiwipete 48 / 43 for Black, perft positions 3–5: 16, 38, 44) and makes and unmakes every generated move.
- [x] **2.4b** The attack check.
  - Verify: for hand-built positions, attacked / not attacked is right for squares hit by each attacker type (pawn of each colour, knight, bishop, rook, queen, king), including blocked sliders.
  - `ChessMoves.IsSquareAttacked(board, square, byColor)`, looking outward from the square (see [Legality checking](docs/legality-checking.md)). The test compares the whole attacked set, all 64 squares, with a hand-written list for each position.
- [x] **2.5** Legal move list (play, attack check, undo), with the `prohibitSelfCheck` option; setting it to `false` skips the check filtering (for Fog of War later).
  - Verify: 20 legal moves from the standard start; pinned pieces and moves into check are excluded; castling out of, through or into check is excluded; with `prohibitSelfCheck = false` those moves are included.
  - `ChessMoves.GenerateLegalMoves`/`GenerateLegalPieceMoves(..., prohibitSelfCheck)` filter the pseudo-legal list in place (`FilterLegalMoves`: make, `IsSquareAttacked` on the mover's king, unmake), plus `ChessMoves.IsInCheck`. Castling also needs every square from the king's square to its destination unattacked, checked as in the Java with the king lifted and the rook still on its square; the rook's squares don't count (in Chess960, O-O-O with the king staying on c1 is allowed with d1 attacked). The test also checks whole-side counts against published perft depth 1 (start 20, Kiwipete 48, positions 3–5: 14, 6, 44), and en passant that would expose the king along the rank.
- [x] **2.6** **Test:** a test behaviour that builds a few positions by hand with the constants (the standard start, plus positions with castling, en passant and promotion available). It checks that make followed by unmake restores every array exactly, and that the legal move counts match counts worked out by hand or with the Java. Include the Java bug cases (see [Bugs found in the Java](docs/java-bugs.md)): capturing a rook on its starting square removes that side's castling right, castling is refused when the rook is gone or another piece is in the way, castling removes both of that side's rights, and pawns can't capture their own pieces. Full perft from published positions needs FEN, so it comes in step 3.
  - Verify: the whole step 2 test behaviour logs only `PASS`.
  - `TestPositions` in `RulesTest.cs`: shallow perft (`CheckPerft`, a `[RecursiveMethod]` perft that also checks every root move's make/unmake restores both arrays exactly) for the standard start built with the constants (to depth 3), a hand-built position with castling both ways for both sides, en passant and promotion all available (`r3k2r/1P6/8/3pP3/8/8/8/R3K2R w KQkq d6`: 36, 719, 21611; 36, 852 with `prohibitSelfCheck = false`) and its mirror for Black (same counts), and the published perft positions 2–6 to depth 2 or 3. The counts for the hand-built position come from the Java on `port-reference` (a throwaway perft class run against its jar, which also reproduced every published count used here). The Java bug cases are checked through the legal move list: lines played move by move (`CheckLine`) with the castling rights and the castling moves on offer checked after each ply and every ply undone exactly, plus single-piece lists for a missing rook, pieces in the castling path and pawns next to their own pieces.

### 3. FEN

**Read:** [udon-constraints](docs/udon-constraints.md) (regex, no I/O), [move-encoding](docs/move-encoding.md) (castling files, undo and halfmove clock), [modes](docs/modes.md) (Chess960), [java-bugs](docs/java-bugs.md) (comparing against the Java), [testing](docs/testing.md)

- [x] **3.1** FEN parsing and writing, including Chess960 castling letters and the king/rook starting files.
  - Verify: parsing then writing returns the same string for the standard start, Kiwipete, a position with en passant, and Chess960 positions; the king and rook starting files are set; malformed FENs are rejected with a `false` result rather than a broken board.
  - `ChessFen.ParseFen(fen, board, state, out halfmoveClock, out fullmoveNumber)`/`WriteFen` in `Assets/Siggi/ChessBoard/Rules/ChessFen.cs`, parsed by hand (no regex). On failure the arrays are left untouched. Castling accepts `KQkq` (X-FEN: the outermost rook on that side), Shredder letters, or a mix; writing uses `KQkq` when the rook files are a and h, Shredder letters otherwise, as the Java. Stricter than the Java on purpose (see the end of [Bugs found in the Java](docs/java-bugs.md)). The checks are `TestFen` in `RulesTest.cs`: round trips (the positions above, partial rights, Shredder and X-FEN on Chess960 positions, an inner rook named by its letter, counters left out), the state's files, and 36 malformed FENs that must leave the arrays as they were, plus `null`.
- [x] **3.2** **Test:** round-trip a set of FENs; compare against the Java's output for the same positions.
  - Verify: every FEN in the set round-trips, and matches the Java's output.
  - `TestFenJava` in `RulesTest.cs`: 29 FENs (perft positions 1–6 and position 4 mirrored, en passant including one the pawn can't take, promotion, high counters, the PGN test set's final positions including mate and stalemate, Chess960 in Shredder letters). Each must write back as the Java's `FEN.serialize(FEN.deserialize(fen))`, which is the FEN itself for all but `HAha` with the rooks on a and h (written `KQkq` by both). It also compares the FEN after every legal move with the Java's, as a move count and the int sum of the FENs' Java `String.hashCode` (order-independent, so no move matching is needed); the test works out the halfmove clock and move number after each move as the board will. The expected values come from a throwaway Java class run against `port-reference`'s classes (not kept).
- [x] **3.3a** **Test:** a test behaviour running perft from published positions (standard start, Kiwipete, the other standard perft positions, some Chess960 positions), loaded from their FENs, and checking the counts. Record the timings.
  - Verify: every count matches the published values, to the depth the Udon speed allows; record the depths and timings next to this item.
  - Done (2026-10-11): the deep perft section of `RulesTest.cs` (`StartDeepPerft`), run after the other checks and spread across frames in `Update` (`perftMsPerFrame`, default 20 ms; `deepPerft` off skips it). It walks the tree with an explicit stack instead of recursion, and one walk gives every shallower depth's count too. After each root move the position must be restored exactly. The counts are the published ones (chessprogramming.org Perft Results and Chess960 Perft Results), and the Java on `port-reference` gives the same. All 17 pass; Editor (ClientSim) work time, about 103 s in all:

    | Position | Depth | Final count | Legal lists | Time | ms per list |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | Standard start | 4 | 197,281 | 9,323 | 8.1 s | 0.87 |
    | Kiwipete | 3 | 97,862 | 2,088 | 4.0 s | 1.90 |
    | Position 3 | 5 | 674,624 | 46,256 | 29.4 s | 0.64 |
    | Position 4 | 4 | 422,333 | 9,738 | 18.4 s | 1.89 |
    | Position 4 mirrored | 4 | 422,333 | 9,738 | 18.2 s | 1.87 |
    | Position 5 | 3 | 62,379 | 1,531 | 2.7 s | 1.73 |
    | Position 6 | 3 | 89,890 | 2,126 | 3.1 s | 1.45 |
    | Chess960 1 | 4 | 326,672 | 12,739 | 12.5 s | 0.98 |
    | Chess960 2–10 (the published list's next nine) | 3 | 10,471–31,058 | 500–1,149 | 0.4–1.3 s each | 0.88–1.11 |

    A legal list costs about 0.9–1.9 ms in the Editor, so about 1.3–2.9 ms in the client at 1.5× slower. That's roughly 1.8× the 1.5 stand-in for the same positions (start 0.87 vs 0.49 ms, Kiwipete 1.90 vs 1.29 ms). The PGN import and notation estimates in 1.5 should be read with that in mind: about 3–7 plies per 10 ms budget rather than 5–10.
- [x] **3.3b** Published counts only cover normal rules, so also run some positions with `prohibitSelfCheck = false` and compare with the Java's counts for the same setting.
  - Verify: the counts match the Java's.
  - Added to the same deep perft section of `RulesTest.cs` (`AddPerftSelfCheckOff`, logged as `..., prohibitSelfCheck off`): standard start (depth 4: 197,742 against the published 197,281), Kiwipete (3), perft position 3 (4), position 4 and its mirror (3; White starts in check, so Black can capture the king at ply 2 and ply 3 is White moving with no king), position 5 (3), the 2.6 castling/en passant/promotion position (3), Chess960 1 (3) and Chess960 3 (4, O-O swaps king and rook). The expected counts come from a throwaway Java perft run against `port-reference`'s classes with `Board.prohibitSelfCheck = false` (not kept); the same class reproduced the 2.6 counts with it on. All 9 pass (2026-10-11), about 4.4 s of Editor work in all: the start at depth 4 in 1.3 s, Chess960 3 at depth 4 in 1.5 s, the rest 0.07–0.33 s each. A move list without the check filter costs 0.07–0.16 ms against 0.9–1.9 ms with it, so the filter (make, attack check, unmake per move) is about 85–90% of a legal list's cost.

### 4. Notation, endings and draw detection

**Read:** [legality-checking](docs/legality-checking.md), [move-encoding](docs/move-encoding.md) (undo, halfmove clock), [game-endings](docs/game-endings.md), [pgn](docs/pgn.md) (Export: castling notation), [testing](docs/testing.md)

- [x] **4.1** SAN generation with direct disambiguation and `+`/`#` suffix; `O-O`/`O-O-O`.
  - Verify: the expected SAN for hand-built positions with two knights or two rooks (disambiguated by file, by rank, and by both), pawn captures, promotions (with and without capture), castling on both sides, check and checkmate.
  - `ChessNotation.MoveToSan(board, state, move, legalMoves, legalCount, scratch, prohibitSelfCheck)` in `Assets/Siggi/ChessBoard/Rules/ChessNotation.cs`, taking the position's legal list from the caller (see [Legality checking](docs/legality-checking.md)). The `+`/`#` suffix makes and unmakes the move; only a check pays for the opponent's legal list (in `scratch`). With `prohibitSelfCheck` off there is no suffix (Fog of War, see [Modes](docs/modes.md)). The step 4 checks go in `RulesTest.cs` (`TestSan`), which keeps one test behaviour for the rules: 42 hand-picked moves (two and three knights, two rooks, a pinned knight that doesn't count unless `prohibitSelfCheck` is off, pawn captures, en passant, promotions and underpromotions with and without capture or check, castling both sides for both colours, with check and in Chess960, fool's mate, a back-rank mate, check with an escape), then every legal move's SAN in 22 positions (those plus perft positions 1–5) against the Java on `port-reference` (a throwaway class printing `notation` for each legal move, not kept). All 64 pass in Play Mode (2026-10-11).
- [x] **4.2** Checkmate and stalemate detection.
  - Verify: fool's mate is checkmate; known stalemate positions are stalemate; a position in check with an escape is neither.
  - `ChessMoves.PositionStatus(board, state, legalCount, prohibitSelfCheck)` returns `StatusNormal`, `StatusCheck`, `StatusCheckmate` or `StatusStalemate` for the side to move (Java `Board.getCheckType`, plus stalemate, which the Java doesn't have). It takes the position's legal move count from the caller, who has the legal list already, so it only adds one attack check. With `prohibitSelfCheck` off there is no check or checkmate, and no move at all reports stalemate for now (see [Open decisions](docs/open-decisions.md)). `MoveToSan` keeps its own check for the suffix, since it only needs the opponent's legal list when the move gives check. Checks in `RulesTest.cs` (`TestStatus`): 20 positions (start; fool's, scholar's, back-rank and smothered mate, Siggi-vs-Nuka's final position; five stalemates for both colours including a pinned knight and Sam Loyd's; check escaped by king move, block, capture and en passant; `prohibitSelfCheck` off). All 20 pass in Play Mode (2026-10-11).
- [x] **4.3a** Halfmove clock (a local per-ply array computed from the move history, not synced), and the fifty/seventy-five-move rules.
  - Verify: the halfmove clock resets on captures and pawn moves, includes the starting position's halfmove clock, undo restores the previous value, rebuilding the array from a move history matches the values recorded while playing it, and the fifty- and seventy-five-move conditions trigger at exactly 100 and 150 plies.
  - `ChessDraws` in `Assets/Siggi/ChessBoard/Rules/ChessDraws.cs` (4.3b and 4.3c add repetition and insufficient material to it): `ResetsHalfmoveClock(move)`, `HalfmoveClockAfter(clock, move)`, `BuildHalfmoveClocks(moves, moveCount, startClock, clocks)` (entry *i* = the clock before ply *i*; returns the current clock), `IsFiftyMoveRule(clock)` (≥ 100) and `IsSeventyFiveMoveRule(clock)` (≥ 150). The board doesn't exist yet, so the array is held by the test, which plays as the board will. Checks in `RulesTest.cs` (`TestHalfmoveClock`): which moves reset (pawn pushes, double pushes, en passant, promotions with and without capture, piece captures; not castling either side, king, knight or rook moves); a 17-ply game from a starting clock of 5 against hand-worked clocks; rebuilding from its history; undo back to the start; undoing five plies, playing on and rebuilding; the thresholds; a knight shuffle first reaching the rules after ply 100 and 150; starting clocks of 95 and 149 counting towards them. All 10 pass in Play Mode (2026-10-11).
- [x] **4.3b** Repetition detection.
  - Verify: threefold and fivefold are detected for a knight shuffle; positions that differ only in castling rights or en passant don't count as repeats.
  - `ChessDraws.CountRepetitions(board, state, moves, moveCount, halfmoveClock, scratchBoard, scratchState, scratchMoves, prohibitSelfCheck)` returns how many times the current position has occurred, counting now, and `IsThreefoldRepetition(count)` (≥ 3) and `IsFivefoldRepetition(count)` (≥ 5) test it. It walks back up to the last `min(halfmoveClock, moveCount)` plies with `UnmakeMove` on a scratch copy, stopping once the castling rights differ, and compares every second position's 64 squares and en passant. En passant only counts when the capture can actually be made (`EffectiveEnPassantFile`: legal with `prohibitSelfCheck` on, pseudo-legal with it off), as FIDE 9.2.3 says, so a double push with no pawn beside it doesn't stop a repeat. Checks in `RulesTest.cs` (`TestRepetition`): a knight shuffle from the start (counts after each ply, threefold first after ply 8, fivefold after ply 16) and from a FEN whose halfmove clock is above the plies played; kings stepping off and back losing castling rights (not counted) and a shuffle keeping one side's rights (counted); en passant after d4 with a capture available (not counted), with no pawn beside it (counted), with the capture illegal because it opens a rank to the king (counted), and the same with `prohibitSelfCheck` off (not counted); the thresholds; `EffectiveEnPassantFile` for eight positions. Every check also confirms `CountRepetitions` leaves the position unchanged. All 10 pass in Play Mode (2026-10-11).
- [x] **4.3c** Insufficient material.
  - Verify: K v K, K+B v K, K+N v K and K+B v K+B with same-coloured bishops are detected; K+B v K+B with opposite-coloured bishops, and anything with a pawn, rook or queen, are not.
  - `ChessDraws.IsInsufficientMaterial(board)`: no pawn, rook or queen, and either a single knight or bishops that all stand on one colour besides the kings (the standard cases, generalised to any number of same-coloured bishops, which can't mate either; see [Draw rules](docs/game-endings.md#draw-rules)). It only looks at the material; whether it applies with `prohibitSelfCheck` off, where kings can be taken, is in [Open decisions](docs/open-decisions.md). Checks in `RulesTest.cs` (`TestInsufficientMaterial`): 22 positions. Dead: K v K (also the `lichess-long-draw.pgn` final position), K+B v K and K+N v K for both colours, K+B v K+B with both bishops dark and both light, K+B+B v K on one colour. Not dead: K+B v K+B and K+B+B v K on opposite colours, a pawn for either side, K+R v K, K v K+Q, K+B v K+P, same-coloured bishops plus a pawn, K+N+N v K, K+N v K+N, K+N v K+B, K+B+N v K, the start. Every check also confirms the board is unchanged. All 22 pass in Play Mode (2026-10-11).
- [x] **4.4** **Test:** known positions for each ending and draw rule; SAN for ambiguous moves (two knights, two rooks, promotions, captures).
  - Verify: the whole step 4 test behaviour logs only `PASS`.
  - The single positions are already covered by 4.1–4.3c (`TestSan`, `TestStatus`, `TestHalfmoveClock`, `TestRepetition`, `TestInsufficientMaterial`). 4.4 adds `TestGames` to `RulesTest.cs`: whole games played as SAN (each move found by its `MoveToSan` among the legal moves, ignoring `+`/`#` and reading `0-0` as `O-O`; not the PGN import, which is step 5) with every ending check run after each ply, as the board will run them. Each game checks the SAN of every move played, the final FEN (halfmove clock and move number included), and the first ply at which each ending (checkmate, stalemate, threefold, fivefold, fifty, seventy-five, insufficient material) holds, so an ending showing up early or in the wrong game fails too. 14 games: the nine PGN test set games with a fixed move list (fool's mate and Siggi-vs-Nuka checkmate at the last ply, Sam Loyd's stalemate, threefold in `repetition-names`, `lichess-long-draw` dead at ply 142 and nothing earlier; en passant, promotions, disambiguation and Chess960 with no ending), against the [PGN test set](docs/testing.md#pgn-test-set)'s final FENs and the files' own SAN (Siggi-vs-Nuka's against the Java's export of it, which adds the `+`/`#` VRChess leaves out); a 16-ply knight shuffle (threefold at 8, fivefold at 16); the fifty-move rule at ply 4 from a halfmove clock of 96, the seventy-five-move rule at ply 4 from 146, a capture restarting the count from 97; and a capture leaving K+N v K. Pre-checked outside Unity (making two expectations wrong fails both). All 415 checks pass in Play Mode (2026-10-11).

  - `ChessPgnImporter` in `Assets/Siggi/ChessBoard/ChessPgnImporter.cs`, the importer behaviour from [Architecture](docs/architecture.md): `StartImport(text, capacity, budgetMs, target, eventName)`, then `ImportNext` in `Update` until the budget (`DefaultBudgetMs` = 10) is used, with at least one step per frame; `eventName` is sent to `target` when it finishes; `Abandon()`; `ImportFor(budget)` for a frame by hand or (huge budget) an import in one go; frame count, longest frame and total time kept. See [Import steps](docs/pgn.md#import-steps). Checks in `PgnTest.cs` (`StartFrameImports`, through its new `importer` field; the summary line now comes after them, a few frames after Start): `lichess-long-draw` with 10 ms, `Siggi-vs-Nuka` with 1 ms, and `formatting` and `malformed/illegal-move` at one step per frame (0 ms). Each is imported in one go (result and ply count checked) and then over frames, and must give the same result, failing move, plies, position, moves, SAN, clocks and tags, in at least 2, 2, 24 and 4 frames. An abandoned import stops after its one frame and sends no event. Each import logs an `importer timing:` line. Pre-checked outside Unity: everything passes except the two "at least 2 frames" checks, since plain .NET imports each of those files in about 1 ms. An importer that doesn't reset its cursor fails the checks. All 133 pass in Play Mode (2026-10-11). Timings in the Editor: `lichess-long-draw` takes 17 frames at 10 ms (longest 12.4 ms, 172 ms in all, the same in one go); `Siggi-vs-Nuka` takes 50 frames at 1 ms (longest 3.8 ms, 89 ms); one step costs about 1–4 ms. The budget question is recorded in [PGN import](docs/pgn.md#import-local-rare).

  - Verify: seated players can change mode and clock unless forced; Start works only with both seats filled; 1000 generated Chess960 positions are all valid (bishops on opposite colours, king between the rooks, same for both sides) and many are distinct.
  - `SetMode`/`SetModeAs`, `SetClock`/`SetClockAs`, `StartGame`/`StartGameAs`, `IsPlayer`, `IsModeForced`/`IsClockForced` on `ChessBoard`, and `ChessGame.Chess960Position`/`Chess960Fen` (Scharnagl numbering, 518 = standard); details under [Implementation](docs/board-lifecycle.md#implementation) and [Chess960](docs/modes.md#chess960). Decided here: the mode can be changed only in the lobby, the clock also on a loaded board; choosing the forced value is refused like any other while forced; the forced values are copied over the players' choices on Start (7.2 can apply them on snapshot too, so the lobby shows them); a time control is no clock or 1 s to 24 h with a 0 to 1 h increment; Start sets the clocks and turn start (running them is 7.3). The forced bits are read from the snapshot fields, which these checks set directly until 7.2 takes the snapshot. Checks in `BoardTest.cs`: who may change mode and clock in each lifecycle state (local and named players, non-players, invalid modes and time controls leaving things unchanged); forced mode, forced clock, both, forced "no clock" with an increment, and a forced clock on a loaded board; Start refused with one seat, by a non-player, on reset, loaded, in-game and ended boards, and accepted by either player or the local player in both seats, giving the starting position, clocks and turn start; positions 518, 0 and 959; all 960 positions valid, numbered as Scharnagl (worked out independently from the back rank, so all distinct) and their FENs read back; 1000 positions from `RandomChess960Index` valid with at least 500 distinct; 20 Chess960 starts from valid positions with matching starting FEN, counters and replay board. Pre-checked outside Unity: 174 pass; ignoring either forced bit, not applying forced settings, starting with one seat, changing the clock in game, a skewed random index, allowing an increment with no clock, a wrong bishop file, a wrong knight slot or missing castling rights each fail checks (or crash the run). All 174 pass in Play Mode (2026-10-11).
- [x] **7.2** Settings snapshot when the board leaves the reset state; re-snapshot on request.
  - Verify: the snapshot is taken on the first join (and on loading a game); changing the arbiter afterwards doesn't affect the board; a re-snapshot picks up the change.
  - On `ChessBoard`: the Inspector fields `arbiter` and `boardNumber` (wired in the scene to the scene's arbiter, board 0; an empty field logs an error on `Start` and snapshots everything off), `TakeSnapshot()`, `IsGameSettingOn`/`GameNumber`, and `Resnapshot()`/`ResnapshotAs(who)` for organizers (`arbiter.IsOrganizer`) on any board but a reset one; details under [Snapshot](docs/settings.md#per-board-overrides). Decided here: the snapshot applies the forced lobby settings as it's taken (mode and clock in the lobby, only the clock on a loaded board, nothing once a game has started), so the lobby shows them; a re-snapshot that lifts forcing leaves the shown values as the players' choices. Loading doesn't exist until 7.9a, so these checks take the loaded board's snapshot with `TakeSnapshot()` directly, and 7.9a checks that loading calls it. Checks in `BoardTest.cs` (`TestSnapshot`, on the arbiter's overrides for the board's number, cleared after; the 7.1 checks now first override every bit of that board off, so the arbiter's defaults can't force anything on them): none on a reset board; taken on the first join; unchanged by later arbiter changes, the second join and Start; re-snapshot in game (mode, clock and times kept), on an ended board, in the lobby (new forcing applied, old forcing lifted); refused for a non-organizer (`simpleAuth` emptied for a moment), no player, a reset board and with no arbiter; forced values shown in the lobby; a loaded board gets the forced clock but keeps its mode, and joining it doesn't re-snapshot; conflicting numbers corrected; the defaults without overrides, unchanged when the defaults change until a re-snapshot; an out-of-range board number gets the defaults; no arbiter gives everything off. Pre-checked outside Unity: 244 pass (also with the arbiter forcing mode and clock by default; with no arbiter only the "arbiter assigned" check fails); no snapshot on join, a snapshot on every join, no organizer check, re-snapshot on a reset board, no forcing on snapshot, the forced mode on a loaded board, forcing in game, or the defaults instead of the board's effective settings each fail checks. All 244 pass in Play Mode (2026-10-11).
- [x] **7.3** Clocks: running time from the turn-start timestamp, increment, clock history, flag fall.
  - Verify: with a short time control, a move reduces the mover's time by the time taken and adds the increment; the clock history has one entry per move; a side running out of time is detected.
  - On `ChessBoard`: `ServerTime`, `HasClock`, `IsClockRunning`, `RemainingTime`/`RemainingTimeAt`, `FlaggedColor`/`FlaggedColorAt`, and `PlayMove`/`PlayMoveAt`, the move-recording path the input API (7.4a) will call; details under [Implementation](docs/clocks.md#implementation). Decided here: playing a move needs a recording path before the input API exists, so `PlayMoveAt` takes a packed move, checks it against the legal list itself and records its SAN, halfmove clock and move number too; the elapsed time is whole milliseconds rounded down, and a time before the turn start counts as none passed; the flag falls at exactly 0, and a move is refused once it has, so only the side to move can flag; the clock runs in replay and stops in every other state but in game; no clock writes `-1` to the clock history; a time is capped at `int.MaxValue`; the history arrays double together when full. Checks in `BoardTest.cs` (`TestClocks`, at given server times in binary fractions so the milliseconds are exact): 60+2 with each move's time and increment, the stopped clock of the side not to move, the turn start reset and the clock history (one entry per move, `-1` after); still running in replay but no moves; flag fall at exactly 0 and not 0.5 s before, staying fallen, a move refused after it and accepted 0.25 s before; illegal moves refused; stopped when ended, not running in the lobby or on a loaded board; 1+0 with 1 ms to spare and a flag fall after exactly 1 s; the real server time through Start; the overflow cap; no clock (`-1` history, no flag fall, SAN `Qh4#`, the position); 266 plies past two growths with every clock, SAN and halfmove entry and the empty entries after. Pre-checked outside Unity: 341 pass (also with the arbiter forcing mode and clock by default; with no arbiter only the "arbiter assigned" check fails); leaving out the increment, the elapsed time, the turn start reset, the flag check on a move, the clock history, the legality check, the replay check, the move number, the overflow cap or the `""` in grown notation, letting both clocks run, a negative remaining time, running when ended, stopping in replay, or rounding the elapsed time up each fail checks. All 341 pass in Play Mode (2026-10-11).
- [x] **7.4a** Input API: `PiecePickedUp`, `PieceDropped`, promotion choice.
  - Verify: legal drops are played, illegal ones rejected; a pawn reaching the last rank answers "promotion choice needed", and the follow-up call with each piece plays the right promotion.
  - On `ChessBoard`: `PiecePickedUp`/`PiecePickedUpAs`, `PieceDropped`/`PieceDroppedAs`, `PromotionChosen`/`PromotionChosenAs` and `InputPromotionNeeded`; details under [Implementation](docs/player-input.md#implementation). A matched move is played with `PlayMove` (7.3), which records it and runs the clocks. Decided here: only the side to move's player may pick up or drop, and only that side's pieces (a player in both seats moves both); nothing in replay, out of game, or once the side to move's flag has fallen; castles are left out of drop matching until 7.4b; a promotion drop plays nothing and the choice is a separate call with the same squares, so the board keeps no pending promotion; activity timestamps wait for 8.5. Checks in `BoardTest.cs` (`TestPieceInput`, `TestPromotion`): pickups by the right and wrong player, colour, empty and out-of-range squares, non-players; illegal drops, a drop back on its own square, out of turn, from an empty square, a promotion choice on a non-promotion, all leaving the position; five legal drops with their SAN and position; nothing in replay, ended, lobby, loaded or after the flag; the local player in both seats; each of the four promotions on b8 (choice needed, position unchanged until chosen, piece, move type, SAN), capture promotions, Black's, refused choices (pawn, king, none, type 7, the other player, a non-player, an illegal square, a king move) and the local player's methods. Pre-checked outside Unity: 448 pass (also with forced defaults; with no arbiter only the "arbiter assigned" check fails); no seat check, a seat of either colour, picking up either colour, no replay or flag check, promoting to a queen unasked, ignoring the chosen type, allowing a pawn, or ignoring the to square each fail checks. All 448 pass in Play Mode (2026-10-11).
- [x] **7.4b** Input API: castling by drop. (`KingDroppedOnCastleSpace` and `IsCastleDropSpaceNeeded` were removed in 9.6.)
  - Verify: castling by dropping the king on its destination and on its own rook works; in the ambiguous Chess960 cases, the destination drop is the normal move and `KingDroppedOnCastleSpace` castles.
  - On `ChessBoard`: castle matching in `PieceDropped`, `KingDroppedOnCastleSpace`/`KingDroppedOnCastleSpaceAs(name, side)` with `CastleKingSide`/`CastleQueenSide`, and `IsCastleDropSpaceNeeded(side)` for the view (9.3b); details under [Implementation](docs/player-input.md#implementation). Decided here: a drop on the destination is ambiguous exactly when a normal legal king move from the same square ends there (or the king doesn't move), worked out from the legal moves rather than from "the king moves one square"; a drop on the rook always castles (no other move can end on the king's own rook); the castle drop space castles in unambiguous positions too; a promotion choice never matches a castle. Checks in `BoardTest.cs` (`TestCastling`): standard castles on the destination and on the rook, both sides and colours, with SAN, squares and the position after; no space needed there; refused without rights, through check, by the other player or out of turn, a rook dropped on the king, a promotion choice on the rook, a non-player, invalid sides, in replay and once ended; the space castling in a standard position; Chess960 king f1 and rook h1 (Kg1 on the destination, the space and the rook castle, three-square queen side on the destination, no space in replay), king d1 and rook a1 (Kc1, the space castles), the king already on g1 and on c1 (drop on own square refused, the space and the rook castle), king f1 and rook g1 swapping on a drop on g1 with no space offered; no space in the lobby; the local player's methods. Pre-checked outside Unity: 593 pass (also with forced defaults); leaving castles out, no rook-square match, no destination match, the destination winning over the normal move, castling on a promotion choice, the space ignoring the side or who may move, offering the space always, not for a king that doesn't move, in the lobby or in replay, or swapping g and c each fail checks. All 593 pass in Play Mode (2026-10-11).
- [x] **7.4c** Input API: the buttons.
  - Verify: each button method acts only when it applies, and is rejected otherwise (e.g. resign by a non-player).
  - Decided at the start of this step: every button's effect has its own later step, so 7.4c adds a `Can…` check per button on `ChessBoard`, worked out from the state fields that already exist, and the Verify is checked on those; each later step's method refuses whenever its check doesn't hold, and the view (9.4a/b) shows a button only while it does. Checks: `CanJoinAs`, `CanSetModeAs`, `CanSetClockAs`, `CanStartGameAs` (now used by their 7.1 methods), `CanResignAs`, `CanOfferDrawAs`, `CanAcceptDrawAs`/`CanDeclineDrawAs`, `ClaimableDraw`/`CanClaimDrawAs`, `CanEnterReplayAs`, `CanLeaveReplayAs`, `CanStepToAs`, `CanRequestTakebackAs`, `CanAcceptTakebackAs`/`CanDeclineTakebackAs`, `CanContinueAs`, `CanResetAs`, `CanLoadGame`, each with a local-player form; details under [Buttons](docs/player-input.md#buttons). Also decided here: buttons acting for one colour take it (a player in both seats chooses); where organizers count, the `*As` form takes an `organizer` flag; resign, draw offers and claims need the game running (in progress, flag not fallen) and work in replay; a claim is threefold first, then fifty-move, only for the side to move; the replay controls are for seated players and organizers, or anyone while nobody is seated (bystanders after idling come with 8.4/8.5); a takeback needs a game position with at least one move after it, in a running or ended game, and its answer needs the requested ply still shown; "Continue from here" also works from the last position; loading only onto a reset board. Checks in `BoardTest.cs` (`TestLobbyButtons`, `TestResignAndDrawButtons`, `TestReplayButtons`, `TestTakebackButtons`, `TestContinueResetLoadButtons`, `TestLocalButtons`): each check by the right player, the wrong colour, a non-player, an empty name and invalid colours, in every lifecycle state, in and out of replay, after the flag, with the setting on and off, with and without a pending offer or request, the forced lobby settings refusing in both check and method, a threefold repetition after 8 plies (not after 7) and the fifty-move rule at 100 (not 99), the loaded board with nobody, one and both seated, and the local player's forms. All 810 pass in Play Mode (2026-10-11).
- [x] **7.5a** Game endings and result; resign; time forfeit.
  - Verify: checkmate, stalemate, resignation and time forfeit each set the right result; time forfeit is a draw when the opponent can't checkmate and draw rules allow it, and a loss under "No draws".
  - On `ChessBoard`: endings checked after each move in `PlayMoveAt`, `EndGameAt`, `Resign`/`ResignAs(color, name)`, `CheckFlagFall`/`CheckFlagFallAt(now)` (called from `Update` while in game), and `ChessDraws.HasNoMatingMaterial(board, color)`; details under [Time forfeit and Implementation](docs/game-endings.md#time-forfeit). Decided here: the "draw rules allow it" of the forfeit exception is the dead-position rule alone, so under "No draws" or with only the other draw rules on a timeout always loses; "can't checkmate" is the opponent having only its king or the whole board being insufficient material, so K+N against K+P still wins on time; ending a game stops the clock with the side to move's remaining time stored (0 on a forfeit), lets a pending draw offer lapse, keeps a takeback request and replay mode; checkmate and stalemate end the game whatever the draw settings; until step 8 every client ends the game on flag fall (only the owner will). Checks in `BoardTest.cs` (`TestMateAndStalemate`, `TestResign`, `TestTimeForfeit`): fool's mate with a clock (winner, `0-1`, the mating move's clock entry, times kept, no flag fall, move or resign after it), scholar's mate by drops with every draw rule on (`1-0`, `Qxf7#`, the draw offer lapsing), a check that isn't mate, stalemate under "No draws" and every draw rule (`1/2-1/2`, times kept) and a near-stalemate; resign by each player, refused for the wrong colour, a non-player, an empty name, colour 2, once ended, after the flag and in the lobby, from replay (replay and takeback request kept), the clock stopped at the resigning side's time, the local player in both seats; forfeit at exactly 0 and not 0.5 s before, for each colour, once only, times kept, no move or resign after it, the draw exception with the dead-position rule on, every draw rule, "No draws", every draw rule but dead position and only non-draw settings, K v K+N, K+P v K+N, K+N v K+N, bishops on one colour and on both, K+B and K+P against a flagging lone king and K+Q flagging against K+B, in replay, never without a clock, in the lobby or on a loaded board, and with the real server time. Pre-checked outside Unity: 1034 pass (also with forced defaults; with no arbiter only the "arbiter assigned" check fails); no mate detection, the wrong mate winner, no stalemate, the wrong resign winner, resigning without the check, the exception on any draw rule or none, ignoring the material, checking the flagged side's material, only a lone king or only insufficient material counting, counting both colours' pieces, not stopping the clock, keeping the draw offer, the flagged side winning, or a forfeit without a fallen flag each fail checks. All 1034 pass in Play Mode (2026-10-11).
- [x] **7.5b** Draw offers and claims per the snapshot's draw rules.
  - Verify: offer/accept/decline works when agreement is on; claims are offered only when the condition holds and the rule is on; automatic draws (fivefold, seventy-five-move, dead position) end the game; under "No draws" none of them do.
  - On `ChessBoard`: `OfferDraw`/`OfferDrawAs`, `AcceptDraw`/`AcceptDrawAs`, `DeclineDraw`/`DeclineDrawAs`, `ClaimDraw`/`ClaimDrawAs` (each refusing when its 7.4c check doesn't hold), and `AutomaticDraw()`, asked by `PlayMoveAt` after a move that neither mates nor stalemates; details under [Implementation](docs/game-endings.md#implementation). Decided here: an offer lapses when the side it was made to plays a move (FIDE 9.1.2.3), and the offerer's own move keeps it; the automatic draws are checked in the order fivefold, seventy-five-move, dead position (they can't coincide in practice), each only with its rule on, and checkmate and stalemate come first. Checks in `BoardTest.cs` (`TestDrawAgreement`, `TestDrawClaims`, `TestAutomaticDraws`; the scholar's-mate check of 7.5a now makes White's offer just before the mate, since Black's earlier offer would lapse on White's first move): offers refused for the wrong colour, a non-player, colour 2, twice, while one is pending, with agreement off, under "No draws", in the lobby; answers refused for one's own offer, a non-player, the wrong colour, a declined or lapsed offer, after a re-snapshot turns agreement off, after the flag and once ended; decline, accept (`1/2-1/2`, the clock stopped with the time kept), in replay (replay kept), the offer kept by the offerer's move and lapsing on the other side's; threefold claimed by the side to move only, not with the rule off or under "No draws", threefold before fifty-move, fifty-move at 100 and not 99 or after a pawn move, in replay, not after the flag; fivefold after 16 plies and not 15 (clock stopped), not with the rule off or under "No draws" (20 plies); seventy-five-move at 150 and not 149, not with the rule off or under "No draws", a mate on the 150th ply; dead position for K v K and K+B v K, not for K+R v K or bishops on both colours, not with the rule off or under "No draws"; a stalemate leaving K+N v K; an offer lapsing on an automatic draw; the local player's methods. Pre-checked outside Unity: 1300 pass (also with forced defaults; with no arbiter only the "arbiter assigned" check fails); no offer lapse on a move, a lapse on every move, no automatic draws, any of the three ignoring its rule, fivefold at three, the wrong agreement winner, a decline keeping the offer, an offer for the other colour, every claim as fifty-move, accepting, offering or claiming without the check, or an automatic draw before mate or stalemate each fail checks. All 1300 pass in Play Mode (2026-10-11).
- [x] **7.6** Game IDs and `ContinuedFrom`.
  - Verify: IDs match `yyyyMMddHHmmss-xxxxxx` with the UTC start time; `ContinuedFrom` is `<GameID>/<ply>` with the right ply.
  - `ChessGame.GameId(utc, suffix)` and `ChessGame.ContinuedFromText(gameId, ply)`; on `ChessBoard`, `NetworkDateTime()` (`Networking.GetNetworkDateTime()`), `NewGameIdAt(utc)` (random suffix), the new ID given in `StartGameAs`, and `ContinueGameIdAt(ply, utc)` for 7.8b and 7.9b; details under [Implementation](docs/game-ids.md#implementation). Decided here: the start time is VRChat's network time rather than the client's own clock, so it's UTC and every client agrees on it; no ID before Start (the lobby has none); an imported ID in another format is referenced as it is; a continued game references the game it came from directly, so a chain links step by step. The ply passed in is the replay's, so the "right ply" of a takeback and a continued loaded game is checked again in 7.8b and 7.9b. Checks in `BoardTest.cs` (`TestGameIdFormat`, `TestGameIds`): fixed times and suffixes (the docs' example, single digits, the largest values, a short year, suffixes out of range refused); `ContinuedFrom` for plies 0, 3 and 142, a foreign ID, none and null; 1000 random IDs well formed, at least 995 distinct and spread over the range; Start's ID well formed and between the network times before and after, the network time within 2 minutes of `DateTime.UtcNow` (logged with UTC and local time), kept by a move and the game ending; 20 starts (half Chess960) giving 20 distinct IDs; continuing at ply 3 and again at ply 0 from the new ID, from no ID and from a foreign one; reset and Start clearing `ContinuedFrom`. Pre-checked outside Unity: 1336 pass (also with forced defaults; with no arbiter only the "arbiter assigned" check fails); no ID on Start, not clearing `ContinuedFrom` on Start, referencing the new ID, a five-digit random range, an hour off UTC, an unpadded month or suffix, `/ply` without an ID, or a seven-digit suffix allowed each fail checks. The local time zone here is UTC, so the UTC check can't tell local time from UTC on this machine. All 1336 pass in Play Mode, and the `UdonSharp` compiler takes the `DateTime` constructor and subtraction (2026-10-11).
- [x] **7.7** PGN logging on the "in progress → ended" transition, skipping games with no moves.
  - Verify: the Editor log has exactly one `[ChessPGN]` entry per ended game, with the right result and `Termination`, and none for a game with no moves.

Decided here: the log entry is a `[ChessPGN]` line followed by the PGN; the Date comes from the Game ID; Event defaults to empty (`?`) and Site to `VRChat`; `LogGame` takes the ending and winner so 7.8b and 7.10 can log an unfinished game as `*`. Checks in `BoardTest.cs` (`TestPgnLog`; at the end of the run the test logs the board's `loggedGames`, which must equal the number of `[ChessPGN]` entries in the log, since the earlier tests end games too): `DateFromGameId` for an ID, a foreign one, no dash, a letter, empty and null; the whole PGN of fool's mate with 60+2 (tags, clocks, wrapping), nothing logged while in game and nothing more after it ends; no log for a resignation, a flag fall or a draw agreement with no moves; resignation (no clock, `ContinuedFrom`, move text), time forfeit lost and drawn, stalemate, draw agreement, threefold and fifty-move claims, fivefold, seventy-five-move and insufficient material, each logged once with its `Result`, `Termination` and the result ending the move text; Chess960 (`Variant`, `SetUp`, `FEN`); a game starting with Black at move 30 and a name with `"` escaped. Each of these mutations fails checks: no logging, logging games with no moves, logging twice, logging before the ending is set, the first side not worked out, no clock written `?`, no Date, no `ContinuedFrom`, and Event and Site swapped. All 1469 pass in Play Mode, and the Editor log has 33 `[ChessPGN]` entries, each with one `Result` and `Termination`, matching `loggedGames` (2026-10-11).
- [x] **7.8a** Replay mode: replay board, stepping, free moves, custom flag.
  - Verify: stepping reaches every position of the game; free moves change only the replay board and set the custom flag; stepping back onto the game history clears it; leaving replay restores the game exactly.
  - On `ChessBoard`: `EnterReplay`/`EnterReplayAs`, `StepTo`/`StepToAs`, `LeaveReplay`/`LeaveReplayAs`, `ReplayPiecePickedUpAs`/`ReplayPieceDroppedAs`; in replay, `PiecePickedUp`/`PieceDropped` and their `*As` forms do free moves; details under [Implementation](docs/replay.md#implementation). Decided here: stepping undoes the game's moves from the current position each time; there's one step method and the view steps by one with `replayIndex ± 1`; every free move is custom, even one matching the game's next move; free moves place any piece of either colour anywhere, replacing what's there, with no promotion or castling, and are open to whoever may work the replay controls; leaving puts the replay fields back on the current position. Cancelling a takeback request when the replay position changes is 7.8b's. Two earlier checks that set `replayMode` by hand and expected drops to be refused (7.4a, 7.4b) now enter replay through the method and expect a free move that leaves the game untouched. Checks in `BoardTest.cs` (`TestReplay`): a 12-ply game with en passant, captures and castling, each position recorded; entering (refused for a bystander and twice), showing the current position, the clock still running; stepping to every position backwards, forwards and out of order; stepping refused to the ply shown, out of range and by a bystander, accepted by an organizer; free moves of either colour's pieces by either player and an organizer, a pawn onto the king's square unpromoted, refused for an empty square, its own square, off the board or a bystander, promotion choices and the castle space refused, the game untouched, custom until stepping back to its ply or elsewhere; leaving (refused for a bystander and twice) with the whole game state, history arrays included, as before, and play going on; a game with no moves; an ended game; a loaded board set up by hand (anyone while nobody is seated, not once someone is, no leaving); the local player's methods. Pre-checked outside Unity: 1607 pass (also with forced defaults; with no arbiter only the "arbiter assigned" check fails). Each of these mutations fails checks: undoing one move too few, stepping that keeps the custom flag, free moves that don't set it, free moves for anyone, free moves on the game board, entering without showing the position, not resetting the replay fields, the `*As` drop or pickup ignoring replay, and picking up from an empty square. All 1607 pass in Play Mode, with 35 `[ChessPGN]` entries matching `loggedGames` (2026-10-12).
- [x] **7.8b** Takeback request/accept (if the snapshot allows takebacks) and truncation (restoring clocks), logging an unfinished game first and giving the continued game a new ID.
  - Verify: an accepted takeback truncates the history and restores the clocks at that ply; a request is cancelled when the replay position changes; it's refused from a custom position or when takebacks are off; an unfinished game is logged once with `*`; the continued game has a new ID and `ContinuedFrom`.
  - On `ChessBoard`: `RequestTakeback`/`RequestTakebackAs`, `DeclineTakeback`/`DeclineTakebackAs`, `AcceptTakeback`/`AcceptTakebackAs` (each refusing when its 7.4c check doesn't hold); details under [Implementation](docs/replay.md#implementation). Decided here:
    - Leaving replay cancels a request, like stepping and free moves, since a request needs the replay showing its ply.
    - Accepting logs a game in progress before anything changes, then gives the new ID, cuts the history and restores the clocks. Each side gets the clock history entry of its own last move before the ply, or the base time if it hadn't moved or the entry is `-1`.
    - The turn starts at the moment of accepting. A fallen flag is up again, so a time forfeit can be taken back.
    - The continued game is in progress out of replay, with no result, no draw offer and no request.
    - No `*At` form: tests bound the turn start by the server time before and after.
  - Checks in `BoardTest.cs` (`TestTakeback`):
    - Requests refused out of replay, by a non-player, for the other colour, with takebacks off, while one is pending, and from a custom position.
    - Answers refused for the requester, a non-player, the wrong colour, and with takebacks off. Refused calls leave the game.
    - Cancelled by stepping (not accepted back on its ply), a free move and leaving. Declining clears the request and changes nothing else.
    - A 60+2 game taken back to ply 3 while in progress, with a draw offer pending: logged once as `*` with its own ID and whole history, then the position, empty history entries after the ply, both clocks, the turn start, `ContinuedFrom`, a new ID, and the history before the ply kept.
    - The continued game played on and logged in full with `GameID`, `ContinuedFrom` and the moves before and after the takeback.
    - Taken back once ended (nothing more logged, the result gone, `ContinuedFrom` to the game before), then to the start (base times, nothing left to take back), and to ply 4 with White to move.
    - A time forfeit taken back (flag up again).
    - Castling and en passant positions restored exactly and played again, with no clock staying 0.
    - The local player's methods.

Decided here:
    - Continuing clears the loaded game's `ending`/`winner` and all the replay source fields (`replaySourceBaseTime` back to `NoClock`); the clock is the chosen one, always valid. Nothing is logged on continuing.
    - The clock history before the continuation point is emptied (`-1`), as docs/replay.md has it ("the loaded game's clock history isn't carried over"): the continued game's export has no `[%clk]` for those moves, and takebacks may go back past the continuation point, restoring the base time there, which is what both sides had when continuing (the question left open in 7.8b).
    - Continuing is refused from a position that's already over: no legal moves (the final position of a game won by mate, a stalemate), or an automatic draw with its rule on in the snapshot (fivefold, seventy-five-move, insufficient material). Only a loaded game can show such a position.
    - `ContinueGameAs` doesn't apply the forced clock itself: the snapshot applies it on loading and on a re-snapshot, and `SetClockAs` refuses while it's forced.

Decided here:

### 8. Networking (`ChessBoard`, `ChessArbiter`)

**Read:** [networking](docs/networking.md), [ownership](docs/ownership.md), [inactivity](docs/inactivity.md), [organizers](docs/organizers.md), [settings](docs/settings.md) (the arbiter's synced settings, re-snapshot), [board-lifecycle](docs/board-lifecycle.md) (Reset), [legality-checking](docs/legality-checking.md) (notation fill-in), [pgn](docs/pgn.md) (Import, Logging), [game-endings](docs/game-endings.md) (log once per client), [architecture](docs/architecture.md) (listeners, for the debug view), [testing](docs/testing.md)

- [x] **8.1** Debug view: a minimal `ChessBoardListener` that shows the board state as text, with buttons that call the board's input methods (join, start, a few scripted moves, resign, replay, reset) and a few that change settings on the arbiter and re-snapshot. It lets each client act before `ChessBoardView` and the settings menu exist, and stays as a testing tool.
  - Verify: in Play Mode, the buttons play a game through to an ending, and the text matches the board state after each change.
  - `ChessDebugView` (`Assets/Tests/Debug/ChessDebugView.cs`), built in the scene by the editor menu **Tools > Chess Board > Create Debug View** (`Assets/Tests/Debug/Editor/ChessDebugViewBuilder.cs`) for the selected board, or the scene's only one; see [Testing](docs/testing.md#debug-view). It adds itself as a listener on `Start`. A change only marks it, and it redraws on the next frame and four times a second (for the clocks): redrawing on every notification made `BoardTest`, in the same scene, run past Udon's 10 s limit. Decided here:
    - Every button calls the board's local-player method, as the real view will. Moves go through `PiecePickedUp`/`PieceDropped` (a castle drops the king on its rook, a promotion takes a queen): the next move of a scripted game (fool's mate; a knight shuffle repeating `Nf3 Nf6 Ng1 Ng8`, for threefold claims and fivefold) or a random legal move.
    - Buttons that need a colour (resign, offer, takeback request) act for the local player's seat; a player in both seats acts for the side to move (the side that moved last for a takeback request); accept and decline answer for the side the offer or request was made to.
    - The settings buttons change the arbiter's overrides for the board's number (takebacks, all draw rules on or "No draws", clock forced to 3+2, clear), which the board only takes on the next first join or a re-snapshot. Since 8.2 they change the arbiter as an organizer and sync.
    - The text shows the lifecycle, last change, seats and clocks, mode, side to move and check, result and ending, pending offer and request, replay ply, game ID, the snapshot and the arbiter's current settings, the claimable draw, the moves, which `Can…` checks hold for the local player, and the last button's result (also logged as `[ChessDebugView]`).
  - In Play Mode, with `BoardTest` in the same scene: all 3410 `BoardTest` checks pass; the buttons played a knight shuffle to a threefold claim (after "No draws" was lifted and a re-snapshot), a 15 s game to a flag fall, and fool's mate to checkmate, each logged once with its result and `Termination`, the text matching the board after each change (2026-10-12).
- [x] **8.2** `ChessArbiter`: synced settings (instance defaults, per-board overrides, inactivity times) changed by an organizer taking ownership; `OnOwnershipRequest` allowing only organizers; `FirstJoinerAuth`'s synced first-joiner name. The board's ownership rules below rely on organizers being recognised on every client.
  - Verify (several clients): a setting changed by the organizer reaches every client; a non-organizer can't take the arbiter; every client agrees who the first joiner is, and they keep organizer rights after rejoining.
  - `ChessArbiter.BeginChange()` (organizers only; takes ownership), the existing setters plus `SetDefaultSetting`/`SetDefaultNumber`, then `EndChange()` (serializes); `OnOwnershipRequest` allows only organizers. See [Changing settings in-world](docs/settings.md#changing-settings-in-world). `FirstJoinerAuth` was already synced (6.2a). The debug view's arbiter buttons now go through `BeginChange`/`EndChange`, with new buttons for two instance defaults (takebacks, reset-available time) and **Take arbiter** (direct `SetOwner`, to see a refusal), and its text shows the arbiter's defaults, owner and first joiner; **Create Debug View** now replaces an existing view in place. Single-client checks in `SettingsTest.cs` (`TestChanging`): an organizer's `BeginChange` makes them owner and `EndChange` sends; `MayOwn` (what `OnOwnershipRequest` answers) allows an organizer, refuses no player and, with `NoOneAuth` swapped in a non-organizer, whose `BeginChange` is refused; the default setters (bits flipped and masked, times set and clamped, unknown mode corrected, unknown number refused), restored after. Pre-checked outside Unity (155 pass; ownership always allowed, `BeginChange` without the organizer check, `SetDefaultNumber` without `FixNumbers` and `SetDefaultSetting` without the mask each fail checks). The debug view logs the arbiter's settings when they change (`[ChessDebugView] arbiter settings: …`), so a client's log shows a change arriving.

Kötturinn's **Take arbiter** was refused (owner stayed Siggі), and was accepted only after Siggі left and VRChat handed Kötturinn the arbiter. Siggі rejoined, not master, was still organizer: `SettingsTest`'s `BeginChange` took the arbiter back from Kötturinn, and Kötturinn's next **Take arbiter** was refused again. As expected, a non-organizer's `SettingsTest` (and `BoardTest`'s re-snapshot checks, which need an organizer) failed, as did the master checks for the rejoined Siggі.
  - Second production run (2026-10-12): Siggі, first joiner, turned board 0's takebacks override off and the default takebacks off. Kötturinn, joining just after, reported the default takebacks off (its `SettingsTest` editor-defaults check fails on that, as it should for a client holding synced values). Siggі then set the default reset-available time to never, and on rejoining Kötturinn reported it as -1. Kötturinn's arbiter buttons were refused with `(not an organizer)`. The debug view's `arbiter settings` log wasn't in that build (the Udon programs were last compiled before it was added), so the logs show the changes reaching a joining client, not one already present (the same serialization); 8.8 checks that case.
- [x] **8.3** Board synced fields, manual sync, `RequestSerialization`, `OnDeserialization` → local state → listeners.
  - Verify (several clients): moves made on one client appear on the other; a late joiner gets the full state; no synced `string[]` element is ever `null`.
  - The state fields stay plain fields; `PackState()` (from `OnPreSerialization`) copies them into a few synced fields (both boards as `byte[64]`, the ints by `Sync*` index, the moves, clocks and notation cut to the game, the doubles and strings), and `ReceiveState()` (from `OnDeserialization`) checks and unpacks them, works out the halfmove clocks and tells the listeners with `lastChangeReceived` set. Notation is synced as a `string[]`, not a joined string. Every local change but a rejection takes ownership and calls `RequestSerialization`; only the owner checks flag fall. A client logs a game it had in progress when an update ends it, or replaces it unseen (logged from its own copy, on time if the flag had fallen). See [Networking](docs/networking.md#implementation). `BoardTest` gets single-client sync checks (`TestSync`: round trips including a 130-ply history and a custom replay, the halfmove clocks, spare room emptied, rejections not sent, `null` notation sent as `""`, logging on arrival, invalid updates ignored) and now runs only when alone in the instance. The debug view logs each received change with the state it brought (`[ChessDebugView] received …`), and an accepted button's resulting state, for comparing clients' logs.

Production run with two accounts (2026-10-12): Siggі, alone, saw all 3630 pass in VRChat too; Kötturinn joined later, had `BoardTest` skip itself, and got the reset board in its first update. They joined, Siggі started, and they played six moves alternately, each `received move` line matching the mover's FEN and game ID, with ownership passing to each mover. Kötturinn left and rejoined mid-game and got the whole game (4 moves, same FEN and ID) in its first update, then played on. No `null` notation errors, no Udon errors, and no `[ChessPGN]` entries from the run.
- [x] **8.4** Ownership rules in `OnOwnershipRequest`: players, organizers, idle-reset exception.
  - Verify (several clients): both players and organizers can take ownership; a bystander is denied while both players are active; the idle-reset exception is checked once 8.5 is in.
  - `OnOwnershipRequest` → `MayOwn` → `MayOwnAs(name, organizer, BothPlayersPresent(), now)`: organizers, seated players, anyone while a seat is empty or its player isn't in the instance (by display name), and anyone once `IsIdleForResetAt(now)` (not reset, reset-available time not "never" and passed since `lastActivity`). The exception needed the activity time now, so every change the board sends stamps `lastActivity` (8.5 still adds the throttled piece touches, the bystander reset and auto-reset). See [Ownership](docs/ownership.md#implementation). The debug view gets **Take board** (direct `SetOwner`, owner reported then and 3 s later) and an ownership line (both players here, idle time, whether the local player may take it). `BoardTest` gets `TestOwnership` (lobby, game and ended board: players, organizer, bystander, exact names, a player away, the reset-available time, "never", 0, the stamping, `MayOwn`/`OnOwnershipRequest` for the local player alone).
  - Pre-checked outside Unity: 3675 `BoardTest` checks pass; allowing every request, dropping the stamp, ignoring "never", a reset board counting as idle, and the presence check ignoring Black each fail checks.
  - Production run with three accounts (2026-10-12), Siggі the first joiner (organizer): Siggі (White) and Kötturinn (Black) in the lobby each took the board with **Take board**; Thе Cаt, a bystander, was refused (owner still Siggі 3 s later). After Siggі's reset, Kötturinn (White) and Thе Cаt (Black) joined and each took the board, and Siggі, now an organizer outside the seats, took it too. No Udon errors. Not yet seen: `BoardTest`'s 3675 checks in Play Mode (every client skipped them, not being alone), and a bystander allowed once a player has left (beyond this Verify); the idle-reset exception is checked in 8.5.
- [x] **8.5** Inactivity: shared server-time timestamp, throttled activity updates, reset-available and auto-reset (including "never").
  - Verify (several clients, short times): reset becomes available to a bystander after the reset-available time; the board resets itself once after the auto-reset time; "never" disables each; handling pieces doesn't serialize more often than the throttle allows.
  - `CanResetAs` allows anyone once `IsIdleForResetAt`; the owner's `Update` calls `CheckAutoResetAt(now)` (`IsIdleForAutoResetAt`, then the reset button's steps, logged as `[ChessBoard] <board>: reset after N s idle`). Every piece input first calls `TouchAsAt(name, organizer, now)`, which stamps `lastActivity` and sends it as the new `ChangeActivity` (not told to local listeners), at most once per `ActivityThrottle` (5 s). Decided here: during a game outside replay only the side to move's touches count, since the waiting player's would race the move being made and could send the position from before it; in replay whoever may work the replay controls; in the lobby and an ended game outside replay none. A bystander gets the reset button once the board is idle, not the replay controls. See [Inactivity](docs/inactivity.md#implementation). The debug view shows the idle time with both deadlines and the board's send count, and gets **Default auto-reset time** (60 s, 600 s, never) and **Fidget** (a pickup every 0.5 s for 30 s, then logs pickups, activity stamps and sends). `BoardTest` gets `TestInactivity`.
