The owner-only call in `Update` needs real clients.
  - Production run with three accounts (2026-10-12): Siggі, alone, saw all 3820 `BoardTest` checks pass in VRChat. Siggі set the default auto-reset time to 60 s and the reset-available time to 30 s before joining. Bystander reset: Thе Cаt's **Reset** was refused 11 s and 24 s after the last move and accepted after 32 s. Auto-reset: 60 s after the last move, Siggі (the owner) logged `reset after 60 s idle` once, and the other two received one `reset` each. Fidget by Kötturinn, the side to move: 60 pickups in 30 s gave 6 activity stamps and 6 sends, and the other two clients received activity updates 5 s apart (never closer). "Never" for both: Thе Cаt's reset was refused 65 s and 74 s after the last move, and the board hadn't reset itself 110 s after it. Each reset game was logged once as unfinished on every client; no Udon errors. Not seen: the other player's fidget (no sends), which `BoardTest` covers.
- [x] **8.6** Notation sync and fill-in for missing entries.
  - Verify: notation arrives with each move; with entries removed on one client (via the debug view), fill-in recomputes them to match.
  - Notation already went out with every update in 8.3; this step adds the fill-in. `ChessBoard.FillNotationFor(budgetMs)` walks from the position before the first gap through the history, computing each missing SAN with `MoveToSan`, for about the budget (at least one entry); `Update` runs it on every client as `FillNotationFrame()` with `fillBudgetMs` (`DefaultFillBudgetMs`, the import's 10 ms) while gaps may exist. Every change to the history calls `RecheckNotation()`, which drops a walk under way. Nothing is sent. See [Networking](docs/networking.md#implementation-1). Decided here:
    - Listeners hear of it through a new virtual `ChessBoardListener.OnNotationFilled()`, once nothing is missing (a walk over several frames is told at its end), not `OnBoardChanged`: only the move list's text changes, and a new `Change*` value would replace the `lastChange` a view animates from.
    - A game's PGN (`GamePgn`, logging) fills every gap first in one go, so no logged game has an empty move.
  - The debug view gets **Forget notation** (blanks every entry but every fourth, locally) and logs `notation filled in: …` with whether the filled entries match the forgotten ones, and `received notation missing from ply P` for an update with gaps. `BoardTest` gets `TestNotationFill`, run last: loaded games with every entry forgotten filled in as imported (Siggi-vs-Nuka to its mate, promotions from a FEN with Black to move, disambiguation, Chess960 castling); the listeners told once on the next frame and nothing else told or sent; a 130-ply game with every fourth entry kept taking one frame per missing entry (98) with no budget, told once at the end; an update with gaps arriving during a walk; a reset during a walk; export and logging filling in first.
  - Pre-checked outside Unity: 4045 `BoardTest` checks pass (also with forced defaults; with no arbiter only the "arbiter assigned" check fails), with 90 `[ChessPGN]` entries matching `loggedGames`. Each of these mutations fails checks: no recheck on receiving, the PGN not filling in, no budget, listeners told at once, never or during a walk, and no walk back to the gap.
  - First production run with three accounts (2026-10-12): Thе Cаt received all 40 random moves of Siggі and Kötturinn with their notation, and Kötturinn each of Siggі's (`Nfd2`, `Rxh2`, `Nd4+` among them). Two problems, both fixed: the board told its listeners after every frame of a walk, so Kötturinn's **Forget notation** compared mid-walk and reported 22 entries differing (blanks not filled yet), and is now told only at the end; and Siggі's `BoardTest`, alone, was halted by Udon's 10 s limit during the fill checks (it already took about 9 s before them), so it now runs over three frames (`RunChecks`, `RunSyncChecks`, `RunFillChecks`; see [Testing](docs/testing.md#debug-view)).
  - Second production run (2026-10-12), after an upload that still had the code from before those fixes and repeated both problems: Kötturinn, alone, saw all 4045 `BoardTest` checks pass over the three frames (04:42:54 to 04:43:03), with 90 `[ChessPGN]` entries matching `loggedGames`; 130 plies took 23 frames at the 10 ms budget. Thе Cаt then joined, had `BoardTest` skip itself, and received each of Kötturinn's 40 random moves with its notation (`Qxb5+`, `O-O-O`, `axb6`, `Rdf1` among them). Thе Cаt's **Forget notation** blanked 30 of the 40 entries, and one `notation filled in` line followed: a walk of 7 frames, all 40 entries matching. No Udon errors.
- [x] **8.7** Imported games written in one update at the end; abandon if the board changed.
  - Verify (several clients): an import appears on every client in one update when it finishes; changing the board during an import abandons it and tells the importing player.
  - The board runs the import: `StartImport(text)` (`CanImport()`: a reset board, by anyone, with the new `importer` field set) starts the board's own `ChessPgnImporter` (room for `ImportCapacity` = 1024 plies, `importBudgetMs` per frame), which sends `_ImportFinished` when done; a finished import goes through `LoadImport`, so it's sent in the one `ChangeLoaded` update, and a failed one changes and sends nothing. Nothing is sent and nobody takes ownership meanwhile. See [Imported games](docs/networking.md#imported-games). Decided here:
    - The import is abandoned on the change itself, not checked when it finishes: any change made here but a rejection (`NotifyChanged`), or arriving from another client but a touch (`ReceiveState`, `ChangeActivity`). The importer is stopped and a later done event ignored, and the importing player is told at once.
    - The importing client's listeners hear of it through a new virtual `ChessBoardListener.OnImportChanged()` (started, loaded, failed, abandoned), with `importStatus` and `importMessage` (`failed at move 2.: illegal move`, or that the board changed). A loaded import is also an `OnBoardChanged` (`ChangeLoaded`), told first.
    - One importer per board, since starting an import abandons the importer's current one. The importer gets a `paused` flag (`Update` runs no steps while set; for testing).
  - The debug view gets **Import PGN** (Siggi-vs-Nuka, its new `importPgn` field), **Import PGN (paused)** (held, so the board can be changed meanwhile on any client) and **Resume import**, shows the import's status, and logs each change to it as `[ChessDebugView] import …`. **Create Debug View** sets `importPgn` and adds a `ChessPgnImporter` as a child of a board with no `importer`. `BoardTest` gets `TestImport` (refusals with no importer and in the lobby; nothing changed, told or sent while importing; loaded and sent as `ChangeLoaded` with all 54 moves; a second done event ignored; a failed import's reason; abandoned by a local join, a late done event ignored; a rejection and a received touch not abandoning, a received join abandoning; starting again replacing it), then an import over real frames through the importer's `Update` and done event, before the summary.
  - Pre-checked outside Unity: 4111 `BoardTest` checks pass (also with forced defaults; with no arbiter only the "arbiter assigned" check fails), with 90 `[ChessPGN]` entries matching `loggedGames`. Each of these mutations fails checks: no abandoning on a local change or on receiving, abandoning on a received touch or a rejection, the importer not stopped, the done event not checking the status, the load abandoning itself, and importing onto a board that isn't reset.
  - Production run with three accounts (2026-10-12): Siggі, alone, saw every `TestImport` check pass and the import over real frames load Siggi-vs-Nuka in 13 frames (longest 13.1 ms, 142.6 ms in all). Kötturinn and Thе Cаt then joined (`BoardTest` skipped). Siggі's **Import PGN**: `import loaded` (54 moves, game `20250710001921`), and each of the other two logged one `received loaded` with the same 54 moves, FEN and game ID. Siggі's **Import PGN (paused)**, then Kötturinn's **Join White**: Siggі received the join and logged `import abandoned (the board changed before the import finished)` in the same second, and **Resume import** was then refused (no import under way). Siggі's own **Join White** during a held import abandoned it the same way. No Udon errors. The run showed one regression, which has been fixed: **Create Debug View** had copied the board's Udon fields to its proxy before setting the importer, which wiped the board's Arbiter field (every client logged it empty, and Siggі's only `BoardTest` failure was "arbiter assigned"). It now sets only the importer on the proxy.

Pre-checked outside Unity: the view and `ViewTest` run against stubs, all 849 checks passing but the one needing a real frame (Nc6 drawn on the next frame); target marks in replay, pickups or clicks while the picker shows, pickupable not updated when a promotion starts or stops waiting, the last move marking a castle's rook square, the king-stays castle not marking the rook, the picker not turned for Black, no held targets on the pickup or on `Refresh`, a cleared promotion leaving the picker shown, no clamp over the a file, no last move in replay, no marks for a selected piece and Queen playing a rook each fail checks. In VR the picker couldn't always be used: with the hand near a piece, the piece was grabbed instead of the button being pointed at. So no piece is a pickup or a click target while the picker shows; then all 849 `ViewTest` checks pass, and promoting in VR works with the hand near the pieces.

The pieces' colliders stay as they are, on the `Pickup` layer, which doesn't collide with players. A new **Player Blocker** object holds a solid `BoxCollider` filling the table up to its top (below the board's surface, so it doesn't catch pointing or clicks). On `ChessBoardView`: `blocker` and `UpdateBlocker()` (every `Refresh`), which enables the blocker unless the local player has a seat (`board.IsPlayer`). Players collide only on their own client, so each client's blocker stops only its own player. Details under [Walking through the board](docs/physical-pieces.md#walking-through-the-board-step-95a2). Decided here: "part of the game" is having a seat, from joining until the board is reset, so a lobby's and an ended game's players can walk in too, and organizers without a seat can't.
  - Setup: run **Tools > Chess Board > Create Board View** again (this replaces the view, so then **Create View Test** and **Create Debug View** again to rewire them).

Decided here (the user's suggestion): the castle drop space is removed, since a king dropped on its rook castles in every case (no normal king move ends on its own piece), the ambiguous Chess960 cases included. Two Chess960 games with ambiguous castles: `brqbnnkr` (the king already on g1, so castling doesn't move it) and `rkbbrnqn` (the king b1 to c1, one square). Siggі castled `O-O` and `O-O-O` by dropping the king on its rook in VR, the only drop that castles there. Kötturinn castled both times by clicking the rook. On all three clients every `pieces settled` line and **Check pieces** was `all in place`; Siggі's only pieces away were held ones (`held here`), and Kötturinn's one away on **Pickups** too. A bystander on **Pickups** mid-game (Thе Cаt, in the previous run) had 0 pickupable. Seen in passing: Thе Cаt's `RulesTest` once ran past Udon's 10 s limit on joining, with three clients on one machine (it passed on every other run that day). It's a test behaviour and unrelated to this step. Not seen in production: dropping on the destination for the normal king move (`Kg1`/`Kc1`) and a castle by drop on **Pickups**; `ViewTest`'s sixth frame covers both through the same drop path.

### 10. Archive and history wall (`GameArchive`)

**Read:** [archive](docs/archive.md), [networking](docs/networking.md) (Limits), [game-ids](docs/game-ids.md), [replay](docs/replay.md) (continuing a loaded game), [board-lifecycle](docs/board-lifecycle.md) (Reset), [game-endings](docs/game-endings.md) (what gets logged/archived), [testing](docs/testing.md)

- [x] **10.1a** Archive storage: one `GameArchive` controller with 10 synced `GameArchiveHolder`s of 5 games each (50 games, a circular log overwriting the oldest, read back in chronological order). Every board on every client hands each game it logs (finished, or unfinished on reset or takeback) to its own client's archive; only the archive's owner writes to the holders, and only it may own them. Each client also keeps the last 5 games it logged in a local-only holder, archived by whoever owns the archive if missing.
  - Verify: adding games fills the holders in order; the 51st game overwrites the oldest and reading stays chronological; each game reads back with the same data; adding a game serializes only its holder; a game already archived (same ID) isn't added again; a game logged by a board arrives in the archive; recent games missing from the archive are added, oldest first, when the owner flushes (as on becoming the owner).
  - Decided here (the user's design, replacing pages of 20 games with a 200-game capacity and a retry check for two boards adding at once): one writer, the archive's owner, so additions never race; the recent holder covers the owner leaving just as a game ends.
  - On `GameArchive` (new): `holders`, `recent`, `changes`, `Capacity`, `AddGame`, `StoreRecent`, `Flush`, `AllReady`, `IsArchived`, `OnHolderChanged`, `ClearArchive` (tests), and the readers `GameCount`, `SlotAt`, `SequenceAt`, `GameIdAt`, `ContinuedFromAt`, `WhiteAt`, `BlackAt`, `StartFenAt`, `ModeAt`, `EndingAt`, `WinnerAt`, `BaseTimeAt`, `IncrementAt`, `MoveCountAt`, `CopyMovesAt`, `CopyClocksAt`. On `GameArchiveHolder` (new): `Capacity`, `Info*`, `archive`, `localOnly`, the slot arrays, `ready`, `sends`, `SlotOf`, `Info`, `MoveCount`, `MoveOffset`, `CopyMoves`, `CopyClocks`, `WriteSlot`, `Clear`, `Send`, `MayOwn`, `PackState`, `ReceiveState`. On `ChessBoard`: `archive`, handed every logged game by `LogGame`. On `ViewTest`: `finished`. Details under [Implementation](docs/archive.md#implementation).
  - Setup: run **Tools > Chess Board > Create Game Archive** (builds the archive and its holders, and sets every board's `archive`), then **Tools > Chess Board > Create Archive Test**; each creates its program assets on the first run, so run it again once UdonSharp has compiled.
  - Checks: `ArchiveTest`, after `BoardTest` and `ViewTest`; see [Testing](docs/testing.md#archive-test). Pre-checked outside Unity: the archive, holders, board and `ArchiveTest` run against stubs, all 229 checks passing; overwriting the wrong slot on wrap, archiving games already archived, reading unsorted, rebuilding the flattened moves wrongly, taking an invalid update and keeping duplicates in the recent games each fail checks. The builders are editor code and compile only in Unity.
  - First run (2026-10-13): in Play Mode all 230 `ArchiveTest` checks pass (the one the pre-check skipped, the earlier tests' games archived, included), with all 4157 `BoardTest` and 1045 `ViewTest` checks.
- [x] **10.1b** Owner handover: when the archive's owner leaves, the new owner archives the recent games it logged that aren't in the archive.
  - Verify (several clients): only the archive's owner sends holders, and a non-owner is refused a holder; with the owner leaving just as a game ends, the game ends up in the archive (added by the new owner); two boards ending at the same moment both end up in the archive.
  - The handover itself was built in 10.1a (`GameArchive.OnOwnershipTransferred` flushes the recent games); this step adds the tooling to see it with several clients, and the second board. Details under [One writer](docs/archive.md#one-writer) (Handover).
  - Tooling: `GameArchive` logs each game it archives and becoming the owner, and has `held` (for tests: archives nothing, as an owner that left before archiving); `GameArchiveHolder` logs each ownership request it refuses, and a `Send` that finds the holder someone else's. The debug view gets an **Archive** line (logged on each change: games, owner, holders ready and their owners, recent games waiting, the newest three) and the buttons **Take holder**, **Hold archive**, **Resign at :x0** and **Check archive**. New menu item **Tools > Chess Board > Create Board** (`ChessBoardBuilder`): another board beside the last, with the next board number, the arbiter, the archive and its own importer. Details under [Debug view](docs/testing.md#debug-view) (Archive) and [More boards](docs/architecture.md#more-boards).
  - Setup: run **Tools > Chess Board > Create Debug View** again (the new buttons; the canvas is taller). Then **Tools > Chess Board > Create Board**, and with the new **ChessBoard 2** selected, **Create Board View** and **Create Debug View**. Save the scene.
  - Procedure (Siggі first, so the master and the archive's owner; Kötturinn and Thе Cаt joining): (1) Kötturinn presses **Take holder**: refused on both clients, Siggі still the owner 3 s later; every `[GameArchive] archived` line is in Siggі's log only. (2) Siggі presses **Hold archive**; Kötturinn and Thе Cаt play a game on board 1 (fool's mate); every client's archive line shows it waiting; Siggі leaves. The new owner logs `now owned here: 1 recent game(s) added` and archives it; the other client's archive line gets it; Siggі rejoins and sees it too. (3) Two games on the two boards (one player in both seats of each, or two pairs), both players pressing **Resign at :x0** within the same ten seconds: both games in every client's archive.
- First run (2026-10-13), in production with Siggі, Kötturinn and Thе Cаt (Siggі, alone first, logged all 4157 `BoardTest`, 1045 `ViewTest` and 230 `ArchiveTest` checks passing): Kötturinn's **Take holder** was refused (`[GameArchiveHolder] Holder 0: ownership refused for Kötturinn`, Siggі still the owner 3 s later), and every `[GameArchive] archived` line was in the owner's log only (Siggі's, then Thе Cаt's after the handover; none in Kötturinn's). Siggі held the archive, Kötturinn and Thе Cаt played a game, and Siggі left: Thе Cаt, now the owner, logged `archived game 1, … (Kötturinn vs Thе Cаt)` and `now owned here: 1 recent game(s) added` the moment Siggі left, Kötturinn's archive line had it at once, and Siggі rejoining received it. In a new instance, Siggі held the archive again while Kötturinn and Thе Cаt each played both seats on their own board and resigned with **Resign at :x0** at the same server time (`-1309810.017` and `.012`); with Siggі leaving, Kötturinn's line showed both `WAITING`, and Thе Cаt archived both and logged `2 recent game(s) added`. A second pair with Thе Cаt the owner archived both games at once, but they ended 10 s apart: Thе Cаt pressed 1.5 s before the ten seconds were up, which the button pushes to the next ten. Since nothing in Udon runs at the same time, two games arriving together are archived one after the other like any two. Server time was negative in this instance, which the button handles. Seen in passing: one `ViewTest` run (Siggі alone in the second instance) failed `status: game started: the panels show it` (`White: Ana  5:00`): the server time ticked past a whole second between the view drawing its panels and the test reading the time. The test now accepts the panels' text at either moment; in Play Mode afterwards, all 1045 `ViewTest` checks pass (with all `RulesTest`, `SettingsTest`, `BoardTest` and `ArchiveTest` checks), twice.

Loading clears the pick, so the other boards stop offering the game. (2) **Late joiner:** Thе Cаt joins: its archive line arrives at `50/50`, holders ready 10/10, the same newest games, the wall full; **Check archive** matches Siggі's. (3) **Owner leaving as a game ends, archive full:** Siggі presses **Hold archive**; Kötturinn and Thе Cаt play fool's mate on board 1; Siggі leaves: the new owner logs `now owned here: 1 recent game(s) added`, the game is the newest, overwriting the oldest; Siggі rejoins and receives it. (4) **Two boards ending at once:** a game on each board (one player in both seats of each), each playing at least one move, then both pressing **Resign at :00** in the same minute (before its last 3 s): both resign at the same server time and both games are archived on every client. (5) **Continuing a loaded game:** a player picks a long fill game on the wall, **Load it here** on board 1, steps back a few plies, two players join and **Continue from here**, play a move and resign: the new game is archived with `continued from <the loaded game's ID>/<ply>`, and its `[ChessPGN]` entry has the `ContinuedFrom` tag. Load that game in turn and continue it again: it references the second game, not the first.
  - Runs (2026-10-13, in production with Siggі, Kötturinn and Thе Cаt): scenario 4 with **Resign at :00**: Siggі and Kötturinn each played both seats of a board (6 and 8 plies) and both resigned at server time 1519440 (`1519439.985` and `.990`); Siggі, the owner, archived both (`archived game 51` and `52`, in Holder 0 slots 0 and 1, overwriting the oldest of a full archive), and both other clients' archive lines had them at once. Seen in passing: Kötturinn and Thе Cаt were still loading into the instance while Siggі's tests ran, so they weren't counted yet (`BoardTest` didn't skip itself) and received the tests' board states in order on joining. To them a `ViewTest` game cleared by the test-only `ClearState()`, which doesn't log, went from in game to reset, so they logged it as unfinished (`20261013140830-832047`, `1. e4 d5 *`), and their recent games kept it as `WAITING`, since the owner never had it; had either become the owner, it would have archived that test game. It's a test artefact (a real reset logs on every client, the owner included), so the procedure now has the others join only once Siggі's log shows `[ArchiveTest] done`.
  - Second run (2026-10-13, the same three): Siggі alone first (all 4157 `BoardTest` and 313 `ArchiveTest` checks passing), Kötturinn joining after. (1) Six presses of **Add 10 games** on Siggі archived the 60 fill games as #51 to #110 (80 plies, or fewer for the few ending in checkmate), overwriting the oldest in turn, holders 0 to 9 slot by slot; Kötturinn's archive line followed each one. (2) Thе Cаt joined with the archive full: `0/50`, then `50/50`, holders ready 10/10, newest #110. (3) Siggі held the archive, Kötturinn played a 14-ply game in both seats (0-1), Siggі left: Thе Cаt, the new owner, logged `now owned here: 1 recent game(s) added` and archived it as #111 in Holder 2 slot 0 (overwriting #61); Siggі rejoining received it. (5) Fill game #78 (`…661947`, 65 plies, checkmate) loaded on board 1 (both others `received loaded`, notation filled in), continued by Siggі and Kötturinn from ply 62 and ended (0-1, 66 plies): archived as #112 `continued from 20261013141552-661947/62`, with its `[ChessPGN]` `ContinuedFrom` tag. That game loaded and continued from ply 7 (1-0, 11 plies): #113 `continued from 20261013141923-681201/7`, the second game, not the first.
  - Found then: Kötturinn's archive line counted a recent game as `WAITING` again once the fill overwrote it (`…853341`, archived as #40, overwritten by #90): a client counted every recent game not in the archive as waiting, including one archived and later overwritten, so on becoming the owner it would have archived its old games again as the newest (in a busy instance, any client's recent games older than the last 50 archived). Thе Cаt got the handover, so it didn't happen here. Fixed: `GameArchive` remembers per recent slot whether it has seen the game archived (`recentSeen`, marked at every `Flush` on every client) and never archives a seen one again; `IsRecentWaiting(slot)` is new, and the debug view's `WAITING` counts use it. `ArchiveTest` gets `RunOverwrittenRecentChecks` (13 checks, see [Archive test](docs/testing.md#archive-test)). Pre-checked outside Unity: `GameArchive` and `GameArchiveHolder` against stubs, 16 checks of the same sequence passing; without the seen check, the overwritten `rec-2` comes back as the newest. (Kötturinn had again started joining while Siggі's tests ran, so the procedure's rule is about starting to join, not being counted.)
  - With the fix, in Play Mode all 326 `ArchiveTest` checks pass (the 13 new ones included), with all 415 `RulesTest`, 155 `SettingsTest`, 4157 `BoardTest` and 1045 `ViewTest` checks. Seen in passing: an Udon exception (`Networking.IsOwner` throwing in `OnLeftRoom`, relayed into the Editor log by UdonSharp's log watcher) came from another world, Siggі's home world with ProTV (`wrld_4ef4b651…`, visited between runs), not from this one: no client logged one in the chess world.

### 12. Presentation

Splitting the board's world presentation from its UI, changes to how the physical board looks, sounds and plays (replaceable models, piece sets per colour, captured-piece places, new move and check indicators, a falling king, untimed play on after a flag fall, promotion by ghost pieces, sound effects), and a 2D view of any board (with its own replay, a move list, and moving and resizing it). The logical board and the arbiter change only where a substep says so (12.3's and 12.10's default piece sets, 12.4's capture order, 12.7's and 12.8's getters, 12.9). Builders are updated as each substep needs; the prefabs come in step 14.

**Read:** [architecture](docs/architecture.md) (layers, listeners), [physical-pieces](docs/physical-pieces.md), [player-input](docs/player-input.md), [udon-constraints](docs/udon-constraints.md), [testing](docs/testing.md) (view test), and each substep's docs below.

A new `ChessBoardUI` (a `ChessBoardListener` of its own) takes the button panels and their `Button*` actions, the status display, the PGN box and Load PGN, and `historyWall` ("Load it here"). Neither references the other; both listen to the board and act through it. Its builder (a new menu item, or **Create Board View** building both) places the UI around the table as today.
  - Verify: with both rebuilt for each board, every `ViewTest` check passes (split between a view test and a UI test where it needs the parts separately), with every other test; by hand, a game is played to the end with the buttons, and the PGN box and Load PGN work.

Docs: [architecture](docs/architecture.md), [physical-pieces](docs/physical-pieces.md) (status display moves to the UI), [player-input](docs/player-input.md) (buttons, PGN, Load PGN), [pgn](docs/pgn.md) (Input / output), [testing](docs/testing.md).

The builder adds the UI on an object of its own beside the view (`ChessBoardUI (<board>)`, where the view stands) and wires the history wall to it (**Create History Wall** too); `ChessSettingsMenu` takes `ModeText` from the UI. `ViewTest` gets `ui` and `Redraw`; `ArchiveTest`'s `view` becomes `ui`. **Create View Test** now works with two boards (the existing test's view, or the selected board's) and repoints `ArchiveTest` at the test it replaces. A world creator can delete the **Board** and **Squares** objects from a view and put their own model in their place, and can scale the whole view up or down, without anything breaking: placement, drops (`DropSquare`), clicks, highlights, lines, animations, captured pieces and the player blocker all work in the view's local space, nothing reads the board model, and the square targets (which take clicks) stay separate from the model. Fix anything that today depends on them or assumes scale 1.

  - Found here: the view already placed everything in its local space and read nothing from **Board** or **Squares**, and `ChessSquare` needed no change. What could break was a rebuild (which brought the deleted model back and dropped the creator's), and groups (**Pieces**, **Highlights**, **Square Targets**) moved or scaled on their own, since the view places their children in its space. So: **Create Board View** keeps a creator's model and the model parts they deleted (`KeepCustomModel`); the view warns on Start when a group isn't at its origin (`LocalSpaceProblem`); `squareSize` gets a tooltip saying it isn't for resizing. Details under [Board model and scale](docs/physical-pieces.md#board-model-and-scale-step-122).
  - Decided here: scaling is the view's transform (uniform), not `squareSize`; the UI, a separate object, is given the same scale by the world creator. Verified by running every test in each configuration (`ViewTest` plays games by drops and clicks, with captures, highlights and animations, and now checks each piece's world position too) rather than by hand, through a test menu (**Tools > Chess Board > Model Check**) that sets the configurations up without saving.
  - First run (2026-10-14): all 1142 `ViewTest` checks (the new local-space check included), 415 `RulesTest`, 297 `SettingsTest`, 4157 `BoardTest` and 326 `ArchiveTest` checks pass, with no error logged, in four runs: as built; with **Board** and **Squares** replaced by a cube and the view (and its UI) at 0.5; at 2; and at 2 after rebuilding the view, which kept the cube and left out Board and Squares. The scene was then reloaded from disk.
- [x] **12.3** Chess piece sets: a world creator can use any set of piece models, whatever their rotation, size, origin and material slots, and change the set at run time (e.g. a world giving its supporters other pieces).
  - **`ChessPieceSet`:** a behaviour (no synced variables) on a `GameObject` holding one template child per piece type and colour (12), each with the mesh's `MeshFilter` and `MeshRenderer` and its materials, all slots, set for that colour. The template's local position, rotation and scale under the set are the piece's offset, rotation and size relative to its square, in the view's local units, so a model facing any way, of any size or with its origin off its base is set up by moving, turning and scaling its template in the editor, where it can be seen. A one-square guide under the set (hidden in play) shows the size to fit. A different mesh per colour is allowed.
  - **Where a board's set comes from:** the view's own `pieceSet` if set, otherwise the arbiter's `defaultPieceSet`. Both can be changed at run time, locally (not synced): `ChessBoardView.SetChessPieces(set)` (`null` goes back to the arbiter's) and `ChessArbiter.SetDefaultChessPieces(set)`, which refuses `null` (returns `false`). The arbiter only holds the reference, for the views to read; it never touches the set. The build check (like the `SimpleAuth` one) cancels a build while an arbiter's `defaultPieceSet` is empty.
  - **Applying a set:** each piece object takes the template's mesh, materials and local transform for its type and colour, and keeps them on promotion; the colliders are refitted, and the ghosts (12.11) and the falling king (12.8) follow the set's meshes. (Amended in 12.10: one set per colour, each side swapped on its own; and in 12.11: each set also holds 4 promotion templates for the ghosts, a see-through green in the default sets.) A changed set (the view's, or the arbiter's while the view has none) is applied at once, without moving any piece off its square. Whether views notice an arbiter change by polling (as the settings menu polls `changes`) or another way is decided here.
  - Verify: in Play Mode with a second set (models turned 90°, twice the size, origin off centre, two material slots), the pieces stand upright on their squares at the right size with both slots coloured per side; they can be picked up and clicked and their colliders fit; switching sets at run time on the view and on the arbiter changes the pieces in place, `SetChessPieces(null)` goes back to the arbiter's, and `SetDefaultChessPieces(null)` is refused; an empty `defaultPieceSet` cancels a build.
  - Document in `docs/customizing.md`: making a set (the templates, the guide, materials per colour), assigning it on the arbiter or a view, and changing it at run time.
  - Files: new `ChessPieceSet.cs`, `ChessBoardView.cs` (`meshes`, `pieceFilters`, collider fitting), `ChessArbiter.cs`, `Editor/ChessArbiterBuildCheck.cs`, `Editor/ChessBoardViewBuilder.cs` (a default set from the generated meshes and materials in `Models/`, put on the arbiter). Docs: [physical-pieces](docs/physical-pieces.md), [settings](docs/settings.md) (the arbiter's fields), [organizers](docs/organizers.md) (the build check), [customizing](docs/customizing.md).
  - Setup: run **Tools > Chess Board > Create Board View** for each board (select it first; the first run creates `ChessPieceSet.asset`, so run it again once UdonSharp has compiled; the first board's run puts **Chess Pieces** on the arbiter), then **Create View Test** (with the first board selected; it builds the test set) and **Create Archive Test**. Save the scene.
  - Decided here: each template stands in a slot of its own (the set's child, standing for the square), with its own guide, and the template's transform is read relative to its parent, rather than all 12 templates and one guide directly under the set, where they'd overlap; the set hides its children on Start (keep its own object active). The arbiter tells the views of a new default (`OnDefaultPieceSetChanged`): each view registers as one of its piece set listeners on Start (`AddPieceSetListener`). (First built with each view polling `defaultPieceSet` every frame; changed at the user's request, since a cross-behaviour read every frame on every view costs more than a call on each change.) Drops are read from the piece's base (`PieceBase`) rather than its origin. The set is checked by automated checks (`ViewTest`, and the build check's editor test) rather than by hand. Details under [Piece sets](docs/physical-pieces.md#piece-sets-steps-123-and-1210).

- [x] **12.4** Captured-piece places: target `GameObject`s placed by the world creator (per colour, in order) where captured pieces stand, replacing the two columns beside the board. Their height may differ from the board's: a captured piece's trip there arcs from the board's surface to the target's, and a piece taken back (takeback, replay) travels back the same way. Pieces stand in their places in the order they were captured, never reordered, and every client, late joiners included, shows the same order: the order comes from the game's move history (the board has it on every client), not from each client's own history of placements. In replay, the captured pieces are those of the position shown.
  - **A promoted pawn that's captured** goes to its place as a pawn.
  - **Where there's no history to read, the places are worked out deterministically**, from what every client has (positions, never local state), so every client shows the same places. No move history is made up to lead to the position: the pieces missing from it are put straight into places, in a fixed order:
    - *A game starting from a FEN with pieces missing:* the pieces missing from it, per colour, fill the first places, before the game's own captures, in a fixed order (queen, rooks, bishops, knights, pawns). Missing means short of the standard set (1 queen, 2 rooks, 2 bishops, 2 knights, 8 pawns); a type with more than its standard count (a promotion the FEN doesn't record) counts each extra piece against the missing pawns.
    - *A custom replay position:* the places of the game position the free moves started from (the ply shown before them), then the pieces the free moves took off (that position's pieces missing from the custom one, kings included) appended in the same fixed order, king last.
    - *A piece whose promotion the history can't show* (an extra queen in the starting FEN, or a promoted piece a free move took) keeps its type when captured.
  - Verify: in Play Mode, with targets at a different height from the board, a game with several captures puts each captured piece on the next free target of its colour in capture order, the animation arcs smoothly to and from it, and stepping back in replay returns the pieces to the board; a takeback after a capture empties the last place. A promoted pawn that's captured stands in its place as a pawn. A game from a FEN missing pieces starts with them in the first places in the fixed order, the game's captures after them; a FEN with an extra queen and a pawn short shows no pawn captured, and that queen captured stays a queen. Free moves in replay add the pieces they take after the places of the ply they started from. With fewer targets than captured pieces (some targets deleted), the pieces beyond the last target are hidden and come back when a takeback or replay step frees a place for them. A late-joining client shows the same pieces in the same places (in production).
  - **More captured pieces than targets:** the builder places 16 targets per colour (enough for every piece, a king taken by a free move included). A world creator may place fewer; captured pieces beyond the last target are hidden.
  - Files: `ChessBoardView.cs`, `ChessBoard.cs` (a getter for the capture order, if the view can't build it from the history getters it has), `Editor/ChessBoardViewBuilder.cs` (16 default targets per colour beside the board). Docs: [physical-pieces](docs/physical-pieces.md) (mapping, move animations), [customizing](docs/customizing.md) (placing the targets), [replay](docs/replay.md).
  - Setup: for each board, select **ChessBoard** (then **ChessBoard 2**) and run **Tools > Chess Board > Create Board View** (adds **Captured Places**; a later rebuild keeps them), then **Create View Test** (with **ChessBoard** selected) and **Create Archive Test**. Save the scene.
  - Decided here: the view works the order out itself (`ChessBoardView.CaptureOrder`, static, on every `Refresh`) from the board's public board, side to move and move history, so `ChessBoard` is unchanged. The places are read through their world position, so they can stand anywhere; a place's rotation isn't read. Objects with no place (a FEN's spare objects, captured pieces beyond the last target) are deactivated. Rebuilding a view keeps its places. Checked by `ViewTest` (a new frame, `RunCapturedPlaceChecks`) rather than by hand, except the late joiner. The builder's places stand on the side of the player who captures them (White's pieces to Black's right from Black's end, Black's to White's right from White's end; the user's request, the reverse of the old spare columns). The debug view's settle line ends with what stands in each colour's places (`captured: White PR, Black PPBRN`), for comparing clients. Details under [Captured-piece places](docs/physical-pieces.md#captured-piece-places-step-124).
  - First run (2026-10-14): after rebuilding both board views, the view test and the archive test, in Play Mode all 1384 `ViewTest` checks pass (157 new captured-place checks, and `CheckView` now checking the places), with all 415 `RulesTest`, 297 `SettingsTest`, 4157 `BoardTest` and 326 `ArchiveTest` checks, and no error logged. In production (uploaded; every test passing on the first client too): the user loaded `1. a4 b5 2. axb5 a6 3. bxa6 Bb7 4. axb7 Nc6 5. bxa8=Q Qxa8 *` on Siggі's client, continued it and played on to 15 plies (`captured: White PR, Black PPBRN`, the promoted pawn in White's first place as a pawn); Kötturinn then joined late and showed the same (`White PR, Black PPBRN`, all pieces in place). The places were then swapped to the capturer's side and both views rebuilt (bypassing the keep once); every test passes again (the same counts).

The king tips a quarter turn about its base (which stays over its square) towards the board's centre along the files or the ranks, whichever it stands further from it along, lifted by its mesh's bounds so it rolls over its base's edge rather than sinking into the board; along an axis, not diagonally, so the bounds give the lowest point exactly. It falls over `fallSeconds` (0.6 s) once the mating piece has landed, stands up at once when the mate is no longer shown, and does either at once when the placing doesn't animate (a reset, a load, entering or leaving replay, `animateMoves` off). The fall is part of `ObjectPlace` and `ObjectRotation`, so the debug view's placement check needs no change. Checked by `ViewTest` (a new frame, `RunFallChecks`, with timed frames for the animation) rather than by hand. Details under [Move animations](docs/physical-pieces.md#move-animations-step-95b).

The getter became `FallenKingSquare()` (a checkmate's mated king, or a resignation's loser's king, at the game's last ply). A king falls after `moveSeconds` only when a piece travels in that placing (the mating move), so a resignation's falls at once. A draw or a time forfeit lays no king down. In Play Mode all 1592 `ViewTest` checks pass (43 new: White and Black resigning, replay, a draw), with the other four tests as before and no error logged.
- [x] **12.9** Playing on untimed after a flag fall. When a side's time runs out, the game ends and is logged as before (time forfeit). Then, unless the organizers have turned it off (a new setting, a bit on `ChessArbiter` with per-board overrides, in the snapshot and the settings menu, on by default), the game continues at once as an untimed continuation game: a new Game ID with `ContinuedFrom` (the ended game's ID and its last ply), no clock, the same players and position. The continuation is logged when it ends, as any continued game; if the board is reset with no move made since the flag fell, it isn't logged.
  - **Clocks while continuing:** the board still answers the remaining-time getters, with each side's time at the end of the timed game (the flagged side's 0), and says that the clocks are from the previous game, so the UI can show `GG` in place of `0:00` for the side that lost on time.
  - Verify: a `BoardTest` game run out of time logs the timed game with `Termination "time forfeit"`, then continues: moves can be played, there's no clock, the remaining times are those at the flag fall; the continuation, ended by resignation, is logged with its new Game ID and `ContinuedFrom`; a continuation reset with no move isn't logged; with the setting off, the game ends as before. In production (several clients), every client shows the continuation and `GG`.
  - To decide here: whether a drawn time forfeit (the opponent can't mate) continues too; how a takeback to before the flag fall behaves in the continuation; what the PGN's `TimeControl` and `[%clk]` say for it.
  - Files: `ChessBoard.cs` (flag fall, continuation, logging, synced fields), `ChessArbiter.cs` (the new setting), `ChessSettingsMenu.cs` and its builder (its row), `ChessBoardUI.cs` (`GG`), `Rules/ChessPgn.cs`; `Assets/Tests/Board/BoardTest.cs`, `Assets/Tests/Settings/SettingsTest.cs`. Docs: [clocks](docs/clocks.md), [game-endings](docs/game-endings.md) (Time forfeit), [game-ids](docs/game-ids.md), [replay](docs/replay.md) (continuing), [settings](docs/settings.md), [pgn](docs/pgn.md) (logging), [inactivity](docs/inactivity.md), [archive](docs/archive.md), [networking](docs/networking.md).
  - Setup: run **Tools > Chess Board > Create Settings Menu** (the new row). Save the scene.
  - Decided here: a drawn time forfeit plays on too (the side out of time may still mate), unless an automatic draw the snapshot has on applies to the position (a dead position with that rule on), where the untimed game would be over at once. A takeback in the untimed game, even back before the flag fell, stays untimed and keeps the timed game's times. The untimed game's PGN has `TimeControl "-"` and no `[%clk]`: the clock history is emptied on playing on (the timed game's PGN has the clocks). A game played on with no move since the flag fell isn't logged however it ends (reset, resignation, takeback), not only on a reset. The flag fall and the playing on are one change, told as `ChangeEnded` and sent in one update; a receiver that had the timed game logs it on time from the update itself (`ContinuedFrom` naming it at its ply), not from its own clock. New synced state: `untimedAfterFlagFall` (`ClocksFromPreviousGame()`) and `flagFallPly`. The setting is `ChessArbiter.SettingPlayOnAfterFlagFall` (`playOnAfterFlagFall`, on), its menu row **Play on untimed after a flag fall** after the forced time control. The UI also says `Playing on untimed: Black ran out of time` in the status while the game plays on. `ChessPgn.cs`, `inactivity` and `archive` needed no change (the untimed game is archived with no clock like any other). Details under [Clocks](docs/clocks.md#implementation).
  - Checks: `BoardTest`'s `TestPlayOn` (setting off; on: logged on time then playing on with a new ID, `ContinuedFrom`, no clock and the times kept; moves; the untimed game's PGN and its logging on resignation; reset and resignation with no move since not logged, a reset after a move logged; drawn forfeit playing on, a dead position not; in replay; takebacks with and without a move since; a receiver logging the timed game, the round trip, a reset arriving), `SettingsTest` (the bit, its editor field, the menu row), `ViewTest` (`GG`, the status line).
  - First run (2026-10-14): after rebuilding the settings menu, in Play Mode all 4303 `BoardTest` checks pass (146 new), 303 `SettingsTest` (6 new), 1596 `ViewTest` (4 new), 425 `RulesTest` and 326 `ArchiveTest`, with no error logged. In production (2026-10-15, after uploading): Siggі alone passed every test (the same counts); then with Kötturinn on a second client, a 60 s game whose flag fell played on untimed with `GG` on both clients (the user's check), and both clients logged the timed game identically (`time forfeit`, `1-0`, with its clocks): Kötturinn's from the update that carried the play-on. Kötturinn's SettingsTest failed only its 9 organizer checks, as expected.
- [x] **12.10** One piece set per colour: redo 12.3's sets so White and Black each wear a set of their own, swapped independently, rather than one set holding both colours. For example, a world whose supporters unlock 5 piece sets lets each of two players pick one: White wears one player's choice and Black the other's.
  - **`ChessPieceSet`** becomes one side's pieces: 6 templates (`pawn`, `knight`, `bishop`, `rook`, `queen`, `king`, read by `Template(type)`), each with its mesh, materials (all slots) and local transform as in 12.3. A set has no colour of its own: any set can be worn by either side. Templates are set up as White's pieces (facing Black, +z), and the view turns Black's pieces half round, so a set needs no separate setup per side. Light and dark variants of a design (e.g. a supporter set's two colours) are two sets. `Problem()` names a missing template, mesh or renderer as before.
  - **Where a side's set comes from:** the view's own `whitePieceSet` / `blackPieceSet` if set, otherwise the arbiter's `defaultWhitePieceSet` / `defaultBlackPieceSet`, each side on its own (a view can set one side and follow the arbiter's default for the other). Run-time changes stay local (not synced): `ChessBoardView.SetChessPieces(color, set)` (`null` goes back to the arbiter's default for that side) and `ChessArbiter.SetDefaultChessPieces(color, set)` (refusing `null`); `CurrentPieceSet(color)` and `AppliedPieceSet(color)` read them back. Changing one side's set changes only that side's pieces, in place. The arbiter tells its listeners (`OnDefaultPieceSetChanged`) as in 12.3. The build check cancels a build while either of an arbiter's defaults is empty.
  - **Everything of a side follows its set:** its pieces on the board, in hand, travelling and on the captured-piece places (12.4), a promoted piece (taking its own side's template for the new type), and the falling king (12.8).
  - **Players' choices:** the sets stay local, so a world that lets each player pick a set syncs the choice in its own script and calls `SetChessPieces(color, set)` on every client (for the side each player sits on); the docs show how, with the board's player getters and a `ChessBoardListener` to notice who sits where.
  - **Default sets:** **Create Board View** gives the arbiter two sets, **White Pieces** and **Black Pieces** (6 slots each with its guide, from the generated meshes and the White Pieces / Black Pieces materials), replacing the combined **Chess Pieces** (removed when the builder finds it, with the arbiter's field cleared).
  - Verify: in Play Mode, the default sets look as before; giving one side a set (on the view, and as the arbiter's default) changes only that side's pieces, in place, including captured pieces, a promoted piece and a fallen king; one set worn by both sides at once stands right on both (Black's turned half round); `SetChessPieces(color, null)` goes back to the arbiter's default for that side alone; `SetDefaultChessPieces(color, null)` is refused; either default empty cancels a build. `ViewTest`'s piece set checks and the build check's editor test updated to match.

The view turns Black's template offset and rotation half round when it copies them (`ApplyPieceSet(color)`), so the base point is unchanged. `OnDefaultPieceSetChanged(color)` tells the views which side changed; the arbiter's `DefaultPieceSet(color)` reads a default, and `SetDefaultChessPieces` also refuses a colour other than White or Black. The view's `pieceSet` became `whitePieceSet` and `blackPieceSet`. The test set is one set (White's test materials) put on either side or both, rather than a pair. The fallen king is checked through a resignation in the piece set frame, since the fall checks run later. Checked by `ViewTest` and the build check's editor test rather than by hand (the default sets "looking as before" is the bounds check: every piece stands as it did). There's no cancel: dropping the pawn on the last rank commits to promoting. The promotion picker canvas and its Cancel button are removed. A change to the board that ends the wait (as today) still clears it.

Since each side wears its own set, White's and Black's ghosts can have different meshes and materials. `Problem()` names a missing promotion template too. The ghosts take the promoting side's templates' meshes, materials and transforms (Black's turned half round as its pieces are), and follow a change of that side's set at once (as the pieces do).

The ghosts get trigger colliders fitted to their meshes, refitted with the mesh (`DressGhost`). - [x] **12.12** Sound effects: UI button clicked; piece moved (not capturing); piece captured; piece promoted; check; game started (Start or Continue from here); draw offered; takeback requested; game over; game reset; time warning (one sound, at 1:00, 0:30 and 0:10 left on the side to move's clock); time ran out. The user's clips are in `Sounds/`, assigned in the Inspector (the builders assign them by default): `ui-click`, `move`, `capture`, `promote`, `move-check`, `game-start`, `notification-pop` (both draw offered and takeback requested), `game-end`, `table-reset`, `time-warning`, `time-out`.
  - **Who plays what:** `ChessBoardView` plays only the move sounds (moved, captured, promoted, check), from a spatial `AudioSource` on the table. `ChessBoardUI` plays every other sound, from its panels, so a world replacing the UI with its own removes them all with it and has nothing to hunt down in the view.
  - Played on every client, for changes as they happen: not for a late joiner catching up, a load or a reset rebuilding the board (only the reset sound for a reset), nor a test's thousands of changes. Time ran out plays in place of game over for a flag fall. The time warning is heard by everyone near the board (spatial, like the rest).
  - **One sound per change:** a change plays a single sound, the most specific one: a move or capture that checks plays only the check sound, a promotion only the promotion sound (a promotion that checks: check). The one exception is checkmate: the view plays the check sound, and the UI plays the game-over sound once the check sound has finished (the UI waits the check clip's length; it gets that clip too, for its length only, so it still doesn't reference the view).
  - **Replay:** stepping (either way, and jumping to the start or the end) plays the sound the new position would play if its last move had just been made: the move, capture, promotion or check sound, and for a checkmate the check sound then game over. The starting position (no last move) plays nothing. Free moves in replay are moves and play their sounds like any other.
  - **Promotion:** the promoting player hears the move or capture sound when the pawn lands on the last rank (committed from then on, 12.11), then the promotion sound when they choose the piece. Other clients get the whole promotion as one change, so they hear only the promotion sound.
  - Verify: in Play Mode, each event plays its sound once (the time warning at each of 1:00, 0:30 and 0:10 on a short clock); a capture that checks plays only the check sound; checkmate plays the check sound and then, after it, game over; replay steps play the shown position's sound and the start plays nothing; a promotion plays the move or capture sound on landing and the promotion sound on choosing; a late joiner hears nothing on joining, and the other client hears only the promotion sound for a promotion (in production); with the UI removed, the view still plays the move sounds and nothing else.
  - Files: `ChessBoardView.cs`, `ChessBoardUI.cs`, their builders, the clips in `Sounds/`. Docs: [physical-pieces](docs/physical-pieces.md), [player-input](docs/player-input.md) (buttons), [clocks](docs/clocks.md).
  - Setup: run **Tools > Chess Board > Create Board View** for each board (adding **Sounds** to the view and the UI, with the clips), then **Create View Test**. Save the scene.
  - Decided here: each behaviour notes a change in `OnBoardChanged` and plays its sound on the `Refresh` after it, only when it was the only change since the last `Refresh` (a received update is one change; a test's thousands, or a burst like join-join-start, play nothing), never in the first second after it starts (`StartupSilence`, the board's own start-up reset) and never for a late joiner's first update (the first received change when nothing was seen before it). Game over waits the check clip's length after any move that ends the game (stalemate and the automatic draws too, as the view plays that move's sound), and a replay step onto such an end does the same; a resignation, agreement, claim or flag fall at the last ply doesn't. A free move counts as a capture when the position has fewer pieces than before. The time warning samples the side to move's clock on every status update and warns as it passes a mark of `timeWarningSeconds` (Inspector, 60, 30, 10) within one turn. The click plays for every press, refused or not, on the presser's client only, but not for the replay step buttons, whose step plays its move sound alone (decided on hearing it). Each behaviour has one spatial `AudioSource` on a **Sounds** child at the board's centre (the UI's rather than one per panel). `muted` (for tests) stops the playing while `lastSound` and `soundsPlayed` still record it. Details under [Sound effects](docs/physical-pieces.md#sound-effects-step-1212) and the UI's [Sound effects](docs/player-input.md#sound-effects-step-1212).
  - Checks (`ViewTest`, two frames after the fall checks): every Verify item that the test can read from `lastSound`/`soundsPlayed` (see [Testing](docs/testing.md#view-test)). By hand: hearing them, and with the UI removed the view's move sounds alone. In production: a late joiner hearing nothing on joining, and the other client hearing only the promotion sound for a promotion.
  - First run (2026-10-20): after rebuilding both board views (each view and UI with **Sounds** and the clips) and the view test, in Play Mode all 1832 `ViewTest` checks pass (the sound checks included), with all 425 `RulesTest`, 303 `SettingsTest`, 4303 `BoardTest`, 326 `ArchiveTest` and 449 `Board2DTest` checks, and no error logged.
  - Second run (2026-10-20, after the step buttons stopped clicking): all 1846 `ViewTest` checks pass with the other five suites and no error logged. Heard by hand in Play Mode: every sound once at its event, the time warning at 1:00, 0:30 and 0:10 on a 3-minute clock and the time-out at 0:00, the fool's mate's check then game over, the view's move sounds alone with the UI disabled. In production (Siggі first, Kötturinn joining a game in progress): every test passing on Siggі's client (Kötturinn's 9 organizer `SettingsTest` failures and skipped board tests as expected), the late joiner hearing nothing on joining, and a promotion played right on both clients (each muted in turn).
- [x] **12.13** 2D chess board view: `ChessBoard2DView`, a flat (UI canvas) board showing one board's position at a time, with left / right buttons to choose which board. It shows what the board shows (replay included), which board it is (`Board N`, numbered from 1 as the settings menu does) and its players and status lines as the status display writes them (`GG` after a flag fall included, 12.9). Nothing on it acts on a board: the selector is its only input. The pieces wear the user's 2D sprites (`Sprites2D/`; placeholders were planned until they came).
  - **Boards:** a `boards` array set in the Inspector (as the settings menu's), in that order. It listens to the board it shows (`AddListener` / `RemoveListener` on switching), or polls; decided here.
  - **Synced** (Inspector bool): when on, the chosen board is a synced field and a press takes ownership, so everyone sees the same board, late joiners included; when off, each player chooses for themselves.
  - **Stepping:** left and right step to the previous or next board that isn't reset (`LifecycleReset`), wrapping round (right from the last board goes to the first, left from the first to the last), skipping reset ones. The board shown isn't left when it's reset. With no board out of the reset state, both buttons do nothing. It starts on the first board in `boards`.
  - Verify (several clients for the synced case): with boards 1 to 5 and only 2 and 4 in use, right from 2 goes to 4 and from 4 to 2; resetting 4 while shown keeps 4 shown; with all reset, the buttons do nothing; a board in use shows its moves as they're played, and its replay. Synced: a press on one client changes every client's view, and a late joiner sees the chosen board. Unsynced: each client chooses its own.
  - Files: new `ChessBoard2DView.cs`, a builder for it (`Editor/ChessBoard2DViewBuilder.cs`), the sprites in `Sprites2D/`; `ChessBoardUI.cs` (static status texts; `ChessBoard.cs` needed no getters); `Board2DTest` and its builder; the debug view and its builder (the sprites); `ArchiveTest.cs` (`finished`). Docs: new `docs/board-2d.md` (added to the design documents table), [architecture](docs/architecture.md) (listeners), [networking](docs/networking.md) (the synced choice), [ownership](docs/ownership.md).
  - Decided here: the view listens to the board it shows (moving its listener as it steps) rather than polling; other boards' lifecycles are read only on a press. Stepping is a static `StepTarget(inUse, current, direction)`, so the rule is checked with five boards without five in the scene. The texts come from static forms of the UI's status methods taking the board (`ChessBoardUI.PlayersTextFor`, `StatusTextFor` and the rest; the instance methods forward to them), so `ChessBoard.cs` needed no new getter. The pieces wear the user's sprites (`Sprites2D/`, `PawnW.png` … `KingB.png`) from the start instead of placeholders; the builder sets their import type to Sprite. The debug view uses the same sprites in place of its FEN letters (the user's request). A new view stands behind the boards (+z), facing them; synced, anyone may take the view (no `OnOwnershipRequest`). Details under [2D board view](docs/board-2d.md).

Then in production (2026-10-17), the scene's 2D view turned Synced with **Tools > Chess Board > Toggle 2D View Synced** (added for this, in the test builder) and each switch logged by the view (`[ChessBoard2DView] …: showing Board N (on start / chosen here[, sent] / chosen by <name>)`): with Siggі and Kötturinn, every press on either client reached the other within the second (four switches, both ways), and moves played showed on the 2D view (the user's report); Thе Cаt joining late while Board 2 was chosen started on Board 1 and switched to `Board 2 (chosen by Kötturinn)` 12 s later, as VRChat delivered the synced field. Unsynced (toggled back and uploaded again), Siggі and Kötturinn each stepped to Board 2 on their own (`chosen here`), nothing arriving on the other. No Udon exception on any client.

- [x] **12.14** 2D view replay of its own: the 2D view (12.13) steps through the shown board's game by itself, without touching the board. A row of four buttons along the bottom, left to right `<<` (the start), `<` (one move back), `>` (one move forward), `>>` (the latest position). The buttons are always shown, and disabled (greyed, not clickable) when their action can't be done: `<<` and `<` at the start, `>` and `>>` on the latest position.
  - **Its own ply:** the view keeps the ply it shows (`viewPly`, 0 the start, `moveCount` the latest) and builds that position itself from the board's game (`moveHistory` and `moveCount`, undoing moves on a local scratch position as `StepTo` does, through the static rules), not from `PieceAt`. Its tints and texts follow the position it shows: the last move into it, a king in check in it, and the replay line naming the move when it isn't the latest (12.13's "shows what the board shows" changes to this).
  - **Following the game:** on the latest position when a move is made, it moves on to the new latest position; anywhere earlier, it stays where it is. A takeback or a continuation that cuts the history below the ply shown brings it to the new latest position; a reset or a load also goes to the latest.
  - **Independent of the board's replay:** the board entering, stepping, free-moving in or leaving replay leaves the 2D view as it was, and it can still step through the whole game while the board is in replay (a custom replay position isn't shown: the view shows the game's positions only).
  - **Switching board** (the selector, or a synced switch arriving) starts on the new board's latest position.
  - **Locked to one board:** a new Inspector field `lockedBoard` (a `ChessBoard`; empty for the selector as in 12.13). When set, the view shows only that board, its left / right selector buttons are disabled and hidden (`Board N` still shown), and stepping between boards is refused, for a world that puts a fixed 2D view beside each table. The builder can make a view locked to the selected board.
  - **Synced:** with **Synced** on, the ply shown is synced along with the chosen board (one shared view, late joiners included); off, each player steps by themselves.
  - Verify: in Play Mode, each button goes where it should and is disabled exactly at the ends; on the latest position a new move moves the view on, one move back it stays; the board entering replay and stepping leaves the view where it was, and the view steps through the game while the board is in replay; a takeback below the ply shown brings it to the new latest; switching board starts on the latest; a locked view hides and disables its selector and shows only its board. Synced (several clients): a step on one client moves every client's view, and a late joiner sees the ply chosen.
  - To decide here: whether the scratch position is rebuilt from the latest each step (undoing back, as `StepTo`) or kept and moved a move at a time, and whether a long game is fast enough in one frame; whether a locked view still uses its `boards` array.
  - Files: `ChessBoard2DView.cs`, `Editor/ChessBoard2DViewBuilder.cs`, `ChessBoardUI.cs` (`GameStatusTextFor`, `PlyTextFor`), `Board2DTest` and its builder (**Toggle 2D View Synced** acting on the selected view). Docs: [board-2d](docs/board-2d.md), [replay](docs/replay.md) (the 2D view's replay is separate), [networking](docs/networking.md) (the synced ply), [testing](docs/testing.md).
  - Decided here: the scratch position is rebuilt from the latest on every redraw (`BuildPosition`, undoing back as `StepTo` does), not kept and moved a move at a time, so nothing can leave it out of step; `<<` on a 300-ply game takes 3.6 ms in ClientSim. A locked view doesn't read its `boards` (the builder still fills it, so clearing the lock works); `SetLockedBoard` changes the lock at run time. The synced ply is `-1` for the latest, so clients following the game stay on it whatever arrives first, and the owner sends `-1` when its view goes back to the latest by itself. The status lines are the UI's without the board's replay position (`ChessBoardUI.GameStatusTextFor`), a takeback request reading `White asks for a takeback` (the UI's "to here" is the board's replay position), and the view's own replay line (`ChessBoardUI.PlyTextFor`). Following the game goes back to the latest when the history no longer reaches the ply or has another move there, on a reset or load among the changes, and when the ply is the latest again; a takeback above the ply leaves it. The step buttons are greyed by `interactable` (the button's disabled tint) and their labels' colour. The builder's locked view comes from a new menu item, **Create Locked 2D Board View** (with the board selected), 1.2 m behind its board; **Create 2D Board View** keeps a rebuilt view's lock, and **Create Another 2D Board View** adds one more (the test scene keeps a synced one beside the unsynced one the tests use, so both cases run in one build: the user's request in the several-client run). A synced ply the client's game doesn't reach beyond yet waits (`waitingPly`), the latest shown meanwhile, until the board's moves arrive: found in the several-client run, where a late joiner got the view's update before the board's and stayed on the latest. Details under [2D board view](docs/board-2d.md#its-own-replay-step-1214).
  - Setup: **Tools > Chess Board > Create 2D Board View** (rebuilding the scene's view with the step buttons), then **Create 2D View Test**, then **Create Another 2D Board View** and, with it selected, **Toggle 2D View Synced** (the synced second view). Save the scene.
  - Checks: `Board2DTest` ([Testing](docs/testing.md#2d-view-test)): the step buttons through every end, following the game, the board's replay and free moves leaving the view, takebacks above and below the ply, a reset, a synced ply arriving before the board's moves, switching board, locking, and a 300-ply game's timing.
  - First run (2026-10-18): after rebuilding the 2D view and its test, in Play Mode all 320 `Board2DTest` checks pass, with all 425 `RulesTest`, 303 `SettingsTest`, 4303 `BoardTest`, 1659 `ViewTest` and 326 `ArchiveTest` checks, and no error logged. Then in production (2026-10-18), with the second view `ChessBoard2DView (2)` synced and the first not: with Siggі and Kötturinn, every step and board switch on the synced view reached the other client within a second (`chosen here, sent` / `chosen by Kötturinn`), while the unsynced view's steps stayed local (`chosen here`). Thе Cаt joining late while ply 1 of Board 2 was chosen got Board 2 but stayed on the latest: its view's update arrived before Board 2's moves. Fixed (the waiting ply, with its `Board2DTest` checks: 334 pass, every other test as before, no error) and uploaded again; Thе Cаt joining late on ply 1 of 6 then logged `showing ply 1 of 6 (received earlier, the game here now reaches it)` on the frame Board 2's moves arrived. No Udon exception on any client. The late joiner's `chosen by` names the instance master (Siggі) rather than the view's owner (Kötturinn), as `Networking.GetOwner` isn't up to date there yet; only the log line is affected.
- [x] **12.15** Move list on the 2D view: to the right of the board, a move history running the full height of the view (top to bottom), so the selector row and 12.14's replay buttons end at its left edge rather than spanning the whole width. Two columns, White's moves on the left and Black's on the right, a row per move number (a game starting with Black to move, from a FEN, starts with an empty White cell).
  - **Current move:** the move that led to the position shown (12.14's ply) is highlighted yellow; none at the start.
  - **Clicking a move** jumps the view to the position after it (12.14's ply, synced as 12.14's steps are).
  - **Scrolling:** when the moves don't fit, a scrollbar; whenever the ply shown changes (the step buttons, a click, following the game on the latest position, switching board), the list scrolls just enough to keep the current move in view.
  - **Notation:** the board's SAN (`notation`), a move whose SAN isn't filled yet showing once `OnNotationFilled` brings it.
  - Verify: in Play Mode, the list shows White's and Black's moves in their columns (a game from a FEN with Black to move too); the current move is yellow and follows the step buttons and new moves; clicking a move shows its position; a game longer than the list scrolls, and stepping or a new move keeps the current move in view; the board, the buttons and the list don't overlap.
  - To decide here: one UI element per move kept in a pool (and how many) or one text per column with clicks mapped to rows; the list's width, and whether the canvas grows to make room or the board shrinks.
  - Files: `ChessBoard2DView.cs`, new `ChessMoveListCell.cs` (a cell's click), `Editor/ChessBoard2DViewBuilder.cs`, `Board2DTest`. Docs: [board-2d](docs/board-2d.md), [testing](docs/testing.md).
  - Decided here: a pool of rows, not one element per move nor one text per column: enough rows to fill the list's height and one more (28 rows of 50 pixels), drawn with the rows the scroll position puts there (`DrawMoveList`), so a game of any length needs no more objects. The list is a `ScrollRect` over an empty content rect the view sizes to every row (its `onValueChanged` calls `OnMoveListScrolled`), with the pooled rows in its viewport (`RectMask2D`) shifted by the part of a row scrolled past, and a vertical scrollbar shown only while the rows don't fit (`AutoHide`). A cell's click goes through a `ChessMoveListCell` per cell (`PressMoveCell(cell)`, as the settings menu's buttons), as UI events carry no arguments. The list is 462 pixels wide (move number 80, two cells of 170) and the canvas grows to make room (1362 by 1410 pixels, 1.36 m wide instead of 0.88 m); the board keeps its size. The current move is a yellow cell with black text. The list scrolls only when the board or ply shown changes, so a player's own scrolling stays. Real pointer drags, the wheel and clicks through VRChat's UI aren't covered by the test (it calls `PressMoveCell` and `ScrollMoveList`); they're left to a look in the next client run. - [x] **12.16** Moving and resizing the 2D view: a player can move the view (grab it and put it anywhere) and resize it (e.g. a handle at a corner, keeping its aspect), when the world creator allows it: a new Inspector bool (`movable`, on the holder below, on by default; a view without a holder doesn't move, so existing views stay where they were placed). With **Synced** on, the position, rotation and size are synced to every client (late joiners included); with it off, each player's moves stay local.
  - **Moving with `VRCPickup`:** the view is moved by a `VRCPickup` (on a handle, or on the object the canvas hangs from), and with **Synced** on its position and rotation are synced by `VRCObjectSync` on that object, so other players see it move smoothly while it's held (picking it up takes ownership, as any pickup). With **Synced** off there's no `VRCObjectSync` and the move stays local. `VRCObjectSync` forces continuous sync on behaviours sharing its object, so the pickup is on an object of its own and the view's manual-synced behaviour (its board and ply) stays elsewhere. The moving parts (the pickup, `VRCObjectSync`, the resize handle and whatever behaviour they need) live on a holder object around the view, which the view itself knows nothing of: a view without the holder is a fixed view as in 12.13 to 12.15 and needs nothing new, and 14.8 makes the two into two prefabs. The builder builds a view with or without the holder (a separate menu item or an option, decided here). With `movable` off, the pickup is disabled (`pickupable` false).
  - **Resizing:** `VRCObjectSync` doesn't sync scale, so the size is a synced field of its own (with **Synced** on), set by whoever resizes it.
  - Verify: in Play Mode (desktop and VR), with `movable` on, the view can be moved and resized, and its buttons and move list still work after; with it off, nothing can be grabbed. Synced (several clients): a move on one client is seen moving smoothly on the others while held, a resize shows on every client, and a late joiner sees it where and how large it was left; unsynced, it moves only on the client that moved it.
  - To decide here: where the pickup is (a handle bar along an edge, or the frame itself) and how it's resized (a second pickup at a corner scaling the canvas, between a minimum and a maximum size, or buttons); whether it's kept upright (gravity off and kinematic, so it stays where it's let go); how **Synced** adds or leaves out the `VRCObjectSync` (the builder, from the Inspector tick); whether anything puts it back where it was placed.
  - Files: `ChessBoard2DView.cs` (unchanged in the end), new `ChessBoard2DViewHolder.cs`, `Editor/ChessBoard2DViewBuilder.cs` (the pickup, `VRCObjectSync`), new `Editor/ChessBoard2DViewSyncCheck.cs`, `Editor/ChessBoardViewBuilder.cs` (`MaterialAsset` public), `Board2DTest` and its builder. Docs: [board-2d](docs/board-2d.md), [networking](docs/networking.md) (the synced transform and size), [ownership](docs/ownership.md) (the pickup's owner), [customizing](docs/customizing.md) (allowing it), [architecture](docs/architecture.md), [testing](docs/testing.md).
  - Decided here: a new behaviour, `ChessBoard2DViewHolder`, on the holder object, with the view, a **Grip** and a **Corner** as its children. The pickup is a bar of its own under the view (centred under its bottom edge, 0.5 m wide at most and narrowed to stay clear of the corner at the smallest size: 0.3 m), not the canvas (a pickup's colliders would take the buttons' and the move list's clicks, a desktop click grabbing the view), and the view isn't its child: the holder places the view in `PostLateUpdate` whenever the grip moved or the size changed, from the place it had from the grip on `Start`. Resizing is a second pickup, an 8 cm cube just outside the view's bottom right, sizing it by how far along the view's bottom edge it's held, about the middle of that edge (which keeps its place above the bar), 0.25 to 3 times the scene size (`minSize`, `maxSize`); its size is sent at most every 0.2 s while held and once on letting go, eased to on the others. A VR player's hand turns and tilts the view freely; a synced upright flag, set by picking up the grip (on for a desktop player, off for VR), stands the view and its bar upright, turning only with the grip's heading (turning the grip itself, tried first, was undone by VRChat on letting go and never reached the others); the grip is kinematic without gravity, so it stays where it's let go. In VR the bar and corner grab only from within 0.05 m (`vrReach`, the pickups' `proximity`); desktop keeps the pickups' own reach. The pickups are read each frame (`IsHeld`, `currentPlayer`), so neither has a behaviour (one on the grip would be forced to continuous sync). `movable` off also hides the bar and corner. The builder adds the grip's `VRCObjectSync` when the view is synced, and a build callback (`ChessBoard2DViewSyncCheck`) adds or removes it before every build to match **Synced** (so ticking it needs no rebuild); **Toggle 2D View Synced** matches it too. A separate menu item, **Create Movable 2D Board View**, not an option. `PutBack()` puts it back as placed, but nothing calls it by itself and no button is built (a world's own button can). Details under [Moving and resizing](docs/board-2d.md#moving-and-resizing-step-1216).
  - Setup: **Tools > Chess Board > Create Movable 2D Board View** with `ChessBoard2DView` selected (the first run creates `ChessBoard2DViewHolder.asset`; run it again once UdonSharp has compiled), again with `ChessBoard2DView (2)` selected (synced, so its grip gets a `VRCObjectSync`), then **Create 2D View Test** with `ChessBoard2DView Holder/ChessBoard2DView` selected. Save the scene.
  - Checks: `Board2DTest` ([Testing](docs/testing.md#2d-view-test)): `SizeFor` and `Upright` on their own, then the holder's wiring (`minSize` 0.25, the desktop reach, its `VRCObjectSync` exactly when synced, none on the view or holder), following a moved, turned and tilted grip the upright flag on and off, sizes about the bottom edge and their limits, `movable` off and on, drawing after a move, and `PutBack`. Grabbing and dragging through VRChat can't be scripted: left to the client run.

Uploaded and tried (Siggі in VR, Kötturinn on desktop, the user's report): a desktop pickup stood the view and bar upright for both, after letting go too, and a VR pickup tilted them again; Thе Cаt joining late got the synced view small (`resized to 0.25 by Siggі`) and upright, as left. Nothing a changed helper builds differs. The rebuilt scene is kept. One definition of each copy is left.

### 13. Last changes before the prefabs

Changes to how the board plays and reads, made before the prefabs (14) so they're built in from the start: leaving a seat before a game starts, the player who loads a game keeping the board until they let others in, "Continue from here" for one player in both seats, putting captured pieces back in replay, the UI's text in a language file, synced piece sets chosen from a list on the arbiter, marking the public methods that aren't public API (13.7), and a portrait history wall that scrolls, newest game first (13.8), with one step per mouse-wheel turn there and in the 2D view's move list (13.9), a personal history wall kept in the player's own persistent data beside the instance's, with a PGN export on both walls, the archives and the picked game on the arbiter, and changes told instead of polled (13.10), and the PGN Event and Site tags on the arbiter, with the board count first in its inspector (13.11). Each changes the logical board, the view or the UI (and their builders where they build something new), and is tested by the existing test behaviours as in steps 7 to 12.

**Read:** [board-lifecycle](docs/board-lifecycle.md) (seats, reset), [replay](docs/replay.md), [ownership](docs/ownership.md), [networking](docs/networking.md) (synced fields), [inactivity](docs/inactivity.md), [player-input](docs/player-input.md) (buttons), [physical-pieces](docs/physical-pieces.md) (captured-piece places, who may touch the pieces, piece sets), [settings](docs/settings.md) (the arbiter's piece sets), [customizing](docs/customizing.md) (chess piece sets), [archive](docs/archive.md) (history wall, the archive's reading methods), [pgn](docs/pgn.md) (export), [board-2d](docs/board-2d.md) (the move list), [architecture](docs/architecture.md), [udon-constraints](docs/udon-constraints.md), [testing](docs/testing.md), and each substep's files below.
