- [x] **13.2** The player who loads a game keeps the board. When a game is loaded ("Load it here" on the history wall, or Import after pasting a PGN), the player who loaded it takes ownership of the board and is stored in a new synced string, `loadedBy`, separate from the seats. While `loadedBy` holds no seat, the board is locked to them: only they (and organizers, as everywhere) may act on it (step, make free moves, join, choose the clock, reset, touch the pieces), and everyone else may at most leave a seat they hold. Once `loadedBy` joins a seat, anyone may join the other one, as on a loaded board today.
  - **`loadedBy` stays** when its player joins or leaves a seat: if they join, someone joins the other colour and they then leave (13.1), the lock is back and the board is still theirs, not the other player's. It's cleared only when a game actually starts ("Continue from here"; the game then belongs to its players) or the board is reset; emptying both seats (13.1) keeps it.
  - **Leaving the instance doesn't free the board:** with `loadedBy` gone, nobody else takes it over (today's "anyone while a seat is empty, or a seated player has left" doesn't apply while it's locked). The board's inactivity timers apply as in any other state: once it has been idle for the reset-available time anyone may reset it, and the auto-reset clears it.
  - **Ownership and checks:** `MayOwnAs` lets `loadedBy` own the board while it's loaded and, while it's locked, refuses everyone else but organizers and the idle-reset case. The load methods (`LoadImport`, `LoadArchivedGame`) take the loader's name (new `…As(name, …)` methods, the existing ones passing the local player, as the other inputs do). `MayControlReplay`, `CanJoinAs`, `CanSetClockAs`, `CanResetAs`, `MayTouchPiecesAs` and the free-move checks consult the lock.
  - Verify: `BoardTest`: a load records the loader; while it's locked, everyone else is refused every action (step, free move, join, clock, reset, touching pieces) and the loader and organizers are allowed; after the loader joins, others may join; the loader leaving their seat brings the lock back; both seats emptied (13.1) keeps `loadedBy`; a reset and "Continue from here" clear `loadedBy`; anyone may reset after the reset-available time; `MayOwnAs` agrees. `ArchiveTest` still loads games. Several clients (the debug view, as in 8.8): A loads a game and B can neither join nor step; A joins White and B joins Black; another load by A, who then leaves the instance: B can't take the board until the reset-available time, then can reset it.
  - Files: `ChessBoard.cs` (the field and its sync packing, the checks, `MayOwnAs`, the load methods), `ChessBoardUI.cs` (Import and "Load it here" pass the local player), `ChessPgnImporter.cs` and `GameHistoryWall.cs` if they call the load, `Assets/Tests/Debug/ChessDebugView.cs` (shows `loadedBy`), `Assets/Tests/Board/BoardTest.cs`, `Assets/Tests/Archive/ArchiveTest.cs`. Docs: [replay](docs/replay.md) (Continuing a loaded game), [board-lifecycle](docs/board-lifecycle.md), [ownership](docs/ownership.md), [networking](docs/networking.md) (synced fields), [inactivity](docs/inactivity.md), [archive](docs/archive.md) (loading games), [pgn](docs/pgn.md) (import).
  - Decided while implementing: organizers act on a locked board as on any other (replay, free moves, pieces, reset, ownership), but don't join or choose the clock for the loader: joining never counted organizers, and `CanJoinAs`/`CanSetClockAs` keep their signatures. A load with no name (`LoadGameAs("", …)`, also what the local-player forms do with no local player) records nobody and locks nothing; `BoardTest`'s loads that aren't about the lock use it, so their checks by other names still apply.
  - Done (2026-10-22): `loadedBy` (synced as `syncedLoadedBy`), `IsLockedToLoader()`, `IsLoader(name)`, `LoadGameAs`/`LoadImportAs`/`LoadArchivedGameAs` (the old forms pass the local player, so `ChessBoardUI`'s Import and "Load it here" needed no change, nor the importer or the wall), and the lock in `MayOwnAs`, `MayControlReplay`, `CanJoinAs`, `CanSetClockAs` and `CanResetAs`; cleared by `ClearState` and `ContinueGameAs`. `BoardTest.TestLoadedBy` and two `ArchiveTest` checks; the debug view shows "loaded by" (and "locked"). All six Play Mode suites pass (BoardTest 4439, ArchiveTest 330).
  - Several clients (2026-10-22, Siggі an organizer, Kötturinn not; reset-available 120 s): Siggі's **Import PGN** loaded Siggi-vs-Nuka as `loaded by Siggі (locked)` on both clients; Kötturinn's **Join White**, **Join Black** and **Step** were refused (`may touch no`). Siggі joined White, then Kötturinn's **Join Black** was accepted (`loaded by Siggі`, no longer locked). A second import onto that board was refused, as loads only go onto a reset board; Kötturinn, seated, reset it with the world's button while Siggі was away, and Siggі, rejoining, received the reset board. Siggі loaded again (locked) and left: VRChat handed the board to Kötturinn, the only one left (as when any owner leaves, docs/ownership.md), but Kötturinn's **Reset** 24 s after the load was refused, and a reset 123 s after the load was accepted. No Udon errors. The debug view's lines don't name their board, and the scene has a debug view for each board: tell them apart by the state they show.

- [x] **13.4** Putting captured pieces back in replay. In replay, whoever may make free moves may pick up a piece standing in a captured place and drop it on a square (or, on desktop, select it and click a square), putting that piece on the replay board as a free move: the position becomes custom, and a piece already on that square is taken off. Dropped anywhere else, it goes back to its place.
  - **Board:** a new input, `PlaceCaptured(color, type, square)` / `PlaceCapturedAs(name, organizer, color, type, square)`, accepted only in replay, from someone who may make free moves (`MayTouchPiecesAs`), for a type the shown position has in that colour's captured places; it puts the piece on the replay board, marked custom.
  - **Places:** `CaptureOrder` for a custom position takes a piece put back out of its colour's places (the last place of that type), so it doesn't stand in both. A promoted pawn goes back as a pawn, as its place shows it.
  - **View:** captured pieces become pickups in replay for those players (`UpdatePickupable`), their pickups and drops forwarded with the place instead of a square, and snapped back on rejection as board pieces are.
  - Verify: `BoardTest`: each type put back, onto an empty square and onto an occupied one; refused outside replay, from someone who may not touch the pieces, and for a type not captured; the position is custom, and stepping to a game ply shows the game's again; `CaptureOrder` with pieces put back. `ViewTest`: a captured piece is a pickup only in replay; dropped on a square, it stands there and leaves its place; dropped off the board, it goes back; desktop select-and-place.
  - Files: `ChessBoard.cs`, `ChessBoardView.cs` (`CaptureOrder`, `AssignPlaces`, `UpdatePickupable`, forwarding, desktop mode), `ChessPiece.cs`, `Assets/Tests/Board/BoardTest.cs`, `Assets/Tests/View/ViewTest.cs`. Docs: [replay](docs/replay.md) (Free manipulation, Captured pieces), [physical-pieces](docs/physical-pieces.md) (Captured-piece places, Who may touch the pieces), [player-input](docs/player-input.md).
  - Decided while implementing: `CaptureOrder` moved from the view into a new static rules class, `Rules/ChessCaptures.cs` (with `PlaceOrderType`, `StandardCount`, `HasCaptured`, `MaxCaptured`), so the board checks a put-back against the same places the view shows; the board's `CapturedPieces(captured, counts)` runs it for the position shown and the view reads it from there. Besides `PlaceCaptured…`, the board has `CanPlaceCaptured…` (the check, for the view's pickups and clicks) and `CapturedPickedUp…` (the pickup, a touch, rejection told as for any piece), as board pieces have `CanPickUp` and `PiecePickedUp`. The removal from the places is in `CaptureOrder`'s custom part: a type the custom position has more of than the ply's is taken out of the last place of that type. The view maps an accepted drop's object onto its square at once, since the place that empties needn't be its own. `ChessPiece.cs` needed no change (it already forwards pickups, drops and clicks by index).

All six Play Mode suites pass (BoardTest 4617, ViewTest 1937), no error logged.

Log lines and test output stay in code too.

    - *Hover texts of the physical board:* `ChessBoardView` sets "Select", "Put back" and "Place" on pieces and square targets; the builder gives the pieces "Move"; `ChessPromotionGhost` sets "Promote to " + the piece. The view sets these (the ghosts' too) from its `i18n` at run time, so nothing the builder bakes in is shown.
    - *The movable 2D view's handles:* the grip's and resize handle's texts, baked in by its builder today; `ChessBoard2DViewHolder` sets them from its `i18n` at run time.
    - *Import errors, written by the logical board:* `ChessBoard` keeps `importMessage` as an English sentence ("failed at move N: " + `ChessPgn.ImportErrorText`, "the game couldn't be loaded", "the board changed before the import finished"). The board keeps no player text (it never touches the world): it stores the failure as codes instead (the importer's result and the move it failed at, beside `importStatus`), and `ChessBoardUI` writes the message from those through its keys. `ChessPgn.ImportErrorText` stays, for logs and `PgnTest`.
    - *How a game ended:* `ChessGame.EndingDescription` ("White wins by checkmate", "Draw by stalemate", "Unfinished", …), shown by the UI's status and the history wall, is replaced by a key per ending and winner (a static helper returning the key, beside the ending constants), which each of them looks up. The rules keep no player text.
  - Not player text, left as they are: `ChessArbiter`'s settings summary (logs), `ChessBoardView.InputStyleName` (the debug view only), Inspector tooltips, and the debug view and tests.
  - **One shared `I18n`:** the whole chess system uses one `I18n` of its own, on its own object (**Chess Language**, with `en.json`, not main), never a world's; the builders create it once per scene (reusing it when it's there) as a sibling of the chess objects they build, or on a parent of them. In 14.10 it becomes a prefab. Only the top-level behaviours hold an `I18n i18n` field (`ChessBoardUI`, `ChessBoardView`, `ChessBoard2DView`, `ChessBoard2DViewHolder`, `GameHistoryWall`, `ChessSettingsMenu`) and set their children's texts (rows, settings buttons, move-list cells, promotion ghosts), so few objects need one. The package's `I18nSanityChecks` fills the fields on build and before Play Mode with the `I18n` closest to each behaviour (one on a sibling, else on a sibling of a parent, up to the scene's roots), so with Chess Language beside the chess objects or on their parent it picks ours, with no `I18nLock` and no wiring of our own. A world `I18n` just as close (a sibling at the same level) is ambiguous, and the sanity check blocks the build saying so; the world then moves one of them or groups the chess objects under a parent. Each registers with `AddLanguageChangeListener` and redraws on `OnLanguageChange`. The player's VRChat language picks the pack, and a world's main `I18n` switching language (`SetLanguageGlobally`) switches ours too. A world adds a language by adding a pack to our `I18n`, and a pack with a code already loaded overrides only the strings it has (`I18n` merges them), so translations need no code change.
  - **Cost:** `_()` is a call into another behaviour, and the buttons are relabelled once a second, so each behaviour keeps the strings it uses in local fields, read again on `OnLanguageChange`, and fills in the changing parts (clocks, names) locally rather than calling `_()` on every redraw.

- [x] **13.6** Synced piece sets from a list on the arbiter. The arbiter holds every piece set the world offers in one array, each board syncs which of them White and Black wear (an index per colour), a player's preferred set is put on the board when they join, and each client can force its own choice onto every board. This replaces 12.10's per-view `whitePieceSet` / `blackPieceSet` objects and the arbiter's two defaults, so a world that lets players pick a set no longer syncs the choice in its own script. **No UI of ours changes a board's sets:** a world creator who wants players to choose gives them their own UI, which sets the local player's preferred set (or calls the board's methods below).
  - **A set holds both colours again** (changed from 12.10's one side per set): `ChessPieceSet` gets White's templates (`whitePawn` … `whiteKing`, and the promotion templates `whitePromotionKnight` … `whitePromotionQueen`) and Black's (`blackPawn` … , `blackPromotion…`), each with its mesh, materials and local transform as now, read by `Template(color, type)` and `PromotionTemplate(color, type)`. Templates are set up facing the other side: White's facing +z, Black's facing −z, so Black's are no longer turned by the view. **Black's fallback:** a Black template with no mesh (the template missing, or its `MeshFilter` empty) takes White's mesh and transform for that type, turned half round about the vertical through the square's centre (as the view turns Black's today, so the base point stays the same), and Black's materials when its template has a renderer with materials, otherwise White's. So a set whose two sides differ only in colour gives Black only materials, and a set with one look for both sides gives Black nothing. White's templates are all required; `Problem()` names a missing White template, mesh or renderer.
  - **The arbiter's list:** `ChessArbiter.pieceSets` (`ChessPieceSet[]`), index 0 the default set; `PieceSet(index)` reads one (`null` out of range), `PieceSetCount()`. It replaces `defaultWhitePieceSet` / `defaultBlackPieceSet`, `SetDefaultChessPieces` and `DefaultPieceSet` (no release yet, so no migration, [compatibility](docs/compatibility.md) applying from 16.1). The build check cancels a build while `pieceSets` is empty or any element of it is `null`.
  - **Synced on the logical board:** `ChessBoard` gets two synced ints, `whitePieceSet` and `blackPieceSet` (indexes into the arbiter's `pieceSets`, both 0 by default), in its sync packing and under its ownership as every other synced field; the view holds no synced fields, as the [architecture](docs/architecture.md) says. Set by `SetPieceSet(color, index)` / `SetPieceSetAs(name, organizer, color, index)`, checked by `CanSetPieceSetAs(…)`: refused for an index outside the arbiter's list or a colour other than White or Black. Read by `PieceSetIndex(color)`. Listeners are told by a new change constant (`ChangePieceSet`), on which the view applies that side's set. A reset sets both back to 0. A synced index this client's list doesn't have (it shouldn't happen, every client running the same world) falls back to 0, logged once.
  - **The local player's preferred set:** an unsynced int on the arbiter, `preferredPieceSet` (`-1`, the default, for none), public so a world's own behaviour can read and write it (also through `SetPreferredPieceSet(index)`, refusing an index outside the list other than `-1`, and `PreferredPieceSet()`). Joining applies it: joining a board with both seats empty sets both colours' indexes to it, and joining a board where someone already holds the other seat sets only the joined colour's (a player taking their second seat sets that seat's colour, as it's already theirs). With `-1` a join leaves the sets as they are. The preference lives on the joiner's client, so the local forms (`Join(color)`, `JoinWhite()`, `JoinBlack()`) pass the arbiter's `preferredPieceSet` to a new `JoinAs(color, name, preferredSet)`, and the existing `JoinAs(color, name)` forwards with `-1`. The sets change in the same sync as the join, whatever the rule for changing sets otherwise, and an index outside this client's list is ignored (treated as `-1`).
  - **Local override on the arbiter:** two unsynced ints, `overrideWhitePieceSet` and `overrideBlackPieceSet` (`-1`, the default, for none), set by `SetPieceSetOverride(color, index)` (refusing an index outside the list other than `-1`) and read by `PieceSetOverride(color)`. While a colour's override is set, every board on this client shows that set for that colour whatever the board has synced; clearing it shows the board's own again. The arbiter tells its piece set listeners (`OnPieceSetOverrideChanged(color)`, replacing `OnDefaultPieceSetChanged`), as it tells them of a default change today. E.g. a player who finds a set hard to read sets the default set on every board for themselves.
  - **Which set a side wears:** the arbiter's override for that colour if set, else the board's synced index for it, else 0; `CurrentPieceSet(color)` on the view returns that set and `AppliedPieceSet(color)` the set applied now, as before. `SetChessPieces` on the view goes, with its `whitePieceSet` / `blackPieceSet` fields. Applying is as in 12.10 (only that side's pieces change, in place, everything of a side following its set), except that the view reads Black's templates through the fallback rather than turning every Black template.
  - Decided: `SetPieceSet…` (a world's own UI) is accepted only from someone who may take ownership of the board (`MayOwn` for the local player, `MayOwnAs` for the `…As` form, so the 13.2 lock and organizers apply as they do there); joining applies the preference regardless, as part of the join.
  - **Builder:** **Create Board View** gives an arbiter with an empty `pieceSets` one set at index 0, **Chess Pieces**, as a child of the arbiter: a White row (the generated meshes, White Pieces material) with its promotion row behind it as now, and a Black row whose slots hold only a renderer with the Black Pieces material and no mesh, so Black uses the fallback (and shows it working). The existing **White Pieces** and **Black Pieces** sets under the arbiter are removed, with the arbiter's two old fields cleared.
  - Verify: `BoardTest`: the two indexes start at 0, are set, synced in the packing (read back after unpacking) and set back to 0 by a reset; refused out of range, for no colour, and from someone `MayOwnAs` refuses (e.g. a bystander on a board locked to its loader), accepted from someone it allows; `ChangePieceSet` told. Joining with a preferred set: onto an empty board sets both colours (a loaded board too); with the other seat held sets only the joined colour; the second seat of a player already seated sets that colour; `-1` and an out-of-range preference change nothing; the local `Join` reads the arbiter's `preferredPieceSet`; `SetPreferredPieceSet` refuses out of range. `ViewTest` (the piece set frame, redone): the default set looks as before (the bounds check: every piece stands as it did), Black's fallback faces White and stands on its squares; a Black template of its own is used, turned as set up; Black materials without a mesh are used on White's mesh; setting one colour's index changes only that side's pieces, in place, including captured pieces, a promoted piece, the promotion ghosts and a fallen king; the arbiter's override shows its set on the board whatever it has synced, and clearing it brings the board's back; an override out of range refused. The build check's editor test: an empty `pieceSets` and a `null` element each cancel a build. Several clients (the debug view): A, preferring set 1, joins an empty board and B sees set 1 on both sides; B, preferring set 2, joins the other seat and both see set 2 on B's side only; a reset brings back set 0 on both; B's override shows B's choice on every board only for B.
  - Files: `ChessPieceSet.cs`, `ChessArbiter.cs` (`pieceSets`, `preferredPieceSet`, the overrides, the listeners), `ChessBoard.cs` (the synced indexes, their packing and reset, `SetPieceSet…`, the preference applied by `Join…`, the change constant), `ChessBoardListener.cs` (the constant's comment), `ChessBoardView.cs` (`CurrentPieceSet`, `ApplyPieceSet`, the fallback, `OnPieceSetOverrideChanged`, `ChangePieceSet`), `Editor/ChessArbiterBuildCheck.cs`, `Editor/ChessBoardViewBuilder.cs` (`BuildPieceSet`, the one default set), `Assets/Tests/Debug/ChessDebugView.cs` (the indexes and the preference shown, test-only buttons to cycle the preference and the override; not a player UI), `Assets/Tests/Board/BoardTest.cs`, `Assets/Tests/View/ViewTest.cs` and `Editor/ViewTestBuilder.cs` (its test set, with a Black row of its own), `Assets/Tests/Settings/Editor/ChessArbiterBuildCheckTest.cs`. Docs: [physical-pieces](docs/physical-pieces.md) (Piece sets), [settings](docs/settings.md) (the arbiter's list and overrides), [networking](docs/networking.md) (synced fields), [customizing](docs/customizing.md) (making a set with both colours, adding it to the list, players' choices now through the world's own UI setting `preferredPieceSet`, or `SetPieceSet`), [organizers](docs/organizers.md) (the build check), [testing](docs/testing.md) (the piece set frame).
  - Setup: run **Tools > Chess Board > Create Board View** for each board (the first run builds **Chess Pieces** on the arbiter and removes the two old sets), then **Create View Test** and the debug views' builder. Save the scene.
  - Changed from the plan: `ChangePieceSet` neither abandons an import under way (sent or received) nor clears a promotion waiting for its piece, since the position is unchanged; and the view checks each side's set against the board's on every `Refresh` as well as in `OnBoardChanged`, so a change no listener was told of (a test's `ClearState`) is caught too. Setting the index a side already has is accepted and tells nobody. The builder's slots are named **White Pawn** … **Black King** and **White Promotion Knight** …; `AddSlot` leaves out the `MeshFilter` for no mesh. **Create View Test** also builds a third set, **Test Colours** (red, Black only a blue material), and lists the test set and it on the arbiter after its own (indexes 1 and 2, for the several-client check); `ViewTest`'s frame makes the list the default set and the test set for its own checks and puts the scene's back, and `BoardTest`'s (`TestPieceSets`, in the second frame after the ownership checks) makes it three copies of the first. `BoardTest` also checks that a refused join or one of a seat already held changes nothing, and `StateText` and `CheckCleared` include the indexes.

Then in production (2026-10-24, after an upload, Siggі and Kötturinn on desktop, ChessBoard's debug view): Siggі, preferring set 1, joined the reset board and both clients showed Test Chess Pieces on both sides; Kötturinn, preferring set 2, joined Black and both showed Test Colours on Black only; Siggі's reset brought back Chess Pieces on both sides on both clients; Kötturinn's override 2 showed Test Colours on both boards for Kötturinn only, Siggі still seeing Chess Pieces. - [x] **13.7** Public methods that aren't public API start with `_`. UdonSharp makes a method `public` whenever another of our behaviours, a test, a UI event or `SendCustomEvent` calls it, so `public` alone doesn't say what a world may rely on, and from 16.1 [compatibility](docs/compatibility.md) keeps the public API (rule 6). Every public method of ours that 15's documents won't cover gets a leading underscore (`_UpdateClickables`, `_GhostIndex`, `_PieceBase`, a UI's button handlers, …), so the API is exactly the public methods without one, and 16.1 freezes only those. A Java-named internal method keeps its Java name after the underscore.
  - **The API surface** is what 15.0 to 15.8 describe: the board's UI and input methods and getters (15.1, 15.2), the arbiter's settings and piece sets (15.3, 15.5), the archive (15.4), the 2D view's (15.6), the rules a world may call without a board (15.7), and the `…Problem()` checks a world's editor scripts may call. Deciding each method's side is this step's work, listed in its Done note so 15's documents start from the list. Static methods count the same (the `Rules/` helpers used only inside the rules get the underscore).
  - **Not renamed:** Unity's and Udon's own events (`Start`, `Update`, `Interact`, `OnDeserialization`, `OnPickup`, `OnDrop`, `OnOwnershipRequest`, `OnPlayerJoined`, …), `ChessBoardListener`'s virtual methods and their overrides, `I18n`'s `OnLanguageChange`, and the methods of `io.siggi.ustools`. Fields and constants keep their names: fields shown in the Inspector are settings, and 15.0 says that other public fields are internal.
  - **Called by name:** a renamed method that a UI event (set by the builders as `SendCustomEvent` with its name), a `SendCustomEvent` / `SendCustomEventDelayed` call or the importer's done event (`doneEvent`) calls by name gets the new name there too, using `nameof` where the code already does. The builders are rerun so the scene's persistent listeners hold the new names. An added benefit: VRChat refuses a network event (`SendCustomNetworkEvent`) for a method starting with `_`, so no client can call an internal method on another.
  - The convention goes into CLAUDE.md's code conventions and [udon-constraints](docs/udon-constraints.md), and compatibility's "keep the public API" rule says that methods starting with `_` aren't covered by it.
  - Verify: compiled with no error; every builder and test builder rerun and the scene saved; the six suites pass in Play Mode with the same check counts as before and no error logged, with `PgnTest`'s as before; each of the UI's buttons, the settings menu's, the history wall's and the 2D view's, pressed once in Play Mode, still does what it did (no "event not found" logged); searching our behaviours for `public` methods without `_` finds only the listed API, Unity/Udon events and listener overrides.
  - Files: every behaviour under `Assets/Siggi/ChessBoard/` (including `Rules/`) and its callers, `Editor/*Builder.cs` (event names), the tests and debug views under `Assets/Tests/`. Docs: [udon-constraints](docs/udon-constraints.md), [compatibility](docs/compatibility.md), CLAUDE.md, and every doc naming a renamed method.

The test behaviours and debug views under `Assets/Tests/` aren't shipped and keep their own methods' names; they only call the new ones. - [x] **13.8** A portrait history wall that scrolls, newest game first. The wall (10.2a) is a 3 m wide landscape canvas with 50 one-line rows in two columns, oldest first. It becomes a smaller portrait panel (about 1.2 m wide and 1.6 m tall, still 2.5 m left of the boards and facing them) with one column of entries in a vertical scroll view, the latest game always at the top.
  - **Two lines per game, one entry:** each game's text keeps today's wording with the `: ` after the players replaced by a line break: `#<sequence>  <White> vs <Black>` on the first line, `<ending> (<result>), <N> moves[, Chess960]` on the second, e.g. `#12  Ana vs Bo` / `White wins by checkmate (1-0), 12 moves`. `wall_row` and `wall_row_chess960` in `en.json` change to match (`\n` in place of `: `). Both lines are one entry: one `GameHistoryRow` button whose target covers both, so pressing either line picks the game, and the selected colour covers both.
  - **Newest first:** entry 0 (the top) shows the latest archived game, entry `count - 1` the oldest. The archive's indices stay oldest first (`GameArchive` unchanged); the wall maps between them: an entry shows `_RowText(count - 1 - entry)`, `_SelectRow(entry)` picks that game, and `DrawSelection` colours the entry of `_SelectedIndex()`. `_RowText(index)` and `_SelectedIndex()` keep taking and returning archive indices, so "Load it here" (`ChessBoardUI`) doesn't change. Once a new game is archived, every entry moves down one and the new one is at the top; a pick stays on its game (it's kept by ID).
  - **Scrolling:** the entries sit in a `ScrollRect` (vertical only, a viewport with a mask, a scrollbar on the right as the 2D view's move list has, 12.15), dragged with the pointer or scrolled with the mouse wheel on desktop. Entries for games not in the archive are hidden and the content is as tall as the games shown, so the list scrolls only when they overflow it, and an empty archive shows just the title. The wall doesn't move the scroll position on its own, except to the top when it's drawn the first time.
  - **One step per wheel turn** (found in the first Play Mode look, 2026-10-26: one notch threw the list to its top or bottom). A `ScrollRect` moves its content by the wheel's delta times `scrollSensitivity`, and the delta's size depends on the input: ClientSim reads the Input System's `Mouse.scroll` (the project has both input handlers), about 120 a notch on Windows, where the legacy input gives about 1; the VRChat client's is unknown until a client run. So the scroll rect's `onValueChanged` calls the wall's `_OnScrolled`, which, when the wheel turned that frame (`Input.GetAxisRaw("Mouse ScrollWheel")`; `Input.mouseScrollDelta` isn't exposed to Udon), replaces the move with one `scrollSensitivity` step (an entry, 100 pixels) in its direction from where the list was, clamped to `_MaxScroll()`. A drag is left alone. The scroll rect has already set its scrollbar from its own move by then (its `LateUpdate` updates the scrollbar before calling `onValueChanged`), which showed the scrollbar at the list's end for a frame, so `_OnScrolled` sets the scrollbar too (`SetValueWithoutNotify`). The wall gets `scroll`, `viewport` and `scrollbar` for it. The 2D view's move list has the same scroll rect and is fixed in 13.9.
  - **The title:** always `Previous games` (`wall_title`), empty or not: `wall_title_empty` (`Previous games: none yet`) is dropped from `en.json` and the code, and `_TitleText` no longer depends on the count. The builder writes `Previous games` into the title's text in the scene too (the wall replaces it from the language pack at run time), so the wall's orientation shows in the editor.
  - Not changed: the number of entries (one per game the archive can hold), the redraw on the archive's `changes`, picking and "Load it here".
  - Verify: compiled with no error; **Create History Wall** and **Create Archive Test** rerun and the scene saved; the six suites pass in Play Mode with no error logged, `ArchiveTest`'s wall checks now expecting the newest game in entry 0, both lines in each entry's text, entries past the archive's count hidden, the title `wall_title` when emptied too, pressing an entry picking the game it shows (the fool's mate, now at the top, picked by entry 0), the scroll view, viewport and scrollbar assigned, `_MaxScroll()` the content's overflow and a move without the wheel (a drag) kept by `_OnScrolled`; in Play Mode with the archive full (`ArchiveTest`'s 50 games put back, or after it), the wall is portrait, shows the latest game at the top, each game on two lines, scrolls through all of them by dragging and by the wheel (one entry a notch, either way, the scrollbar following without a jump), and pressing the second line of an entry picks its game and lets "Load it here" load it.
  - Files: `GameHistoryWall.cs` (the order, hiding entries, the content's height, the title, the wheel's step), `GameHistoryRow.cs` (if its comments name the order), `Editor/GameHistoryWallBuilder.cs` (the portrait canvas, scroll view and its `onValueChanged`, two-line entries, the title's editor text), `Languages/en.json` (`wall_row`, `wall_row_chess960`, `wall_title_empty` removed), `Assets/Tests/Archive/ArchiveTest.cs` (the order and the entries' text and visibility). Docs: [archive](docs/archive.md) (history wall: order, layout, the row text, the title, picking by entry), [testing](docs/testing.md) (the archive test's wall checks).

- [x] **13.9** One step per mouse-wheel turn in the 2D view's move list. The move list (12.15) is a `ScrollRect` with `scrollSensitivity` one row (50 pixels), like the history wall's, so it most likely has 13.8's bug: in ClientSim one notch of the wheel moves the list about 120 rows, to its top or bottom. It went unnoticed because a game's moves rarely overflow the list by much (28 rows show), so the jump looked like an ordinary scroll to the end.
  - **Check first**, in Play Mode: a game long enough to overflow the list by far (100 moves or more, e.g. a 300-ply game from `Board2DTest`'s long-game check or a PGN import), the wheel turned one notch at a time over the list, in the middle of it. If one notch moves one row, write down what was seen here and change nothing but the docs.
  - **Fix**, as on the wall: `_OnMoveListScrolled` (the scroll rect's `onValueChanged`) keeps where the list was scrolled to; when the wheel turned that frame (`Input.GetAxisRaw("Mouse ScrollWheel")`), it replaces the scroll rect's move with one `scrollSensitivity` step in its direction from there, clamped to `_MoveListMaxScroll()`, sets the scrollbar to match (`SetValueWithoutNotify`, or it shows the list's end for a frame, as on the wall), then draws the pooled rows (`DrawMoveList`) as now. A drag is left alone.
  - **The rare case:** the view also scrolls the list itself (`UpdateMoveList`: the ply shown changes by a step button, a click, a move received or played while following the latest, switching board). If that lands in the same frame as a wheel turn, the fix above would take the view's own jump for the wheel's and undo it, leaving the current move out of view. So the view's own scrolling (`SetScroll`, and `_ScrollMoveList` for tests) updates the remembered position as well, and only the scroll rect's move is replaced. The wall has no such case (it scrolls itself only on its first drawing, which sets its remembered position too), but check that the wall's content shrinking in the frame of a wheel turn (the archive emptied) still clamps to the new end.
  - Not changed: dragging, the scrollbar, the pool, scrolling to the current move.
  - Verify: compiled with no error (UdonSharp's own log line too: the editor trigger's `refreshed` doesn't see UdonSharp's errors); no builder rerun (the move list's `onValueChanged` already calls `_OnMoveListScrolled`); the six suites pass in Play Mode with no error logged, `Board2DTest` adding: after `_ScrollMoveList` (the view's own scroll), `_OnMoveListScrolled` keeps it; a move without the wheel kept; in Play Mode, with a 300-ply game, one wheel notch over the list moves it one row each way, from the middle and at both ends, and the step buttons still bring the current move into view. In the VRChat client (desktop), on the next client run: one notch moves one row in the 2D list and one entry on the history wall, the client's delta being whatever it is.
  - **Seen** (2026-10-26, ClientSim, a 300-ply game loaded from a PGN): before the fix, one notch threw the list almost to its end, a couple of rows short, so the bug was there; with it, one notch moves one row each way, from the middle and at both ends, and the step buttons still bring the current move into view. The VRChat client check is still to do on the next client run.

- [x] **13.10** A personal history wall: the games the local player played, kept in their own VRChat persistent data (PlayerData) across visits and instances, on a second history wall beside the instance's. It behaves almost exactly like the instance wall (13.8), with 20 games by default (configurable) instead of 50, and only the player's own games. Both walls get an **Export PGN** button showing the picked game's PGN in a box, and the personal wall a delete button on each entry. With it, the archives are listed on the arbiter instead of on each board, the picked game is kept on the arbiter, and the walls, boards and UIs are told of changes instead of polling for them.
  - **`GameArchive` becomes a base:** UdonSharp has no interfaces ([udon-constraints](docs/udon-constraints.md)), so `GameArchive` becomes an abstract `UdonSharpBehaviour` with virtual methods, as `ChessBoardListener` is: the `changes` count, `Capacity()`, `GameCount()`, `IndexOf(gameId)`, the per-index readers (`GameIdAt`, `ContinuedFromAt`, `WhiteAt`, `BlackAt`, `StartFenAt`, `ModeAt`, `EndingAt`, `WinnerAt`, `BaseTimeAt`, `IncrementAt`, `MoveCountAt`, `_SequenceAt`, `CopyMovesAt`, `CopyClocksAt`; index 0 the oldest, as now; `_SequenceAt` is 0 on an archive without sequence numbers, as the personal one), `_AddGame(…)` (the board hands games to either kind; it gains the PGN the board logged, `pgn`, which the instance's archive ignores), `HasMovesAt(index)` (whether `CopyMovesAt` and `CopyClocksAt` can give the game's moves and clocks; always on the instance's archive), `PgnAt(index)` (the game's PGN as logged when the archive holds it, `""` on the instance's archive), `CanDelete()` and `DeleteGame(gameId)` (`false` in the base, and on the instance's archive, which nobody may delete from). The base also holds what both kinds share: the walls registered with it and the arbiter (below). Check first that a call through a field typed as the base reaches the subclass's override (it does for `ChessBoardListener`).
  - **The instance's archive becomes `InstanceGameArchive`:** today's `GameArchive` class, renamed inside Unity (or its `.cs`, `.asset` and their `.meta` files moved together) so its script GUID and its program asset's GUID stay, and the **Game Archive** prefab (14.5), the test scene's archive and every reference to it keep working. The new `GameArchive.cs` is a new file (abstract, so no program asset). Fields that take either kind are typed `GameArchive`; what needs the instance's sync (`GameArchiveHolder.archive`, the debug view's **Hold archive**, `ArchiveTest`, the archive's builder) uses `InstanceGameArchive`. Its log lines keep their `[GameArchive]` tag, which the tests and docs read. No migration: this is before the first release (16.1).
  - **The archives on the arbiter:** `ChessArbiter.archives` (`GameArchive[]`, set once per world: the Game Archive and the Personal Game Archive) replaces each board's `archive` field, which goes (before the first release, so no migration; the 14.2 prefab, the scene, the builders, `ArchiveTest`, its builder and the debug view move to the arbiter's list). `LogGame` hands each game, with the PGN it logs, to every archive in the list (that PGN has every SAN, since `PgnWith` fills in missing notation first), and each keeps what it wants: the instance's every game, the personal one only the local player's. A board without an arbiter archives nothing (it already logs an error for the missing arbiter). On `Start` the arbiter introduces itself to each archive in its list (`_SetArbiter`), so the archives and their walls reach it without a field of their own. One world-wide list rather than one per board: a world can no longer send different boards to different archives, which seems rare; a per-board override can come back later as a new field.
  - **`PersonalGameArchive`:** local only, nothing synced. It keeps only the games with the local player's display name as White or Black (the board identifies players by name); a game without the player's name, or with no ID or no moves, isn't saved. `capacity` (default 20, set in the inspector) slots: a new game goes into an empty slot, or once full over the oldest game; a game it already holds (by ID) is replaced in its slot.
    - **Chronology from the game ID:** an ID starts with the game's start time, `yyyyMMddHHmmss` in UTC ([game-ids](docs/game-ids.md)), so the archive's order (index 0 the oldest) is its games sorted by ID, and the oldest to overwrite is the one with the lowest ID. No sequence numbers are stored (`_SequenceAt` is 0). Where an entry's sequence is 0, the wall starts it with the game's date and time, from the ID, in place of `#<sequence>` (`wall_row_personal`, `wall_row_personal_chess960`), in the player's local time if `DateTime.ToLocalTime` is exposed to Udon, otherwise UTC, marked so.
    - **Saved as PGN** to the local player's PlayerData (VRChat Persistence), one string key per slot holding the game's PGN as the board logged it, and one header key (capacity), all starting with `keyPrefix` (default `siggi.chess.personal.`, so a world's own keys don't clash). PGN rather than packed moves (decided 2026-10-26): it's a standard that our own changes can't break, so a later version never needs code to read an older format; export from the personal wall is the stored text; and loading reuses the importer. PlayerData is gzipped and the gzipped size counts toward the limit, which makes PGN about as small as packed moves: measured on a 300-ply game with millisecond clocks, 2.2 KB gzipped as PGN against 2.1 KB as packed ints in base64, so 20 games of 300 plies take about 43 KB, 20 of 80 plies about 15 KB. The archive logs `PlayerData.GetPlayerDataStorageLimit()` and its usage on each save. PlayerData keys can't be deleted once written (VRChat's docs), so an empty slot, including a deleted game's, is the empty string.
    - **Two tags only in the saved copy** (decided 2026-10-26), since the wall's list needs them without reading the moves: the archive writes `[PlyCount "…"]` (a standard supplemental tag: the number of plies) and `[Ending "…"]` (custom, as `GameID`: the ending's stable name, e.g. `resignation`, `agreement`, `checkmate`, from `ChessGame`, since `Result` and the English `Termination` sentence don't tell a resignation from an agreed draw) as the slot's first two lines, then the PGN exactly as the board logged it. Extra tags are allowed in PGN, so a slot stays valid PGN. Everywhere else they're stripped: `PgnAt(index)` drops the first two lines when they're these tags (no regex: the archive put them there, first and in this order), so Export PGN shows the logged PGN. The board's own PGN (the log, its PGN box) doesn't change. Loading a game from a slot imports the saved copy with the tags, and the importer reads `Ending` when it's there, so the loaded game keeps the ending it was logged with instead of working it out again; a pasted PGN has no such tag and imports as now.
    - **Reading:** on restore the archive reads each slot's tags only (the tag regex the importer uses, never the move text): ID, `ContinuedFrom`, White, Black, the mode (`Variant`), `Ending` and `Result` (ending and winner), `FEN`, `TimeControl`, `PlyCount`, for the readers and the wall. A slot whose tags don't read (no `GameID`, damaged) counts as empty and is logged. Nothing is read until `OnPlayerRestored` for the local player; a game logged before then waits in memory and is saved once restored. A `capacity` lowered later leaves the slots past it unread; raised, the slots already saved are read.
    - **Moves in memory for this session's games:** a game handed in by a board this session keeps its packed moves and clocks in memory beside its PGN (`HasMovesAt` true), so loading it is the instance archive's instant copy, not an import. Games read from PlayerData have only their PGN until loaded through the importer. Not cached: the moves of a game imported earlier this session (it would need the board's import result taken back into the archive; rare enough to leave until loading proves slow in the client). Memory: 20 games of 300 plies are under 50 KB.
    - Other players can read a player's PlayerData (VRChat's docs), so the history isn't secret; [archive](docs/archive.md) says so.
    - `CanDelete()` is `true`; `DeleteGame(gameId)` writes the empty string to its slot and moves `changes` on.
  - **The pick, on the arbiter:** the game the local player picked is two local fields on the arbiter (not synced: each player's pick is their own, as now), `selectedArchive` (`GameArchive`, `null` for none) and `selectedGameId`, replacing the wall's own `selectedGameId` and `selectionChanges`. A wall's entry pressed calls `arbiter._SelectGame(archive, gameId)` (through its archive's arbiter): it picks that game, or clears the pick when it's the game already picked. Picking a game in another archive than the one picked before simply replaces the pick; the earlier archive hears of it with every other one (below), and its walls stop showing it. A picked game that its archive deletes or overwrites (the oldest, once the archive is full and another game comes in) clears the pick: the archive checks on each change and calls the arbiter's `_ClearSelection()`, whose notification hides "Load it here" on every board and the pick on the walls. No message says why (decided 2026-10-26: players know an old entry goes when a new one comes in). `_ClearSelection()`, `HasSelection()`.
  - **Told, not polled:**
    - **Walls register with their archive** on `Start` (`archive._AddWall(this)`, as views and UIs add themselves to their board's `listeners`). The archive calls each of its walls when its games change (`_OnArchiveChanged`, from wherever `changes` moves on now: a game archived, a holder update, the personal archive restored, a game deleted), replacing the wall's `Update` polling `changes` (`_RefreshIfChanged` stays for tests and the first drawing).
    - **The arbiter tells every archive and every board on any change** (`_OnArbiterChanged(change)`, `change` saying what: the settings, from an organizer here or a received update, or the pick): the archives from its `archives`, the boards as they register with it on `Start` (`arbiter._AddBoard(this)`). Each archive tells its walls (`_OnSelectionChanged`: redraw the picked entry's colour, the Export PGN button); each board tells its listeners through a new `ChessBoardListener.OnArbiterChanged(board, change)`, so the UI shows or hides "Load it here" at once and brings its buttons up to date on a settings change. The settings menu registers with the arbiter too and replaces its `Update` polling of the arbiter's `changes`. The UI's once-a-second button check stays (it also covers what changes with time, such as inactivity).
  - **The wall, reused:** `GameHistoryWall` shows either kind, through its `archive`. Its entries match the archive's `Capacity()`: at start it makes the entries it lacks by instantiating its last one (and its `GameHistoryRow`), so a capacity changed in the inspector needs no rebuilt wall. `titleKey` (default `wall_title`) picks its title from the language pack; the personal wall's is `wall_title_personal` (`My games`). Until its archive is restored the personal wall shows just the title. The entry drawn picked is the arbiter's pick when `selectedArchive` is its archive.
  - **Export PGN** on both walls (`exportButton`, `_PressExport`), shown while the arbiter's pick is in the wall's archive: it opens a PGN box on the wall for the local player (a read-only multi-line `TMP_InputField`, as the board UI's copying part, 11.2a, with the same hint on how to copy), holding the picked game's PGN as loading it onto a board and pressing **PGN** would show it (the tags from what the archive holds, the Date from the game ID as `ChessBoard.GamePgn` takes it, the result, the time control, the clocks), so `GamePgn`'s writing moves into a static method both use. Pressed again (`Hide PGN`), or the pick changed or cleared, the box closes. From the personal archive the PGN is `PgnAt(index)` (the logged PGN, the two saved tags stripped), at once. The instance's archive keeps no notation, so for its games the wall works the SAN out from the packed moves over the next frames, time-budgeted as the board's notation fill-in ([networking](docs/networking.md#filling-in-missing-notation)), the box saying `Working…` until it's in. Where the box goes on the wall (over the list's lower part, or beside it) is decided here.
  - **Delete**, on each entry of the personal wall (`deleteButtons`, `_PressDelete(entry)`), shown only while `archive.CanDelete()` (never on the instance's wall) and the entry has a game: the first press turns it to `Sure?` for 3 seconds, a second press within them calls `DeleteGame`.
  - **Load it here,** on every board's UI, shown while the board `CanLoadGame()` and the arbiter has a pick: `ChessBoard.LoadSelectedGame()` (and its `…As` form) finds the archive and the game from the arbiter's two fields and loads it: when the archive `HasMovesAt` the game, the new `LoadArchivedGameFrom(source, gameId)` (and its `…As` form; `LoadArchivedGame(gameId)` stays and looks the game up in the arbiter's archives); otherwise (a personal game from an earlier session) an import of its `PgnAt`, as **Load PGN**'s Import starts one (`StartImport`, the presser as `loadedBy`), its progress and failure shown on the UI as an import's are. Once the load is accepted (or the import started), the arbiter's pick is cleared. The loaded board syncs as now, so other players need nothing from the loader's personal archive. The UI's `historyWall` field goes: the UI needs no wall, only its board's arbiter.
  - **Scene:** **Tools > Chess Board > Create Personal Game Archive** (a new builder, beside **Create Game Archive**) adds it to the arbiter's `archives`, as **Create Game Archive** now adds the instance's; **Create History Wall** gets a personal counterpart, **Create Personal History Wall**, which places the wall beside the instance's (further left, facing the boards the same way), with its delete buttons and `titleKey`. Both wall builders add the Export PGN button and the box. `en.json` gets `wall_title_personal`, `wall_row_personal` and `wall_row_personal_chess960`, the export, hide and delete labels, `Sure?` and `Working…`.

### 14. Prefabs

World creators add the chess objects by dragging prefabs into their scene, as with other world assets, rather than through the **Tools > Chess Board** menu. Each prefab is a self-contained object for production use (no test or debug behaviours, no `Assets/Tests/` references), saved under `Assets/Siggi/ChessBoard/Prefabs/`, with its meshes, materials and Udon program assets already saved as assets (`Models/`, the `.asset` programs) so it works in a fresh scene. The builders stay as the way the prefabs are (re)built: each substep's builder creates its prefab when it's missing and otherwise updates it in place, keeping its existing objects (rule 1 of [compatibility](docs/compatibility.md): from the first release, 16.1, recreating an object loses worlds' overrides on it), and the scene's objects become instances of them. They aren't shipped, nor kept: once the prefabs carry their data versions (14.12), 14.13 deletes them, and from then on the prefabs themselves are the source, changed in place in Unity. A prefab can't reference objects in the scene, so references between the objects (a board to its arbiter and archive, a view to its board, …) are set after placing them: by hand in the inspector until 14.11 fills them in automatically. Placed instances get their network IDs from the VRChat SDK as scene objects do (see commit `b3c91ef`). A new `docs/prefabs.md` (added to the design documents table) describes each prefab, what to set on it after placing it, and the minimum set a world needs. Decided: every chess prefab shares one `I18n`, the Chess Language prefab (14.10), placed once per scene as a sibling or on a parent of the chess objects, so the `I18n` sanity check links them to it; `docs/prefabs.md` says so.

**Read:** [overview](docs/overview.md) (project layout), [architecture](docs/architecture.md) (layers, views of instance-wide objects), [udon-constraints](docs/udon-constraints.md) (components, not `new`), [networking](docs/networking.md) (synced objects), [testing](docs/testing.md) (the scene the tests run in), [compatibility](docs/compatibility.md) (how prefabs and saved fields may change once worlds use them), and each substep's files below.

It's both the arbiter's default set and the starting point for a world's own set (15.5): a world creator drags it into the scene and **unpacks it completely**, so their set is entirely the world's own and a package update never changes it, rather than making a prefab variant, whose properties not overridden would follow the package's set (a Black slot left to the fallback taking a mesh the package later gives its default, against rule 1 of [compatibility](docs/compatibility.md)). - [ ] **14.12** Data versions and the migrator ([compatibility](docs/compatibility.md), rule 5): a saved `dataVersion` on each of our components that a world places (the arbiter, board, view, UI, both archives, both history walls, settings menu, 2D view, piece set and the auth behaviours), set to the current version by the builders, so the prefabs carry it once the builders are gone (14.13). Its default in code is 0, not the current version: a component saved before the field existed loads with the code's default, so any other default would make old data look current; a component a world creator adds by hand also starts at 0 and is raised by the migrator. And an editor script, run when a scene is opened, when it's saved and before every build (beside the build check and the wiring of 14.11), that brings each component in the loaded scenes (prefab instances and unpacked copies) from its version up to the current one, step by step, re-copies the UdonSharp proxy to its Udon data, logs what it changed and blocks the build (with a dialog) on a component it can't upgrade. Its first migration: a component from before versions (`dataVersion` 0) is version 1, unchanged. Also check whether UdonSharp carries a field's value over with `[FormerlySerializedAs]` on both the proxy and the Udon data, and write the answer into rule 2 of the compatibility doc.
  - Verify: an editor test (like **Test Build Check**) with a component set to version 0 sees it raised to the current version and logged; one with a version newer than the code's blocks the build; a migration added for the test (a field renamed in a test behaviour, its old field kept hidden) carries the value over on a prefab instance with that field overridden and on an unpacked copy, the old field cleared; opening, saving and building the test scene changes nothing when everything is current.
  - Files: new `Editor/ChessMigrator.cs` (in the package), every component above (`dataVersion`), every builder (setting it), the prefabs (rebuilt or saved with it set), `Editor/ChessArbiterBuildCheck.cs` and the wiring script (14.11, run in the same hooks). Docs: [compatibility](docs/compatibility.md), [organizers](docs/organizers.md) (the build check), [prefabs](docs/prefabs.md).
- [ ] **14.13** Delete the builders. With the prefabs done (14.1 to 14.10) and carrying their data versions (14.12), the builders have done their job: from the first release (16.1) a prefab may only be edited in place (rule 1 of [compatibility](docs/compatibility.md)), which a builder that rebuilds from scratch can't do, and a builder kept unused would drift from its prefab and could overwrite it with an older layout if run. From here on the prefabs are the source and are changed in Unity; a builder can still be brought back from Git history if ever needed. Nothing in 14 changes the prefabs. The package is the contents of `Assets/Siggi/ChessBoard`, with `io.siggi.ustools` (`SimpleAuth` and its built-ins, fetched from the VPM listing `https://vpm.siggi.io/index.json`) as a dependency in its `package.json` (`vpmDependencies`). The editor scripts the package needs stay in `Assets/Siggi/ChessBoard/Editor/`: `ChessArbiterBuildCheck.cs`, `ChessBoard2DViewSyncCheck.cs` (12.16), the wiring script from 14.11 and the migrator from 14.12.
  - **The tests:** the test builders under `Assets/Tests/*/Editor/` point the tests at prefab instances (as 14.11 left the test scene) rather than at objects a builder made, so the tests run against what ships. What they still call moves into the test editor code (e.g. `Assets/Tests/Editor/`, moved with its `.meta` where a whole file moves): `ChessBuilderUtil` (12.18, 12.19; used by every test builder), `ChessBoardViewBuilder`'s `PieceMeshes`, `BuildPieceSet` and `RepointReferences` (`ViewTestBuilder`), and `ChessBoard2DViewBuilder`'s `HolderOf`, `MatchObjectSync`, `LoadPieceSprites` and `NewPieceImage` (`Board2DTestBuilder`, `ChessDebugViewBuilder`), or each is replaced by reading the prefab. Anything the wiring script or the migrator uses from them moves into those.
  - **Generated assets:** the meshes and materials in `Models/` and the sprites in `Sprites2D/` stay as the saved assets they are, keeping their GUIDs (rule 3); a later change edits them in place.
  - Verify: no builder and no **Tools > Chess Board > Create …** menu item is left (the test and debug ones under `Assets/Tests/` stay); Unity compiles without errors; every test builder still sets up its test, and every Play Mode test passes against the prefab instances; the build check still cancels a build with an empty Simple Auth field; the wiring script and the migrator still run on save and change nothing in the test scene.
  - Files: `Editor/ChessBoardBuilder.cs`, `Editor/ChessBoardViewBuilder.cs` (the view and the UI, 12.1), `Editor/ChessBoard2DViewBuilder.cs`, `Editor/ChessSettingsMenuBuilder.cs`, `Editor/GameArchiveBuilder.cs`, `Editor/GameHistoryWallBuilder.cs` (deleted with their `.meta` files), `Editor/ChessBuilderUtil.cs` (moved); the test builders (`ArchiveTestBuilder.cs`, `Board2DTestBuilder.cs`, `ChessDebugViewBuilder.cs`, `ViewTestBuilder.cs`); comments still naming a builder (14.1 to 14.9 remove most). Docs: those naming the builders or their menu items ([architecture](docs/architecture.md), [archive](docs/archive.md), [board-2d](docs/board-2d.md), [customizing](docs/customizing.md), [organizers](docs/organizers.md), [physical-pieces](docs/physical-pieces.md), [settings](docs/settings.md), [testing](docs/testing.md)), and [prefabs](docs/prefabs.md) (what the package contains, and that the prefabs are the source).

### 15. API documentation

Nine documents under `docs/api/`, one per substep: an index of what they share (15.0), then documents for world creators building their own UI or presentation on top of the board (15.1 to 15.4, and the 2D view, 15.6), their own piece sets (15.5, a guide), using the rules without a board (15.7) and the text keys (15.8). Together they define the public API that 13.7 marked out. Each describes what exists in the code at the time of writing (methods, their parameters, return values and when calls are refused), with short UdonSharp examples; each is checked against the code rather than the other `docs/` files alone. Where a part of the API has no way to be notified of changes, the document says how to detect them as the existing code does (the `changes` counters on `ChessArbiter` and `GameArchive` are polled, not pushed); adding listeners there would be a change to the plan, decided with the user first.

**Read:** [prefabs](docs/prefabs.md) (what a world places, from step 14), [architecture](docs/architecture.md) (listeners, views of instance-wide objects), [udon-constraints](docs/udon-constraints.md) (what callers can and can't do from UdonSharp), and each substep's files below.

- [ ] **15.0** `docs/api/README.md`: the index, and what every API document would otherwise repeat. Links to 15.1 to 15.8 with a line on each. How the objects relate (arbiter, board, its view and UI as listeners, archive and history wall, 2D view, Chess Language) and which reference which, as [prefabs](docs/prefabs.md) places them. The shared numbering: colours, squares (0 to 63, a1 first), pieces and piece types as ints, `-1` for none, packed moves (linking [move-encoding](docs/move-encoding.md)). What a call's return means on a client that doesn't own the board: whether it's applied here at once or sent to the owner, when other clients see it, and that the listener (`OnBoardChanged`) is how a change shows up everywhere, the caller's client included. The `…As(name)` forms, and why a world's own UI uses the plain ones. Calling from UdonSharp: holding a reference set in the Inspector, no `new`, return codes in place of exceptions ([udon-constraints](docs/udon-constraints.md)). **What's supported:** only what these documents name; public methods starting with `_` (13.7) and public fields not shown in the Inspector are internal and may change in any release, as [compatibility](docs/compatibility.md) says.
  - Verify: every link resolves; each numbering, return and timing rule stated matches the code (`ChessBoard.cs`, `Rules/ChessPosition.cs`, the move encoding); the "what's supported" statement matches 13.7's list and compatibility's rule.
  - Files: `ChessBoard.cs` (ownership, sending and receiving, the `…As` forms), `ChessBoardListener.cs`, `Rules/ChessPosition.cs`, and the documents of 15.1 to 15.8 as they're written (the index's line on each is checked again when the last is done). Docs: [architecture](docs/architecture.md), [networking](docs/networking.md), [ownership](docs/ownership.md), [move-encoding](docs/move-encoding.md), [prefabs](docs/prefabs.md), [compatibility](docs/compatibility.md), [udon-constraints](docs/udon-constraints.md).

What to call when a player presses each button (join White / Black, leave a seat (13.1), mode and clock selection, Start, resign, offer / accept / claim draw, takeback request / accept / decline, enter / leave replay and stepping, "Continue from here" (on a loaded board, and for a player in both seats, 13.3), reset, Load PGN and import (and who holds a loaded board, 13.2), PGN copy), the `Can…` / `Is…` getters that decide whether each button should be shown, and the status getters (turn, clocks, check, result, pending offers and requests, import progress); the UI text through `I18n` and `en.json` (13.5); registering for changes through `ChessBoardListener` (`OnBoardChanged` and the last-change value, `OnNotationFilled`, `OnImportChanged`) and how to add a listener to a board. Which changes abandon an import under way (every change made here or received, except a rejection, a touch (`ChangeActivity`) and a piece set change (`ChangePieceSet`), which leave the position as it was), so a UI showing import progress knows that a set change doesn't end it.

- [ ] **15.5** `docs/api/piece-sets.md`: making a custom chess piece set, a step-by-step guide for a world creator with their own models, from import to players choosing it. Unlike 15.1 to 15.4 it's mostly editor work rather than calls, so it walks through the steps in order, and its API part (letting players choose) is as the others are.
  - **Preparing the models:** one mesh per piece type (a set may share one, as the promotion pieces do), facing any way, of any size, with its origin anywhere and any number of material slots, as the template's transform fixes all of that; what the view reads from the mesh: its bounds, for the `BoxCollider` fitted to each piece and ghost (the grab box) and for a fallen king's lift, so stray geometry far from the piece makes both wrong; no colliders of the model's own needed; the import settings that matter (scale factor or the template's scale, a model from Blender lying on its back fixed by turning the template); materials and shaders that work in VRChat, and keeping a set light enough for 32 pieces per board (and for Android/Quest, if the world builds for it).
  - **Making the set:** dragging the **Chess Pieces** prefab (14.1) into the scene, unpacking it completely and renaming it, and why not a prefab variant (a package update would change what the world hasn't overridden); saving it as a prefab of the world's own to reuse it; or adding a `ChessPieceSet` to an empty object and its slots by hand. Where it may live (under the arbiter or anywhere in the scene; its own object kept active, as it hides its slots on Start), and checking that each of the 20 fields (**White Pawn** … **Black Promotion Queen**) points at its own **Model**.
  - **Setting up each White template** on its slot's one-square **Guide**: mesh and materials (one per slot of the mesh), then moving, turning and scaling the **Model** until it stands upright on the guide at the size wanted, facing Black (+z, the slot's blue axis), with the slots themselves unscaled and unturned; what that transform becomes on the board (offset, turn and size in the view's units, scaling with the view).
  - **Black's three ways**, per type: materials only (Black wears White's model turned, in its own materials, as **Chess Pieces** does), nothing (White's model and materials), or a model of its own set up facing White (−z), used as set up.
  - **The promotion pieces:** White's four promotion templates required, Black's optional with the same fallback; a see-through material (the package's **Promotion Ghost** or the set's own); the row floating over the pawn, so a much taller set's ghosts may want a smaller scale.
  - **Adding it to the world:** listing it in the arbiter's **Piece Sets** (its place there is its index; index 0 the default every reset brings back; no empty entries, the build check cancelling a build), and that reordering the list changes which set an index means, for a world's own scripts naming indexes.
  - **Checking it:** in Play Mode, putting it on a board (a test button calling `SetPieceSetOverride`, or `preferredPieceSet` and a join), the errors the view logs for a set with a missing White template, mesh or renderer (`Problem()`), and a short troubleshooting list (a piece floating or sunk into the board, facing the wrong way, Black looking like White, a pink material, a grab box far too big).
  - **Letting players choose:** short UdonSharp examples of a world's own UI: a button setting the local player's `preferredPieceSet` (`SetPreferredPieceSet`, with when it's put on a board), one changing a board's side (`SetPieceSet(color, index)`, its return values and who it's refused for), and a per-player override (`SetPieceSetOverride`, `-1` to clear); reading what a side wears (`PieceSetIndex`, the view's `CurrentPieceSet`) and noticing a change (`ChangePieceSet` through `ChessBoardListener`).
  - [customizing](docs/customizing.md)'s piece set section shrinks to a summary linking to the guide, so the steps aren't kept in two places.
  - Verify: every field, method, return value, constant, object and log text the guide names exists in the code (or the prefab) with that meaning; following the guide from the start in a scene with the package's prefabs, with models other than the placeholders (imported turned and at another scale, origin off the base; one Black type with a model of its own and one with materials only), gives a set that the view applies with no error logged, every piece standing on its squares facing the right way (by eye, and against the default set's bounds as `ViewTest`'s piece set frame checks them), shown through an override and through a preferred set and a join in Play Mode; each logged problem the troubleshooting list names is produced by removing that part. The test set made for the check isn't kept.
  - Files: `ChessPieceSet.cs`, `ChessArbiter.cs` (`pieceSets`, `preferredPieceSet`, `SetPreferredPieceSet`, the overrides), `ChessBoard.cs` (`SetPieceSet…`, `CanSetPieceSet…`, `PieceSetIndex`, `ChangePieceSet`, joining with a preferred set), `ChessBoardView.cs` (`ApplyPieceSet`, `CopyTemplate`, the fitted colliders, the fallen king's lift, its piece set log lines), `Editor/ChessArbiterBuildCheck.cs` (the `pieceSets` check), the Chess Pieces and Chess Arbiter prefabs (14.1), `Models/` (**Promotion Ghost**, **Piece Set Guide**); `Assets/Tests/View/ViewTest.cs` (the piece set frame). Docs: [customizing](docs/customizing.md) (Chess piece sets), [physical-pieces](docs/physical-pieces.md) (Piece sets, promotion ghosts, the fallen king), [settings](docs/settings.md) (the arbiter's piece sets), [prefabs](docs/prefabs.md), [ownership](docs/ownership.md) (who may change a board's sets).
- [ ] **15.6** `docs/api/board-2d.md`: the 2D view from a world's own scripts. Choosing what it shows (`boards`, `lockedBoard`, the left / right buttons' methods, which board it starts on) and its own replay (the `Step…` constants, `LatestPly`, stepping from a world's own buttons, how `synced` changes who sees a step); the move list (a cell pressed, the current move); restyling (the square, tint and label colours, `whiteSprites` / `blackSprites`, the labels, the move list's row height and colours); the movable holder (`ChessBoard2DViewHolder`, 12.16) and what it lets players do. As the clearest example of a presentation built as a `ChessBoardListener`: how it reads the board in `OnBoardChanged` and `OnNotationFilled` and redraws, as a pattern for a world's own read-only display (a scoreboard, a spectator screen).
  - Verify: every field, method and constant named exists in `ChessBoard2DView.cs` / `ChessBoard2DViewHolder.cs` with that meaning; the stepping and board-choice flow matches what its own buttons do; `synced` matches `ChessBoard2DViewSyncCheck`.
  - Files: `ChessBoard2DView.cs`, `ChessBoard2DViewHolder.cs`, `ChessMoveListCell.cs`, `Editor/ChessBoard2DViewSyncCheck.cs`, the 2D view prefabs (14.8); `Assets/Tests/Board2D/Board2DTest.cs`. Docs: [board-2d](docs/board-2d.md), [customizing](docs/customizing.md) (Letting players move the 2D view), [replay](docs/replay.md), [architecture](docs/architecture.md) (listeners).
- [ ] **15.7** `docs/api/rules.md`: the rules without a board. The static methods a world may call on its own arrays (13.7's list): reading and writing FEN (`ChessFen.ParseFen` / `WriteFen`), the board and state arrays they fill (sizes, what each state slot holds), the legal moves of a position and of a square, making a move on the arrays, the position's status (check, mate, stalemate) and the draw checks, SAN of a move, and writing PGN (`ChessPgn`); with examples such as a puzzle checking the player's move, or a FEN shown on a sign. What they don't do (no clocks, no networking, no history beyond what the caller keeps) and the costs (a legal-move search per call; keep the arrays local, as [udon-constraints](docs/udon-constraints.md) says).
  - Verify: every method named exists under `Rules/` with that signature and meaning, and is in 13.7's API list; each example compiles in a scratch behaviour (not kept) and gives the result the document says in Play Mode.
  - Files: `Rules/ChessFen.cs`, `Rules/ChessPosition.cs`, `Rules/ChessMoves.cs`, `Rules/ChessRules.cs`, `Rules/ChessCaptures.cs`, `Rules/ChessDraws.cs`, `Rules/ChessNotation.cs`, `Rules/ChessPgn.cs`, `Rules/ChessGame.cs`; `Assets/Tests/Rules/RulesTest.cs`, `Assets/Tests/PGN/PgnTest.cs`. Docs: [move-encoding](docs/move-encoding.md), [legality-checking](docs/legality-checking.md), [modes](docs/modes.md), [game-endings](docs/game-endings.md), [pgn](docs/pgn.md).
- [ ] **15.8** `docs/api/text.md`: the text keys. Every key of `en.json`: where it shows (UI, 2D view, history wall, settings menu, view), its placeholders (`${name}` …), and the keys built in code (`ChessGame.EndingKey`'s endings, `hover_promote_` + the piece type). How a world's own UI shows the chess texts (its own `i18n` field on Chess Language, `ChessUtil.Text`, `I18n.Format`, again on `OnLanguageChange`), and how a translation pack uses the list (linking [customizing](docs/customizing.md)'s Languages). The keys are public API from 16.1: a key is never renamed or removed, since a world's pack holding the old one would fall back to English without a word, and a string whose meaning changes gets a new key. [compatibility](docs/compatibility.md) gets that rule.
  - Verify: the document lists exactly the keys of `en.json` (counted against it) plus the built ones, each with the placeholders it has there; every place named matches where the code reads the key; the example's calls exist with those signatures.
  - Files: `Languages/en.json`, `ChessUtil.cs`, `ChessBoardUI.cs` (`_LoadGameTexts`, the `Text*` constants), `Rules/ChessGame.cs` (`EndingKey`), every behaviour's `LoadTexts`; `Assets/Tests/View/ViewTest.cs` (the key checks). Docs: [architecture](docs/architecture.md) (Player text), [customizing](docs/customizing.md) (Languages), [compatibility](docs/compatibility.md).

### 16. First release

The package's first release, once everything before it is done: the prefabs (14), the API documents (15) and any steps still to be added to the plan, which go before this one. Until then nothing outside this project depends on the package, so the [compatibility](docs/compatibility.md) rules don't apply yet.

**Read:** [compatibility](docs/compatibility.md), [prefabs](docs/prefabs.md), [testing](docs/testing.md).

- [ ] **16.1** First release (version 1): the package's version 1, `CHANGELOG.md` in the package (rule 8, its first entry), and the compatibility fixture: a scene saved now under `Assets/Tests/Compatibility/` with an instance of each prefab carrying typical overrides (a moved UI, a scaled view with its own board model, moved captured-piece places, a piece set of the world's own (an unpacked copy of Chess Pieces, 14.1) listed at index 1, a changed setting on the arbiter, a world script calling `SetPieceSet` and `SetPreferredPieceSet` and listening with a `ChessBoardListener`), kept unchanged from then on as the previous release's world. From here on the [compatibility](docs/compatibility.md) rules apply to every change.
  - Verify: the fixture opens with no error, the migrator changes nothing in it, and every Play Mode test passes against it (the tests pointed at its boards by their builders).
  - Each later release: before releasing, copy the package as it was released into a fresh project (or check out the release's commit), save the fixture there with that version, open it under the new version, and check that the migrator upgrades it with no error, nothing it overrode is lost, and every Play Mode test passes; then save it as the next release's fixture. Docs: [testing](docs/testing.md) (the compatibility fixture and the release check), [compatibility](docs/compatibility.md), [prefabs](docs/prefabs.md) (the version and the changelog).
